# PROJECT_STATE.md — Hydra Living Status Document

> **What this file is.** The single, always-current narrative of the Hydra project: what it is, what has happened, what is true right now, what is owed, and what happens next. It is written so that any human or AI agent can read this one file and act correctly without archaeology through commits or chat logs.
> **Update rule (binding on the coding agent):** this file is updated in the *same commit* as any milestone progress, gate decision, finding, verification result, or package amendment. A commit that changes project reality without updating this file is a process defect. Each update appends to §12 (changelog) with date + summary.

## 0. RESUMPTION BRIEFING (read first — a fresh session resumes from the repo alone)

### (a) Standing rules currently in force

> **Rules 1–26 are in force as of 2026-08-25.** Numbering is historical, not priority-ordered, and
> 8b is a corollary to 8 rather than a rule of its own. The most load-bearing for a fresh session
> are **12** (verification infrastructure gets no presumption of correctness), **16** (a CI outcome
> is evidence only as a quoted `verdict=` line), **19** (an oracle must be able to produce the
> failure it guards — with §7.41's five shapes and two hunting heuristics), **20** (reconcile
> against the auditor's own text before declaring a finding closed), **23** (executed documentation
> is a test; read documentation is a claim) **24** (re-measure every number, including the
> owner's), **25** (a prose caveat cannot protect a machine-readable claim — audit every automated
> verdict for whether its failure mode is expressible) and **26** (a residual may name its site
> correctly and still be wrong about its blast radius; an estimate of cost is not evidence).
1. **Attribution:** the owner `Pavan Manchikatla <91258136+PavanManchikatla@users.noreply.github.com>` is the **primary author + committer on every commit** (repo-local `git config user.name/user.email`); every commit message ends with `Co-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>`. Do **not** use `-c` overrides.
2. **Push cadence:** small logical commits, conventional messages, **push after every commit**; local never leads remote by more than a day.
3. **§11 same-commit:** any commit that changes project reality (milestone progress, gate verdicts, findings, TLC/sim results, amendments, owed items) updates this file **in the same commit** and appends one line to §12.
4. **History-rewrite prohibition:** `git` history rewrites are **forbidden** (the one authorized attribution backfill is done; never again).
5. **Ratification-pause:** any finding that changes a package decision (defaults / formats / scope) **pauses for owner ratification** before the next milestone; findings that don't, proceed and are logged in §7/§12.
6. **Escalate-with-traces:** verification deviations **escalate with the trace**; the model/spec is **never** adjusted to make a run pass.
7. **Thermal rule:** local TLC is **smoke-only** (parse, Mut2, Mut4); baseline-safety / baseline-liveness / Mut1 / Mut3 are **CI-owned** (`.github/workflows/tlc.yml`). Any exceptional local run uses `-checkpoint 1 -workers 1 nice -n 19` and must be `-recover`-resumable.
8. **#25577 reply-monitoring:** check https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/25577 for maintainer replies **at each session start**; draft any response and get **owner approval before posting**.
8b. **⚑ AN APPROVAL IS CONDITIONED ON THE FACTS THAT JUSTIFIED IT; WHEN THOSE CHANGE, THE APPROVAL LAPSES AND THE DECISION RETURNS TO THE OWNER (corollary to rule 8, design authority, 2026-08-23, from §7.48).** *Permission to do X is permission to do X-as-described.* An owner who approves filing a bug approves reporting **a finding**; it is not approval to file **a duplicate of an already-fixed bug**, and the agent discovering that difference must **report back rather than proceed**. The instance: the `gguf_init_from_file` filing was approved and **not posted** — a tracker search first showed the abort reported four times and fixed on upstream `master`, our pin merely predating the guard. **Binding in both directions:** an agent may not treat a lapsed approval as standing, and may not quietly re-scope the action to fit the approval it has. Related to rule 12 (no layer between a fact and a claim gets a presumption) applied to *authorisation*.

9. **Fix-the-sim-never-the-budget:** a mutation the randomized sim can't catch within its step budget is a **sim-weakness to fix**, never a budget to relax.
10. **Parked decisions never block work:** proceed with milestone work; don't idle on a pending owner decision unless the work literally depends on it.
11. **Defect-repair sequence (from F-UNSERVABLE, ratified 2026-07-12):** when a defect is found at any layer, the repair sequence is always **classify per-layer with evidence → ratify → amend the authority documents top-down (spec, then model, then code) → regression-test at the layer that found it → rerun verification proportional to the semantic change** (smoke if the model semantics did not move, full if they did). If the *model* is found to also misclassify, stop and escalate the trace rather than amending anything.
12. **Verification-infra gets no presumption of correctness (from F-RECOVER/F-LIVENESS-FAIR, §7.12):** every untested link in the verification/CI chain (checkpoint round-trips, artifact uploads, resume logic, masked-failure guards, **and CI result *classification***) is **observed, not assumed** — demonstrated working at least once before any gate leans on it, and a results table may say GREEN only when the log itself says so (never map a CI job's `success` to GREEN). The layering doctrine applies to the harness, not only the protocol.
13. **TLC checkpoints are model-version-bound (from F-LIVENESS-FAIR):** any `.tla` change voids all prior checkpoints; the `-recover` chain must restart fresh — never `recover=true` across a model edit.
14. **The two-process bit-exact anchor is the standing M2 regression gate (owner-ratified 2026-07-13):** `tests/local_pair.rs::two_worker_teacher_forced_no_sample_bit_exact` (and its runner) is the regression anchor for **all remaining M2 work** — **no later slice may land red against it**. Greedy sampling extends it one step (`sampler_pipeline.rs::greedy_sample_across_pipeline_matches_unsplit_argmax`) and is held to the same bar.
15. **Sampler ownership boundary (spec §1.4, slice 3):** the **coordinator constructs only the config-defined initial checkpoint**; **every** subsequent sampler snapshot is produced at **S_P**. Any sampler logic drifting into the coordinator is an architecture-rule defect. The RNG (Philox, counter-based, keyed per session) advances **only inside a stochastic sample step**.
18. **⚑ THE INVOCATION IS PART OF THE VERIFICATION INFRASTRUCTURE (design authority, 2026-08-23 — rule 12's THIRD instance).** *Running a checker differently from how the project runs it produces a result about a different system.* **Consequence, binding: verification tooling is invoked ONLY through the project's own runners/scripts (`verification/run-tlc.sh`, `run-gate.sh`, the workflow steps), never ad-hoc — and if an ad-hoc invocation is unavoidable, its result is PROVISIONAL until reproduced through the runner.** **The instance:** the H1/M13 smoke was run as a direct `java -cp tla2tools.jar tlc2.TLC …` and reported **"Deadlock reached"** on both mutation legs, which read as the amendment having broken the model. It had not. `run-tlc.sh` passes **`-deadlock`** — TERMINAL is deliberately absorbing (VERIFICATION-README) — and the pulled counterexample showed a *legitimate* terminal state (both stages LOST), not a fencing stall. Re-run through the project's own flags, **both mutations fire**. **The near-miss is the point: an escalation was already being drafted from a result about a system the project does not run.** **Rule 12's three instances now span the whole stack** — (i) the CI job's ✓ vs its `verdict=` line (§7.14/§7.15), (ii) a persisted tool-output *file* read as live job state (§12, 2026-08-23 housekeeping), (iii) a checker invoked with different flags than the project's. **The generalisation: every layer between a fact and the claim about it — the classifier, the log, the reader, and now the command line — is infrastructure, and none of it gets a presumption of correctness.**

17. **A defect class is audited across EVERY parser of untrusted input in the same wave, and each becomes a fuzz target (design authority, 2026-08-23, from the FOURTH instance of the §7.28-D2 allocation class).** *A class fixed in one instance and left in another is not fixed.* `Vec::with_capacity(declared)` was found in GGUF (M4·1), then again in the manifest (audit C1's seam), then again in the bootstrap blob (audit L2) — **three discoveries of one class, each treated as a bug**. The rule turns the third discovery into the last one: when a class surfaces, **enumerate every untrusted-input parser and check each**, and give each a fuzz target **or a written justification for why it needs none** — a claim of "clean by construction" is a claim, and rule 12 says claims about verification infrastructure get no presumption. **The enumeration lives in `crates/hydra-fuzz/Cargo.toml`'s dependency list**, so a parser absent from it is itself the finding. **Untrusted input means anything the process did not compute itself**: the network, the disk, a model file, a provisioning blob — not merely "the network".

16. **CI results are evidence only as quoted `verdict=` lines or receipt files (owner-ratified 2026-07-13, from §7.14/§7.15).** A CI outcome enters this record **only** as a quoted Classify `verdict=` line or a receipt file under `verification/ci-results/`. **Job status, matrix labels, and green check-marks are never evidence — at any layer, including the design authority's own summaries** (the owner noted the ratification echoed the false claim; the receipt discipline caught both). Corollary of rule 12; §7.14/§7.15 stand as the precedent.

19. **⚑⚑ AN ORACLE MUST BE ABLE TO PRODUCE THE FAILURE IT GUARDS (design authority, 2026-08-23, elevated from §7.37/§7.38 — *this is the project's DOMINANT DEFECT MODE*).** *A test whose driver is a perfect actor proves only that the happy path works.* **Consequence, binding:** (a) **before trusting any regression gate, state what it structurally CANNOT see** — every gate carries a one-line **"cannot see"** annotation in §6, and a gate without one is not yet trustworthy; (b) where the gap matters, **the fix is an adversarial driver, not a note** — a "cannot see" line is a description of the oracle, never a licence to leave it blind; (c) a green result may be cited only for what its driver could have falsified. **Provenance — three instances in three waves, all found by the audit and none by the verification:** 1c's *enshrining control* (a control asserting the exact inconsistency C3 forbids, which would have gone red on the fix); 1d's *perfect coordinator* (every driver sent each position once, in order, in its starting epoch, to a stage it never activated — so no retransmit, gap, stale epoch or unactivated serve was expressible, **the rule-14 anchors included**); and M6's *blind simulator* (a hard-coded fsync that erases the very crash window the mechanism exists to survive). **The generalisation:** rule 12 says verification infrastructure gets no presumption of correctness; rule 19 says the same about its **reach** — an oracle's silence is evidence only over the space its driver can generate, and that space is almost always smaller than the claim being made about it.

20. **⚑⚑ THE AUTHORITATIVE TEXT OF AN EXTERNAL FINDING IS THE AUDITOR'S OWN; A DIRECTIVE'S GLOSS IS A POINTER, NEVER A SUBSTITUTE (design authority, 2026-08-23, from the M7 gap — §7.42/§7.47).** *A summary that renames a finding displaces the source it summarises.* **Binding:** (a) **reconcile against the source before declaring any finding closed** — "X is fixed" must be a claim about the auditor's X, not about a label someone assigned it; (b) **an ID-reconciliation table is a REQUIRED ARTIFACT for any future external review** — gloss vs source title, per finding, with an explicit verdict (*agree* / *labels shuffled, work complete* / *aimed at the wrong target*), and the review is not closed until it exists; (c) **the source document is banked in-repo before remediation begins**, not after. **The instance:** the Wave-2 directive said only *"M7 (contiguity)"*; the auditor's M7 is **`GENERATION_COMMIT` contiguity including retain-or-fail on commit error**. Boundary-store contiguity was implemented instead — real work, wrong record type — and the mistake was undetectable from the directive alone, because the directive was the only text in the repo. **The error was the agent's** (§7.42(c)); the rule is what makes it non-repeatable. This is rule 12's doctrine applied to *evidence provenance*: a transcription is a layer between a fact and the claim about it, and no layer gets a presumption of correctness.

21. **⚑ BEFORE CALLING MISPLACED PROTOCOL BEHAVIOUR A DISCIPLINE FAILURE, TEST WHETHER THE DEPENDENCY GRAPH PERMITS THE CORRECT PLACEMENT (design authority, 2026-08-24, from §7.51).** *A build can enforce a violation.* The coordinator's hand-rolled activation transaction looked like a shortcut somebody took; it was **the only shape the crate graph allowed** — the wire codec lived in `hydra-worker`, `hydra-worker` depends on `hydra-coordinator`, so a coordinator that encoded a frame would have closed a dependency cycle and **would not compile**. Correct placement was unreachable, so the code went where it could, and BLUEPRINT §2's rule was violated **by the build**, not by a decision anybody made. **Binding: when protocol behaviour appears outside `hydra-state`, first ask whether the correct home is reachable from where the code sits.** If it is not, the fix is the graph and the misplacement is a symptom; treating it as carelessness produces a review comment and leaves the cause. **Corollary: a rule the toolchain makes unfollowable will be broken silently and repeatedly, and the breakage will look like negligence.**

22. **⚑ WHEN CLOSING A §8 ITEM, VERIFY ITS SITE BEFORE ITS SUBSTANCE (design authority, 2026-08-24, from §7.57).** *A residual that misnames its own site is one someone will look for in the wrong place.* M4·0b's residual said the recovery strategy path lived in `pair.rs`; it lived in the **demo/CI binaries and five test files**, and `pair.rs` never drove a recovery at all. The substance was right, so the work was right — but a reader trusting the residual would have opened the wrong file, found nothing, and concluded the item was already closed. **Binding: before acting on an owed item, grep for the thing it names and confirm it is where the item says.** The cost of checking is one command; the cost of not checking is an item that looks done because its evidence was never where it claimed.

23. **⚑⚑ DOCUMENTATION THAT IS EXECUTED IS A TEST; DOCUMENTATION THAT IS ONLY READ IS A CLAIM (design authority, 2026-08-24, from §7.58).** The M4·3 quickstart was written carefully, reviewed, and **wrong in three places** — a DER file `curl` refuses, a `--dev` CA unrelated to the paired one, and a certificate SAN naming the device instead of the address clients dial. **Reading it found none of them; running it found all three in a single pass.** **Binding: any documented procedure a user is expected to follow is executed as written before it is published, and the record says when it was last executed.** A README's commands are as much a part of the tested surface as the code they invoke — and the rule-19 note is the deepest part of it: *every transport test dialled by DEVICE NAME, because that is how peers connect, so a DNS-only SAN and an IP-SAN certificate were indistinguishable to the entire suite.* The only caller that dials an **address** is the human following the quickstart.

24. **⚑ THE HONESTY RULES BIND NUMBERS THE DESIGN AUTHORITY SUPPLIES, EXACTLY AS THEY BIND THE AGENT'S OWN (design authority, ratified 2026-08-24).** *A handed-down figure is an input to verify, not an authority.* The M4·3 brief gave "~18k lines of hand-written Rust on ~676k lines of vendored C/C++"; measuring **this tree** gave ~21.7k `src/` Rust and ~729k vendored. The owner was quoting the **auditor's** measurement as though it were current; the agent measured and used the measured figures, naming the counting basis. **That is the required behaviour, not an exception to deference:** rule 16's *"job status is never evidence — at any layer, including the design authority's own summaries"* applies to quantities as well as verdicts. **Binding: a number arriving from any source is re-measured before it is published, and the basis is stated with it.**

25. **⚑⚑ A PROSE CAVEAT CANNOT PROTECT A MACHINE-READABLE CLAIM (design authority, ratified 2026-08-25, from §7.62).** *If a program emits a verdict token, the program must be capable of emitting the honest one.* **The instance:** `vendored-gguf` printed `verdict=GREEN` for a parser it had **never once called**, because the driver computed each verdict as `crashes.is_empty()` and **a no-op never crashes**. PROJECT_STATE §0(b) and the Wave-3 directive both said, correctly and repeatedly, that this target's CI status was *"unavailable, never green"* — **and nobody had checked whether the program agreed.** **Binding: audit every automated verdict for whether its FAILURE MODE IS EXPRESSIBLE.** A verdict computed from the absence of an event is only as strong as the code's ability to produce that event; where it cannot, the verdict must be a **distinct token** (`UNAVAILABLE`, `SKIPPED`, `INCONCLUSIVE`) that no grep and no reader can mistake for success, and the summary must **state the gap rather than omit it** (`unavailable=N`). **Why this is not merely rule 16 restated:** rule 16 says a CI outcome enters the record only as a quoted `verdict=` line — so the discipline that exists to stop unearned claims **would have transcribed this one faithfully**, and a session banking the leg per rule 16 would have written GREEN into a receipt in **perfect procedural compliance**. *The caveat lives in one file; the verdict travels in every log.* **This is rule 12 one layer deeper than it had been applied: not "the classifier may be wrong", but "the thing being classified may be lying, and the classifier has no way to know."** §7.31's lesson reaches its third layer here — test names, then a mechanism name, now a **verdict token**.

26. **⚑ A RESIDUAL MAY NAME ITS SITE CORRECTLY AND STILL BE WRONG ABOUT ITS BLAST RADIUS; AN ESTIMATE OF COST IS NOT EVIDENCE (design authority, ratified 2026-08-25, from §7.64).** *Rule 22 made us verify an owed item's **location**; this makes us distrust its **size**.* **The instance:** the `ACCEPT_LEGACY_ZERO_COMPLETION_HASH` row described itself as *"a single named constant… a one-line change with a compiler-visible effect"*. The site was real and correctly named — and the estimate was wrong in a way that mattered: the allowance was load-bearing for `hydra_wire::encode_finalize_activation` itself, hence for `hydra-worker::pair` and the three demo/CI binaries, hence for **`container-2node`**, so a flip-to-false would have turned the **standing multi-node verifier red**. It also exposed that the canonical *"activation succeeds"* test had **never once supplied real completion evidence**. **Binding: §8 rows state estimates AS ESTIMATES** — "expected one line", never "is one line" — and **no cost estimate is quoted as a reason to defer or to rush**; the only thing that establishes a change's blast radius is making the change and reading what breaks. **Corollary to rule 22, and its complement:** rule 22 costs one `grep`; this one costs one build, and both are cheaper than the item that looked done because nobody paid either.

> been gated.** M4·0 (coordinator driver) + M4·0b (recovery/lifecycle) + M4·0c (strategy path) ·
> M4·2 (pairing) · M4·3 (packaging, docs, README) · M4·4 (dashboard). **The external security

A control that asserts the buggy shape is valid is a harness that cannot express the failure it nominally guards.

>
> **⚑ THREAT-MODEL RULING (normative, BLUEPRINT §1.9 + spec scope): v1 assumes every certificate holder is HONEST.** mTLS + signed manifests defend against **outsiders and accidents**, never a **compromised stage**. What rests on it is named there. **Worker-compromise resistance is v2 scope.** **The assumption excuses malice; it never excuses accidents** — which is why H1's denial-of-activation and M1's unbounded hold are both fixed anyway.
>
> **⚑ STANDING LESSONS FROM THIS AUDIT (§7.31, §7.35):** *a test whose name promises more than its assertion is worse than no test, because it terminates inquiry* — and its design-layer twin, *a mechanism whose name promised authentication while providing misrouting prevention, thereby suppressing the question of where authentication actually lived*. **§7.35 is flagged as most likely to recur when v2 revisits worker trust.**

Two findings resolved this milestone: **F-UNSERVABLE** (§7.10, spec→v0.10.3) and **F-LIVENESS-FAIR** (§7.13, spec→v0.10.4, two defects incl. a shipping-code fix).

**M2 slices 2–4 COMPLETE + owner-RATIFIED (2026-07-13); slice 5 IN PROGRESS — sub-slices A & B done. Next work — sub-slice C (D1 recovery replay).**
- ✅ **Slice 5 sub-slice B DONE: the HTTP surface** (`hydra-coordinator` `session.rs`/`event_log.rs`/`server.rs`). axum `POST /v1/chat/completions`, streaming SSE. **The emit-after-commit gate is the law and structural:** an event is appended to the event log **only inside `Session::commit_group`, after `append_generation_commit` (the fdatasync'd durable write) returns** — so if durability stalls/fails, no event exists and nothing leaves the process past `generation_durable_pos`. **Proven by absence** (`emit_after_commit_gate_holds_by_absence`: a stubbed failing `fdatasync` → 0 events, durable_pos never advances). Dense stable SSE `id:`s; **`Last-Event-ID` resume yields a byte-identical suffix at *every* cut point** (`last_event_id_resume_yields_byte_identical_suffix`) — the `EventLog` is a pure function of the durable ledger prefix (derived, never parallel-tracked). Token pieces run through the slice-4 `Utf8Streamer` so **a multibyte glyph split across a group-commit boundary buffers and emits whole** (`multibyte_glyph_straddling_a_commit_boundary_emits_whole`, emoji straddle). `Idempotency-Key` dedups session creation (one session / one generation, same body). Group commit is **k=8 count OR 50ms deadline** (the deadline path tested); **bounded-buffer backpressure pauses at the commit stage** (slow client → no commit → no emit-ahead). **Named deferrals (per owner, recorded here):** (i) the `DELETE` **cancellation endpoint** wiring — I9's cutoff logic already lives in the M1 ledger (`Ledger::cancel`/`cancel_cutoff_pos`); the HTTP surface lands with hardening; (ii) the full `DETACHED` **TTL-detach choreography** (spec §8) — pausing works now, the timeout choreography follows; (iii) tool-call/`PAUSED_TOOL` flow — M3 scope as always. OpenAI-compat is minimal by design (one model, chat completions, streaming) — compat breadth is not correctness.
- ✅ **Slice 5 sub-slice A DONE: the real commit stream** (`crates/hydra-coordinator`, `commit_stream.rs`). `CommitStream` wraps the real `hydra-wal` `WalWriter` on a coordinator disk file (file header fdatasync'd + dir fsync'd before any record). `append_initial_commit` writes `INITIAL_COMMIT` (the three admission hashes from slice 4 + the config-defined initial checkpoint); `append_generation_commit` writes `GENERATION_COMMIT` per group commit embedding `snapshot(q)` for `last_output_pos` (relayed from the SAMPLED ring, re-embedded field-by-field — the coordinator holds no sampler, spec §1.4), with **I19's equalities validated on write** (`validate_generation_commit_i19`; a violating snapshot returns `CommitError::I19` and **nothing reaches the disk**). `generation_durable_pos`/`committed_sampler_checkpoint_id` advance **only after the fdatasync'd append returns** (the emit-after-commit substrate). `GroupCommitter` owns the k=8 count threshold (the 50ms deadline is the async loop's, sub-slice B). Tests (`tests/commit_stream.rs`): durable INITIAL + two GENERATION commits read back via the real `WalScan` with I19 holding on read; an I19-violating snapshot refused before the disk; group-commit thresholds at k=8. This is where M1's virtual-disk discipline meets a real file — the torn-write contract already proved the codec (`hydra-wal`), this proves the **wiring**.

(Nothing is lost: the solver P2·3 needs P1·2's banked real-hardware data regardless of when P2·3 runs.)
  - **P2·1 (Phase 2) — startup benchmark** (30–120 s sustained per device, EWMA-updated) — consumes P1·2's real data as its first fixtures. **✅ DONE 2026-08-22.** New **pure** crate **`hydra-sched`** (no engine, no I/O, no clocks — samples are handed in, as `hydra-state` is handed events, so every scheduling decision is deterministic and container-CI-testable): `capability.rs` with `Sample`/`SustainedBench`/`Measurement`/`Ewma`/`DeviceCapability`/`CapabilityRegistry`. **Guards, each for a specific placement hazard** (an over-stated device is handed too many layers and the pipeline runs at its slowest stage): a zero/NaN sample is **refused** (it would read as infinitely fast); an under-length run is **refused, never downgraded** to a weaker number; warm-up windows are discarded (cold windows read *slower* and would under-allocate); the estimate is a **median**, so one preemption spike cannot drag it; the EWMA **seeds on first update** (a zero-initialised "lower is faster" metric would make an unmeasured device win every layer); and an unmeasured device is **omitted, never defaulted**. `spread`/`is_stable()` report a noisy box instead of averaging it away. **`hydra-bench` upgraded to the sustained startup benchmark** (P1·2's short workload preserved verbatim behind `HYDRA_BENCH_QUICK=1` so `docs/heterogeneity.md` stays reproducible). **17 unit tests**, including the P1·2 fixtures: fed the recorded real-hardware numbers the pure allocator reproduces the recorded **capability ratio 4.0 : 2.1 : 1.0** *and* the **deployed 14/7/3 layer split** (`[0,14)`/`[14,21)`/`[21,24)`) exactly. **Two things found and recorded honestly, not smoothed over: (i)** a fixed-step window is **not comparable across a heterogeneity set** (24 steps ≈ 0.4 s on the Mac but ≈ 1.6 s on the slowest node, so the fast box is measured in windows short enough for OS jitter to dominate) — replaced with **duration-targeted (~1 s) windows**; spread fell **39.5 % → 16.6 %** while the estimate barely moved (0.693 → 0.674 ms/layer-tok), i.e. the fix removed **noise, not signal**. **(ii)** The first real sustained measurement (Mac, 2026-08-22) reads **0.674 ms/layer-tok / 61.8 tok/s**, versus P1·2's quick-mode **1.00 / 41.5** — because the quick mode measures one **cold** post-prefill window and the sustained mode discards warm-up and measures steady state. **P1·2's absolute numbers are warm-up-inclusive and understate steady-state capability; its RATIO — the thing that drives placement — stands, because all three nodes were measured identically. Numbers from the two modes must never be compared.** The Mac run reports **`spread=16.6 % → UNSTABLE`**, which is correct and was **not** tuned away: that box was concurrently running this session's builds and tests. **The threshold was not moved to make it pass.**
  - **P2·2 (Phase 2) — link prober + contention-group discovery** (full-mesh probe matrix). **✅ DONE 2026-08-22** — `hydra-sched::link`. **Full-mesh matrix over ORDERED pairs** (`probe_plan` → `n(n-1)`): links are **not symmetric**, and averaging the two directions would hide exactly the asymmetry that decides which end of a pipeline a stage belongs on (a home uplink is routinely an order of magnitude slower than its downlink). Per-link EWMA of **both** RTT and throughput, and `cost_ms(bytes) = rtt + bytes/throughput` — the number a **boundary crossing** actually costs, which is why bandwidth alone is not enough: P1·1b's real WAN runs were explicitly **latency-bound** (≈1.12 tok/s, `docs/wan-run.md`), and a 0.5 B boundary residual is only ~3.5 KB, so the RTT term dominates outright. **Contention groups** by union-find over solo-vs-concurrent throughput, **transitively closed** (A–B and B–C ⇒ one group). **The conservative bias is deliberate and asserted in a test:** the two errors are not symmetric — over-declaring contention costs some parallelism, whereas **missing** contention oversubscribes a shared bottleneck and makes the placement wrong *at runtime*, so an **unprobed pair is assumed to contend** (`assume_unprobed_contends`, opt-out only for an exhaustively-probed mesh). Contention is declared when **either** direction degrades, because a shared bottleneck usually starves one side first. Same hazard discipline as P2·1: self-links, zero/NaN/∞ RTT and throughput all **refused**, and an **unmeasured link has no cost and is never treated as free**. **15 tests**, including a fixture asserting the recorded `docs/wan-run.md` **topology shape** — Tailscale WAN leg vs sub-ms VNet leg — as an **ordering** constraint, deliberately not as absolute values, because that run banked "latency-bound WAN" and "sub-ms VNet" qualitatively and **never banked an RTT matrix**. No numbers were invented to look like data.
  - **P2·3 (Phase 2) — Halda-style placement solver** (exhaustive search over ≤3 stages × layer splits; HiGHS only if needed) — validated against P1·2's real-hardware data + the placement DoD (within 15 % of brute-force TPOT). **✅ DONE 2026-08-22** — `hydra-sched::solver`. Enumerates **every ordered device subset** (order matters — link cost is directional) × **every contiguous split**, filtered by per-device `max_layers`, priced with P2·1 capability + P2·2 link costs. **At M3's scale this solver IS the brute force, so it is optimal by construction rather than merely within 15 %** — and that is asserted by an independent re-enumeration in the tests, not claimed. **Memory is the binding constraint** (a solver without it answers a question nobody has: if the model fitted on the fastest node, Hydra would not exist) — and P2·10b is what made `max_layers` a real measured quantity. **Fixture validation (the DoD's ask):** under the pipelined objective with links cheap enough not to bottleneck, the solver reproduces **P1·2's deployed 14/7/3 split exactly** — every alternative is provably worse (15/6/3 → 15.0 ms, 13/8/3 → 15.1, 14/6/4 → 16.1 vs 14/7/3 → 14.0). **Hazard discipline carried through:** an unmeasured cluster **cannot be placed**; a model that does not fit is **refused, not squeezed**; and an **unpriced link disqualifies a placement** rather than being treated as free. **See §7.23 (F-TPOT-OBJECTIVE)** — building this surfaced that "TPOT" in the M3 DoD is ambiguous and the two readings pick different placements; both are implemented and the choice is **flagged for ratification, not made here**. **Not yet consumed:** P2·2's contention groups — a single-stream pipeline activates one link at a time, so contention bites on concurrent sessions and chunked prefill, i.e. at **P2·4 (admission control)** and **P2·7**. Recorded so the gap is visible rather than assumed handled.
  - **P2·4 (Phase 2) — admission control + KV-reservation. ✅ DONE 2026-08-22** — `hydra-sched::admission`. **Scope honesty, stated in the module doc:** v1 admission answers exactly one question — **"does this session fit, with headroom?"** — and is **not multi-tenancy** (spec §12 reserves multi-session for v2). **Everything is computed from measured quantities, nothing guessed:** shard weight bytes are the **real P2·10a/b splitter output** (a number that could not even be stated per device before sharded loading — every worker held the whole model); **KV bytes are computed from the model config** as `2 (K+V) × layers_on_THIS_stage × n_kv_head × head_dim × bytes_per_elem`, scaling with the stage's own layers rather than the whole model, and priced for the **FULL requested context** because KV grows during a session; capability and stability come from P2·1, links and contention groups from P2·2. **Headroom per §11 (numeric values from the 2026-08-22 ruling, since §11's prose defers to v0.8 without restating them): memory 15–30 %** — clamped, so a caller **cannot opt out by passing 0** — and **compute/thermal ≥ 20 %**. **Refuses, never squeezes:** there is deliberately **no "trim the context to make it fit" path — the API cannot express it**, so a smaller context must be *asked for* and the reduction is the caller's explicit decision. Every `Refusal` **names the device, demand, budget and shortfall** (a refusal that doesn't say what would have fitted isn't actionable), and **all** reasons are returned, not just the first. **P2·1's UNSTABLE flag gets a real consequence here** rather than remaining decoration: an unstable device is refused unless `allow_unstable` is set explicitly. **⚑ THIS CLOSES THE RECORDED CONTENTION GAP (§7.23's note).** Traffic classes — `BoundaryCopy`, `ShardTransfer`, `Heartbeat` — are priced against **one shared group budget**, per §11's "contention-group airtime shared by all traffic classes", so a shard rebuild saturating an uplink shows up as pressure on the boundary path instead of being accounted separately. **A group's capacity is the MINIMUM member throughput, never the sum** — the whole meaning of a shared bottleneck is that members cannot all run at solo rate at once, and summing would reintroduce exactly the optimism the grouping exists to prevent. **The machinery is v1's; the multi-session consumer is v2's.** **One real gap found and fixed while testing:** demand on a **never-probed** link lands in no discovered group and would have passed as *free* traffic — **being priced, not being grouped, is now the gate** (the tautological test that hid it was rewritten rather than the behaviour excused). **14 admission tests** (59 in the crate); clippy clean.
  - **P2·5 (Phase 2) — §11 stability contract** (min placement lifetime, one-migration-at-a-time, load-shed ladder, explicit termination). **✅ DONE 2026-08-22** — `hydra-sched::stability`. **Scope honesty in the module's first paragraph (as P2·4 did): this introduces NO new data-plane mechanism.** It emits a **decision** — hold / shed / re-place / terminate — as a **recommendation to the coordinator**; with one session and no live-migration machinery, **"re-place" means the recovery/supersession path already built and proven** (`BEGIN_RECOVERY` → catch-up → install → activate at epoch+1). **Nothing here moves a tensor.** It is also the **scheduler-side justification for the model's `SessionTerminate` arrow**, which until now existed only to keep the TLA+ liveness argument honest — it now has **real inputs**. **The contract:** minimum placement lifetime **10 min**; windowed-EWMA triggers **60 s**; **one migration at a time**; **load-shed before redistribution**; explicit termination. **The anti-flap pair is tested as a pair:** the 60 s window stops a spike being read as a trend, the 10 min floor stops even a *real* trend re-deciding faster than a placement can pay for itself. The flap test drives a device alternating good/4×-degraded every 30 s for an hour of simulated wall-clock and asserts **every** decision inside the floor is `Hold`, and that the contract does not act on every swing. **`migration_in_flight` ⇒ `Hold` unconditionally, including under hard failure** — the in-flight recovery is precisely the machinery that will handle a second loss, and starting a concurrent one is what §11 forbids. **Hard failure bypasses the TIMING guards (floor + window) but NOT the ADMISSIBILITY one** — you cannot wait ten minutes for a device that is gone, but you also never route around P2·4. **A candidate admission would refuse is never recommended** (tested under both the normal and the hard-failure path) — otherwise P2·4 is undone by the module that consumes it. **Termination names the exhausted rungs**, so it is an audit trail rather than an assertion, and is unreachable while any rung remains. **Ladder honesty:** rungs are `DeferBackgroundTransfers` → `ReducePrefillChunk` → `PauseNewAdmissions`, walked in canonical order regardless of input order; **`Heartbeat` is deliberately NOT sheddable** (P2·4 prices it precisely so it is never starved); and **`PauseNewAdmissions` is labelled INERT IN v1** (`is_effective_in_v1()`, tested) so the ladder is honest about its own shape rather than pretending v1's ladder is the whole ladder. **16 stability tests** (75 in the crate); clippy clean.
  - **P2·6 (Phase 2) — thermal/memory telemetry in heartbeats. ✅ DONE 2026-08-22** — `hydra-sched::telemetry` (pure) + `hydra-worker::telemetry` (the OS-touching collector), the same split as P2·1. The `Heartbeat` table's five fields have existed in `hydra-proto` since M0 (`queue_depth`, `mem_headroom_mib`, `soc_temp_dc`, `throttled`, `on_battery`); nothing filled them until now. **PROVENANCE IS PART OF THE VALUE — no sensor is invented.** Every field carries `Measured` / `BestEffort` / `Unavailable`, and an `Unavailable` field **carries no value at all**, so "no thermal zone" can never be read as "0 °C" or "plenty of headroom". Consumers needing certainty call `measured_only()`. **Real platform reads:** Linux prefers **cgroup v2** `memory.max`/`memory.current` (inside a container `/proc/meminfo` reports the HOST's memory and would wildly overstate headroom), falling back to `MemAvailable` as best-effort; `/sys/class/thermal`, the core-throttle counter and `/sys/class/power_supply` are read when present and are **`Unavailable` when not — which in a container is most of them.** macOS uses `vm_stat`/`pmset` at heartbeat cadence (never per-token) and reports **`soc_temp_dc` as `Unavailable`: macOS exposes no public SoC-temperature API and this project will not take on private SMC/IOKit access for a telemetry field.** **Two consumers, both wired:** `capability_is_stale()` feeds **P2·1** (a throttling or on-battery box is not the machine that was benchmarked — and it uses `.get()` not `.measured_only()`, a deliberate asymmetry since acting on a soft positive costs a benchmark while ignoring one costs a bad placement); `pressure()` feeds **P2·5**, returning **`Unknown` — never `Nominal` — when nothing is exposed**, because a platform with no sensors must not silently disable the stability triggers on exactly the platform the standing multi-node verifier runs on. Headroom is EWMA-smoothed with P2·1's own `Ewma` (reused, not reimplemented) so one allocation dip is not "memory pressure". **⚑ REAL READING FROM THE DEV MAC (2026-08-22), and an unforced cross-check:** `queue_depth 0 Measured` · `mem_headroom_mib 1324 BestEffort` · `soc_temp_dc None Unavailable` · `throttled None Unavailable` (this box's `pmset -g therm` reports no `CPU_Speed_Limit`, so the doc table was **corrected to observed reality** rather than left at my assumption) · **`on_battery Some(true) BestEffort`** — **which retroactively explains P2·1's `UNSTABLE` capability reading on the same box: the sustained benchmark ran on battery power**, and `capability_is_stale()` returns true for exactly that reason. The two slices agree without having been made to. **15 tests** (87 in `hydra-sched`) + 3 collector tests incl. macOS-specific "does not pretend to read SoC temperature"; clippy clean.
  - **P2·7 (Phase 2) — chunked prefill** (feeds P1·4 if it lands in time). **✅ DONE 2026-08-23 — the last data-plane slice.** **Spec §2.4 to the letter:** the durable event is **`INPUT_CHUNK_COMMIT` (WAL record id 4** — in `WAL-FORMAT.md` and `wal-records.fbs` since v0.10.1, waiting for exactly this slice), added as `CommitStream::append_input_chunk_commit`, and **`prefill_stable_pos` advances ONLY after that record's `fdatasync` returns** — the same emit-after-commit discipline the generation side uses, so an interrupted prefill truncates to a position genuinely on disk. The record carries `boundary_durable_through` per edge so a recovering coordinator can tell what was safe at commit time. **A chunk that would move the watermark backwards (replay, an earlier chunk, an inverted range) is a structured error, never a silent no-op** — and a refused chunk provably leaves the watermark untouched. **Chunk sizing is an admission-time decision** (`hydra-sched::prefill`), **planned against objective (b) per the §7.23 ruling and labelled as such** — prefill is the one place in v1 where multiple positions genuinely are in flight. **The sizing reasoning is written down because it is counter-intuitive:** chunk size is **not** a bandwidth lever (a chunk of *n* positions carries *n* boundaries and takes ≈ *n* × per-position compute, so the rate is unchanged); what it moves is **peak retained memory** — §2.4 releases R3′ per **chunk**, not at end-of-prefill — and **commit cadence** (one `fdatasync` per chunk). So the plan is the largest chunk whose retained boundaries fit the admitted budget, floored at 8 positions so the barrier is amortised. **A budget below that floor is REFUSED, not shrunk** (a sub-floor chunk turns prefill into a disk-barrier storm — the same silent degradation P2·4 refuses), while a segment that is merely *small* still plans fine. **Evidence:** `crates/hydra-worker/tests/chunked_prefill.rs` — **`chunked_prefill_is_bit_exact_with_unchunked_prefill` green on the standing rule-14 harness** (multi-chunk plan, real `CommitStream`, final logits BLAKE3-equal to the unchunked golden), plus the watermark, monotonicity, and two interruption tests (resume from the durable chunk boundary with **every position applied exactly once**; and death before *any* chunk commits resumes from position 0). Planner: 8 tests incl. exact tiling with no gap/overlap and a monotonicity property (more budget never yields more chunks). **⚠️ HONEST LIMITATION, recorded not glossed:** the equivalence test drives the pipeline **continuously** and appends the chunk commits per chunk alongside it — it does **not pause at chunk boundaries**, so it proves the digest equality and the watermark walk but **not** behaviour when a chunk's `fdatasync` *stalls* mid-prefill (the back-pressure case the generation side proves by absence). Closing it needs a chunk-boundary-aware driver in `pair.rs`; **owed in §8.**
  - **P2·8 (Phase 2) — D0 mode. ✅ DONE 2026-08-23.** The explicit configuration where boundary durability is **off** and recovery is **Strategy-B only** (full teacher-forced rebuild from the durable token ledger). **A real defect found and fixed:** `DurableForwarder::copy_and_retain` emitted a `BOUNDARY_COPY` **unconditionally** — only the *release rule* varied by mode — so a "D0" run was still paying the full boundary overhead it exists to avoid. D0 now **does not send the copy at all**; retention still happens for the in-flight window and releases on the downstream ack alone. **Mode plumbing made explicit:** `DurabilityMode::{D0, D1}` replaces reasoning about a bare `require_durable` bool at the API surface (the wire/bootstrap flag is unchanged, so nothing re-encodes). **THE LOAD-BEARING TEST IS THE ABSENCE ONE** — "zero boundary overhead" is not a claim that copies are cheaper, it is a claim that **there are none**, and a D0 run emitting one is a **defect, not an inefficiency**. `crates/hydra-worker/tests/d0_mode.rs::a_d0_run_emits_no_boundary_copy_traffic_whatsoever` drives 12 boundaries over **real mTLS** to a durability endpoint that **counts every frame it receives**, and asserts the count is **exactly 0** — asserted at the receiver, never inferred from the sender's account of itself, and with a settle delay so the absence test cannot race. **Paired with a control** (`the_same_drive_in_d1_does_emit_boundary_copies` → exactly 12) so the zero is provably caused by the mode and not by a harness that could never have counted anything — the same pairing principle as the cert legs. Plus a side-by-side release-rule test making the trade visible: D0 releases the applied prefix with **no** `DURABILITY_ACK`; D1 holds until it arrives. **Honest doc (spec §7):** D0 buys no copy traffic, no durability-target disk and no `DURABILITY_ACK` on the release path, and **pays in recovery cost** — rebuilding re-runs the whole prefix from tokens instead of replaying boundaries. Right when the link is precious and recoveries are rare; D1 when a long context makes a full rebuild the expensive event. **⚠️ SCOPE, stated precisely: P2·8 did NOT write a new kill-recovery test, because the Strategy-B machinery is already proven at the three-assertion bar** — C part 2's `crates/hydra-worker/tests/d1_recovery.rs::d1_recovery_three_kill_windows_are_byte_identical_to_an_uninterrupted_seeded_run` is exactly the **D0-class** shape (single full-range S_P, catch-up replaying **raw committed tokens** from the durable ledger, no boundary durability). Re-proving it under a new name would be ceremony. **What IS owed: a D0-mode kill on the FORWARDING topology** (the existing test is the full-range-S_P shape, not the multi-stage direct-FWD one) — recorded in §8, not implied here.
  - **P2·9 (Phase 2) — tool-call flow: `PAUSED_TOOL` + `SEGMENT_COMMIT` + the I24 family closure** (the deferred `mutation_candidate_leak` 6th mutation + `mutation_preactive_maroon`; §8 owed items).
  - **P2·10 (Phase 2) — `hydra-modelsvc` / per-shard weights (gate condition (ii)) — ✅✅ COMPLETE 2026-08-22: (a) DONE (`b3aa56f`, `4f29edb`), (b) DONE (`0d3dc92` + this commit). **gate-cond-(ii) CLOSED.** *(This row read "THE NEXT SLICE (not started)" until 2026-08-22 — stale from the moment P2·10a landed; swept in the authority-ruling reconciliation.)* GGUF splitter → per-stage shard files + signed manifest; the engine loads only assigned layers. **Binding points + sub-slice plan (P2·10a/P2·10b) are in the ⚡ CURRENT-STATE block at the top of §0(c)** (manifest-as-contract, splitter determinism, shard-load bit-exact = real DoD, memory delta, caveat removal, mmap escalation). **Note: this also unblocks 7B/70B** — sharded weights mean each worker loads only its layers, retiring the "full model per worker" memory ceiling (§8 70B/7B owed items).
  - **Standing multi-node verifier:** the container two-node CI (extended to 3-node in P1·1's fallback) runs on every push through Phase 2.
  - **M3 DoD:** 3-node cluster picks a placement within 15 % of brute-force TPOT — **⚡ RESTATED 2026-08-22 (authority-ruled, cite §7.23): the gate objective is (a) `SingleStreamLatency`, and the 15 % clause is a CALIBRATION check — the deployed placement's MEASURED TPOT must land within 15 % of the solver's PREDICTED TPOT on the same measured inputs.** Comparing the search against itself is vacuous (enumeration is optimal by construction and independently asserted); what must be shown is **the cost model against reality**. Objective (b) `PipelinedThroughput` is reported informationally alongside every placement and is the planning objective for **chunked prefill (P2·7)**; a placement need not satisfy both. Remaining M3 DoD: chaos suite (`tc netem` jitter, worker kills, disk-full on WAL) nightly with **zero silent corruption** (golden-token replay of every recovered session); the Thursday-night scenario degrades p99 but never correctness. **Wired-LAN perf envelope** (§8) is due at this gate — but with no cloud VMs after Aug 5, a real *wired-LAN* run needs the owner's hardware decision (flagged).
  - **Topology (owner-approved): single full-range S_P worker, labeled D0-class.** One worker hosts `[0,-1)` + sampler; catch-up replays **raw committed tokens** from the durable ledger (no boundary-durability needed). **Binding follow-up welded to the worker→worker FWD slice:** the two-stage (multi-node) D1 recovery — where S_P's KV is rebuilt from durably-copied boundaries (`BOUNDARY_COPY`) with S1 survivor-preserved — lands with FWD; C here proves the recovery *machinery* (Case A → catch-up → install → activate → resume), not multi-node boundary transport.
  - **Three orchestration gaps found in C-discovery (2026-07-13), all rule-10 IMPLEMENTATION (they change how effects are carried, not what the SM decides → proceed + log, NO ratification pause; classifications owner-ratified):** (1) **recovery-start state** — the worker builds its stage `Stage::frozen_ready` (`worker.rs`), but BEGIN_RECOVERY **Case A** (`stage.rs:162`) needs state ∈ `{ActiveFinal,Frozen,Preactive}`; a recovery-replacement worker must **start `Frozen` at the base epoch** (entry-path decision, ratified). (2) **catch-up unwired** — the stage SM has `RebuildStep{goal}` but the worker has **no wire mapping** for catch-up (`wire.rs` has none); wire `CATCH_UP_CONTEXT{goal}`→`RebuildStep`, coupled to `REBUILD_APPLY`(=`APPLY_TOKEN` NO_SAMPLE) which rebuilds the engine KV, so the stage reaches `FrozenReady` through the **real** SM. (3) **`Ready` dropped** — `StageEffect::Ready`→`None` (`worker.rs`); emit a `CATCH_UP_READY`/`READY` frame so the coordinator observes catch-up completion. **Any fix that would change what the SM *decides* (hydra-state transition logic / spec / model) → STOP + full rule-10 classification + ratification pause, as F-UNSERVABLE did. The line: does the fix change the decision, or only how effects are carried?**
  - **Recovery flow through the real machinery only (no shortcut around DST-tested paths — even "temporarily" — is the arch defect):** detection (connection death / missed heartbeats) → `BEGIN_RECOVERY` **Case A** through the real stage SM → **catch-up** (`REBUILD_APPLY` of the durable committed tokens, rebuilding KV exactly) → `INSTALL_SAMPLER_CHECKPOINT` **from the last durable `GENERATION_COMMIT`'s snapshot** (read back from the commit stream) → activation transaction → `SAMPLE_NEXT` at **goal+1**.
  - **Three adversarial kill −9 windows (all required — one lucky point is a demo, three hostile ones are evidence):** (1) **mid-decode steady state**; (2) **inside the provisional sampled-ahead window** — S_P has sampled past `generation_durable_pos` (the I7b/I15 truncation case this obligation exists for: recovery must discard provisional sampled outputs above `truncate_to` and restore the installed checkpoint); (3) **between a group commit's fsync and its emission**.
  - **Three assertions, all required — client truth AND disk truth, independently:** (a) **SSE id continuity** across recovery (no gap, no repeat); (b) **post-recovery byte stream ⊕ pre-kill prefix == byte-identical to an uninterrupted seeded run** of the same session (seeded sampling makes the whole comparison exact — use it); (c) **durable side agrees** — the commit stream read back post-recovery satisfies **I19** and contains **no position twice** (a retry may never append).
  - **Timing:** log detection→resumed-stream wall time, **honesty-annotated** (local-pair, dev machine — **not** the <15 s D1 target, a LAN/M3 number).
  - **On green:** close the I7b/I15 obligation in §0(c)/§8 **with the test names**; then **flag Tailscale-VM readiness and PAUSE** per the amended DoD (owner provisions the VM ~15 min).
  - **Sub-commit seams:** the three orchestration gap fixes land as **one commit** (green intermediate on `main`), then the harness + assertions as the next. **Self-monitoring rule (owner, binding): if output quality degrades before C completes (assertion sloppiness, re-reading already-read files, losing the three-assertion structure) → STOP at the nearest green commit, refresh §0, and say so. A paused C is recoverable; a false-green C is the one failure this project exists to prevent.**
- ✅ **Slice 4 DONE: tokenizer + incremental detokenizer** (`crates/hydra-tokenizer`). **Binding decision honored — tokenization is delegated to llama.cpp's own tokenizer**, exposed through `hydra-engine-sys` (added `hydra_model_load_vocab_only`, `hydra_tokenize_ex` with `add_special`/`parse_special`, `hydra_token_to_piece`); the **vocab-only load path works** (no weights → memory-light coordinator; not a full-model dev artifact). Incremental **UTF-8-boundary-safe detokenizer** (`Utf8Streamer`): pieces are bytes, an incomplete trailing multibyte sequence is buffered, genuinely-invalid bytes → U+FFFD, and **no emitted chunk is ever invalid UTF-8** (spec I6 substrate). Tests: (a) incremental detok == batch over multi-byte codepoints (emoji) split across tokens; (b) pure fuzz over random byte-splits never corrupts/never emits invalid; (c) round-trip tokenize→detok == the llama.cpp reference on a fixed prompt set. **Admission hashes** `tokenizer_hash` (BLAKE3 over the whole vocab), `chat_template_hash` (canonical template identity), `rendered_prompt_bytes_hash` — computed exactly as `INITIAL_COMMIT` (spec §2.6a) defines and wired now (embedded in the commit path next slice). Chat rendering is a minimal fixed **ChatML** template for the pinned families (hash correctness over generality). **`§0(c)` D1-replay obligation on record:** D1 recovery replay must include **S_P ring truncation + checkpoint restore per I7b/I15** (Case A `BEGIN_RECOVERY` discards provisional sampled outputs above `truncate_to` and restores the installed checkpoint — the consumer contract for slice 3's producer machinery).
- ✅ **Slice 2 DONE + RATIFIED** (both sub-slices, the coordinator-relayed-FWD interim, per-position-batching parity, provisioned-cert plumbing — all owner-ratified 2026-07-13). `hydra-worker` = thin effect executor over real mTLS; single-worker echo (control-plane `COMMIT/FINALIZE_ACTIVATION` through the DST-tested SM + engine `APPLY_TOKEN`→logits digest + F1 rejection); two-worker `--local-pair` teacher-forced NO_SAMPLE **bit-exact anchor** (boundary serialized→transmitted→injected; S2 logits == unsplit bit-exactly), proven in-process + across two real OS-process workers via `hydra-local-pair`; `kill -9`/restart switch (`SubprocessWorker::kill9`). **The two-process bit-exact anchor is now the standing M2 regression gate (rule 14) — no later slice may land red against it.**
- ✅ **Slice 3 DONE: sampler @ S_P** (spec §2.6a/§2.6b; **I14/I15/I17/I19-producer**). `hydra_worker::sampler`: **Philox4x32-10** counter-based keyed RNG (`rng_key`/`rng_counter`; advances only in a stochastic step), repetition-penalty window, top-p/temperature, greedy=argmax. `SAMPLE_NEXT`→`SAMPLED{post_sample_state_snapshot(q)}` with per-position `SamplerCheckpointRec` snapshots (the snapshot ring, built at S_P now — producer contract first). Verified over the two-worker pipeline (`tests/sampler_pipeline.rs`): (a) **greedy across the pipeline == unsplit argmax, bit-exact** (`greedy_sample_across_pipeline_matches_unsplit_argmax` — extends the anchor); (b) **seeded sampling identical across two full independent runs** (`seeded_sampling_is_reproducible_across_two_full_runs`); (c) **`SAMPLE_NEXT` idempotent from the SAMPLED cache, no RNG re-advance** (`duplicate_sample_next_is_idempotent_and_does_not_advance_rng` — closes §8 owed item + gate-(c) note); (d) `INSTALL_SAMPLER_CHECKPOINT`→`INSTALLED` (`install_sampler_checkpoint_round_trips_over_mtls`, I17). `expected_sampler_checkpoint_id`/config-hash drift is **fatal** (`ERR_CHECKPOINT_MISMATCH`, never silently repaired). **Ownership boundary honored** (rule 15): coordinator builds only the config-defined initial checkpoint; S_P produces all snapshots. Grammar deferred (empty). `WorkerConfig`/bootstrap carry the session `SamplingConfig`.
- **Remaining M2 slices per BLUEPRINT §3** (each its own commit-and-push per slice): tokenizer + incremental UTF-8-safe detok at the coordinator, OpenAI `/v1/chat/completions` + SSE ids + `Last-Event-ID` resume + `Idempotency-Key`, **full D1 recovery replay** (stateful-prefill resume, against the existing kill-switch) — **D1 replay includes S_P ring truncation + checkpoint restore per I7b/I15** (Case A `BEGIN_RECOVERY`: discard provisional sampled outputs above `truncate_to`, restore the installed checkpoint — the consumer contract for the slice-3 producer machinery), worker→worker `FWD` (currently coordinator-relayed for the anchor), the **containerized two-node CI** test (`docker kill` = kill −9, a permanent workflow), 7B then 70B, and the **Tailscale VM data point** (owner provisions — **flag readiness and pause for the VM details; the flag comes when the OpenAI/SSE slice makes an end-to-end run meaningful, not before**) — toward the **amended M2 DoD**; wired-LAN perf → §8/M3; honesty rule §8.

The gate-evidence-table *format* (a)–(f) is preserved in `verification/M1-GATE.md`; reuse its shape for the M2 gate.

### (d) Local-environment facts a fresh session must know

> invocations** — they contend for the target lock and the stall looks exactly like a deadlock in

- **JDK:** `/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk/bin/java` (OpenJDK 26). **TLC jar** = `verification/tools/tla2tools.jar` (**v1.7.4**, sha256 `936a262061c914694dfd669a543be24573c45d5aa0ff20a8b96b23d01e050e88`) — **git-ignored; must be re-fetched** from `https://github.com/tlaplus/tlaplus/releases/download/v1.7.4/tla2tools.jar`.
- **macOS has no `timeout`** (CI Ubuntu does); concurrent TLC runs need a unique `-metadir`.
- **Build:** `cargo test --workspace` (**55 tests**; crates: hydra-proto, hydra-wal, hydra-transport, hydra-state, hydra-sim, **hydra-engine-sys**). Spike: `cmake` in `spike/` against `vendor/llama.cpp/build`; test model in `models/` is **git-ignored** (re-download Qwen2.5-0.5B fp16).
- **`hydra-engine-sys` (M2):** `build.rs` compiles a C++ shim and links the vendored **build-tree** dylibs at `vendor/llama.cpp/build/bin` (a **dev-environment assumption**; portable packaging = M4). If that tree is absent it degrades to a stub (`cfg(engine_unavailable)`) so the workspace still builds; the bit-exact test skips without the git-ignored model. `-p hydra-sim`/CI never build it (so marathon/tlc CI is unaffected).

### (e) C2 — delegated M1-completion protocol (mechanical; no owner round-trip; ratified 2026-07-12)
M1 is **PASSED code-side**; the full flip is delegated. **⚠️ Amended 2026-07-13:** (e) now also carries **F-LIVENESS-FAIR (§7.13)** — BaselineLiveness `Progress` is violated (fairness artifact, escalated, **paused for owner ratification**); the full M1 flip additionally requires that finding resolved. The mechanical `-recover`/fixpoint/Mut3 protocol below is unchanged; use the **fixed** workflow (run 29206917524's checkpoints uploaded — dispatch `recover=true` to continue and observe the round-trip). When the run (and any `-recover` successors) concludes, act per its outcome — do **not** re-ask the owner *for the mechanical items*:
1. **All green** — baseline-safety fixpoint clean **and** Mut3 fired **and** the first `-recover` checkpoint round-trip observed at a 6 h boundary → record in `verification/VERIFICATION-README.md` + PROJECT_STATE §6, **flip §4's M1 row to PASSED (full)**, log §12. **M2 opens immediately.**
2. **Mut3 completes CLEAN (a genuine drain, not timeout/SIGTERM)** → fire the contingency ladder: rung (a) CI rerun with `StateConstraint |msgs| ≤ 30`; still clean → rung (b) `MaxAttempt = 3`; only rung (c) escalates with state-space stats. **M2 may open in parallel only if `baseline-safety` and `-recover` are green** (the sim's Mut3 parity at median 187 is standing evidence the harm is real — a bounded-model miss is a model-bounds fact, not a protocol doubt).
3. **Any baseline-safety violation or `-recover` failure** → **M2 stays closed**; escalate with the trace/logs per the escalate-with-traces rule.
The `-recover` round-trip is the single untested link in the CI chain — it must be **observed, not assumed**.

## 1. Project identity

**Hydra** is an open-source, trusted-LAN inference runtime that runs a single large open-weight LLM (70B-class dense; MoE later) by **pipeline-sharding contiguous layer ranges across 2–3 heterogeneous desktop-class machines** (CUDA desktop, Apple Silicon Macs, CPU nodes). Its differentiator is not speed — physics caps a 70B at roughly 2–7 tok/s on wired desktop hardware — but **correctness under failure**: crash-safe sessions, exactly-once token semantics, teacher-forced recovery, and generation streams that survive any single machine dying mid-sentence without duplicating or losing a single visible token.

Phones are **not** workers in v1 (clients/draft-hosts only, later). One active session per model instance. WAN, MoE, speculative decoding, paged KV, and untrusted swarms are reserved hooks, not v1 scope.

The project was produced through an unusual pipeline worth knowing: research report → protocol spec hardened across **nine adversarial expert-review rounds** (v0.1→v0.9, each round finding real transition-boundary bugs) → **TLA+/TLC model checking** (which found a tenth defect that all nine human rounds missed) → an implementation blueprint executed by an autonomous coding agent, gated at every milestone by a design authority. The layering rule that emerged is doctrine: *every defect is caught by a more mechanical layer than the one that created it* (prose → spec → model checker → simulator → chaos tests). Defend this layering against any urge to shortcut it.

## 2. Authority & document map (reading order for a new agent/human)

| Order | File | Role | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `PROJECT_STATE.md` | You are here — current truth | Narrative authority |
| 1 | `BLUEPRINT.md` | What to build, order, DoD gates, fixed decisions | Process authority; may not be re-decided by an agent |
| 2 | `docs/hydra-session-protocol.md` (**v0.10.4**; + v0.10.2 boundary-precision amendment in BLUEPRINT) | Messages, state machines, **invariants I1–I25** | **Correctness authority — on any conflict, this wins** |
| 3 | `docs/hydra-proto.fbs` + `docs/wal-records.fbs` (crate build copy in `crates/hydra-proto/schemas/`) | Wire + WAL payload schemas | Generated code is source of truth; shadow structs forbidden |
| 4 | `docs/WAL-FORMAT.md` | On-disk format, fsync rules, torn-write contract | Governs `hydra-wal` byte-for-byte |
| 5 | `verification/` (TLA+ model + 6 configs + README) | Machine-checked transition core + mutation tests | CI gate for any transition-logic change |
| 6 | `docs/federated-llm-inference-report.md` | Research rationale, landscape, feasibility | Consult for *why*; don't re-litigate |

**Standing process rules:** (a) an agent proceeds autonomously *within* a milestone; a human gates *between* milestones via each DoD; (b) any finding that changes a package decision (defaults, formats, scope) **pauses for ratification** before the next milestone; findings that don't, proceed and are logged here; (c) verification deviations **escalate with traces** — the model/spec is never adjusted to make a run pass; (d) **commit attribution:** the **owner is the primary author + committer on every commit** (repo-local `user.name`/`user.email` = owner's GitHub noreply); every commit ends with `Co-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>`; **git history rewrites are prohibited** (one authorized rewrite was done 2026-07-11 to backfill attribution; never again); (e) **push cadence:** small logical commits, conventional messages, **push after every commit** — local never leads remote by more than a day; (f) **TLC runs locally are smoke-only** (parse, Mut2, Mut4); baseline/liveness/Mut1/Mut3 are **CI-owned** (`.github/workflows/tlc.yml`) — thermal policy, §9.

## 6. Verification status (the certification ledger)

### 6.0 — THE "CANNOT SEE" REGISTER (standing rule 19, opened 2026-08-23)

> **Binding:** every gate below states what it **structurally cannot falsify**. A gate without a
> line here is not yet trustworthy. Where the gap matters, the entry names the **adversarial driver**
> that closes it — because rule 19(b) says the fix is a driver, not a note.

| Gate | What it proves | **CANNOT SEE** | Closing driver |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Boundary durability** (`boundary_durability.rs`, `durable_live.rs`, the 3-node durability edges) | boundaries are persisted before release; `DURABILITY_ACK` gates R3′ | **A hole.** Every driver fed one boundary per position, in order, every write succeeding — under which a `max()` frontier and a contiguous frontier are *the same function*, so no test could distinguish them (H5/M7) | ✅ **CLOSED (§7.40)** — `boundary_durability_h5.rs` drives out-of-order, duplicate, foreign-fenced and gapped inputs |
| **Rule-14 bit-exact anchors** (`local_pair`, `direct_worker_to_worker_fwd`, shard, chunked-prefill) | split == unsplit, byte-for-byte, on the happy path | **Retransmits** (they never retry), **stale epochs** (one epoch per run), **unactivated serves** (they now activate, but the anchor asserts arithmetic, not eligibility), **gaps**, out-of-order positions, and any adversarial peer | ✅ **CLOSED for the named four** by `idempotency.rs` (Wave 1d) — a deliberately misbehaving driver that resends, skips, speaks the wrong epoch and serves unactivated, then re-asserts byte-identity against a clean run |
| **DST sim** (`hydra-sim`, 1000+ seeds × 10M steps, six mutation-parity switches) | I1–I25 under adversarial schedules, drops, dups, reorder, crash-restart, torn virtual disk | ~~The UNSERVABLE/TERMINATE fsync window~~ ✅ **CLOSED (§7.40)** — the hard-coded `fsync` is gone; those records now take the `pending_wal → WalDurable` path, so the crash window is schedulable like any other. Still cannot see: **no real disk**, **no engine**, **no wire bytes** (it drives `hydra-state` directly, so a codec or FFI defect is invisible to it) | ✅ Wave 2 seam 2 |
| **Fuzz targets** (8: gguf, frame-header, wire-body, manifest, bootstrap, wal-record, boundary-record, **vendored-gguf** — the last **UNAVAILABLE in CI**, §7.62) | the *parsers* do not panic, abort, or hand out unproven slices on hostile bytes | **`Worker::on_frame`** (it stops at `wire::decode`, so the role gate, the C4 fence, M9 sequencing and every SM transition are unreached), **the sampler**, and — as an oracle — it has **no timeout** (a hang is not a crash) and **no allocation ceiling** (a large-but-legal reservation is not a crash). ~~the engine/FFI~~ ✅ **the vendored GGUF parser is now driven (H6, §7.43)** — and it **aborted on its first sweep**. ⚠️ **The generator's own reach is part of this line:** its array-nesting cap made H12 unreachable (§7.44); a cap in a generator is an unstated bound on every verdict it produces | ⏳ **OWED (§8)** — an `on_frame` target with a fence-tuple corpus; a wall-clock watchdog per case; an allocation-ceiling harness. Named rather than implied |
| **TLC** (`BaselineSafety`, `BaselineLiveness`, Mut1–Mut4) | the transition core at standing bounds: safety to fixpoint, liveness under per-class fairness, all four mutations fire | **Ill-formed messages** — the model *constructs* every message, so a receiver's handling of a malformed one is outside its reach entirely (this is exactly how H2 and H3 survived: the code was weaker than the model and TLC could not tell). Also **unbounded values** (`MaxEpoch = 1`, `MaxAttempt = 2`, `MaxPos = 1`), and **anything below the transition core** (no wire, no disk, no engine) | ⏳ **OWED (§8)** — the audit is the current oracle for malformed input; a receiver-fuzz layer against `hydra-state` would close it |
| **Transport & provisioning** (`security_checklist.rs`, the mTLS suites, every `pair.rs` endpoint) | real mTLS, role binding at `accept()`, wildcard-bind refusal, frame caps | ~~A peer that misbehaves at all~~ ✅ **CLOSED (§7.45)** — `platform_hardening.rs` drives a silent peer, an undelivered payload, the IPv4-mapped wildcard, and reads certificate validity out of the DER. Still cannot see: **connection-count exhaustion** (no semaphore yet, §8) and a **slow-but-not-silent** handshake | ✅ Wave 3 |

Also: **that they only ever run on ONE machine, whose submodule working tree carries an uncommitted patch.** Every green engine result is evidence about a tree no clean checkout reproduces, and CI never builds the engine at all (L1) | 🔶 **PARTIAL (§7.45)** — `build.rs` now refuses to build an unpatched engine and `patch_integrity.rs` asserts the patch matches the committed diff; **pinning a fork SHA is owed (§8, owner action)** |
