> **⚑ M3 TRACK-A GATE CLOSED 2026-08-23 (recorded here because §6 is the certification ledger, and a gate verdict is a certification).** `verification/M3-GATE.md` — rows 1–14 MET, row 15 carried as a permanent hardware-contingent annotation. The one row that had been open, **row 8 (placement calibration)**, closed on its own receipt: `verification/ci-results/m3-calibration-2026-08-23.md`, **worst error 2.9 % across two out-of-sample splits against a 15 % gate**, measured on the **production-shaped** path (real sampling, no digest witness) per the §7.25 ruling. **Rule 16 applies to it as to any receipt:** the numbers in that file are quoted from the test's own stdout, and the test is `#[ignore]`-gated because it needs the engine and a git-ignored model — so **this receipt, not a CI check-mark, is how the result enters the record.** **What the M3 verdict does NOT certify:** any wired-LAN performance figure (row 15, owed and unmeasurable without hardware), the contention-group prober against a real RTT matrix (row 6 is green **as logic only**), or any claim that splitting is faster than not splitting (§7.23 — for a model that fits on one device it is not, and row 8's measured 4.7 ms/stage fixed cost strengthens that). **The M3 chaos receipts** are `verification/ci-results/chaos-jitter-32612884434.md` (`verdict=GREEN … held under tc netem jitter`, netem verified installed in the log) and `crates/hydra-coordinator/tests/chaos_disk.rs`.


> **⚡ 2026-08-22 dormancy read (rule 16, quoted).** The TLC workflow now runs **weekly on a schedule** (not `-recover`-chained). Latest = run **32002980633 (Aug-17)**, big legs read: `config=baseline-safety expect=clean violated=0 complete=0 verdict=INCONCLUSIVE`; `config=mut3 expect=violation violated=0 complete=0 verdict=INCONCLUSIVE`. Each weekly run is a **fresh 5h27m time-box** that cannot drain the 320M+-state space → baseline-safety never reaches fixpoint, Mut3 never fires; **⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-08-22 (§7.21 F-UNBOUNDED-SEGMENT): this capacity diagnosis was wrong — the baselines were not merely too slow, the model admitted UNBOUNDED-LENGTH behaviours (`installedCkpt`/`segCommitted` grew forever), so TLC crashed on its 65535-state behaviour-length ceiling and NO time-box, however large, could ever have drained them. Repaired by adding `MaxCkpt` to the core model.** the two `-fast` legs still fail (known §7.17). **M1 full-flip STILL BLOCKED — no GREEN, nothing flipped.** **Structural problem:** the scheduler restarts fresh each week (accumulates no progress), so the `-recover ≤3 rounds` chaining strategy is not actually running. **PROPOSED (tier-2, per the owner-ratified pre-authorization, §8): a reduced-bound certification config that DRAINS within the time-box** — `BaselineSafetyCert.cfg` at `MaxCrashes = 1` + a tighter `StateConstraint` (|msgs| smaller than the current hardcoded ≤20, exposed as a model constant) so it reaches fixpoint; that fixpoint becomes the **certified Tier-1 claim** and the large-bound runs stay recorded as depth-bounded clean evidence ("certified-small beats forever-inconclusive-large"). **🗄️ TIER-2 RETIRED AS A CLAIM, RETAINED AS INFRASTRUCTURE (authority-ruled 2026-08-22).** The standing-bounds fixpoint is **strictly stronger** than anything the reduced-bound certification was designed to buy, so the "certified at (bounds) / larger bounds depth-clean" language below is **superseded and must no longer be used**. What is kept is the machinery: **`HydraCert.tla` + the `tlc-cert.yml` sweep + the mechanical gate job remain the standing MULTI-BOUND REGRESSION HARNESS.** It earned that in a single day — it surfaced F-UNBOUNDED-SEGMENT (§7.21) and then proved the Mut3 repair across bounds (§7.22). **M1's flip criteria are now the five receipts listed in §4/§6 below, not a certified bound.** The historical text that follows is retained for provenance.

**✅ AUTHORIZED 2026-08-22 (design authority; the rule-5 pause is RELEASED) — *superseded by the retirement note above; kept for provenance*.** The tier-2 certification-bound **search** was authorized under a binding **acceptance criterion**: cert bounds (`MaxCrashes = 1` + a tightened `|msgs|`) are valid **iff (A) all four model mutations (Mut1, Mut2, Mut3, Mut4) FIRE at those bounds, AND (B) both baselines reach conclusive verdicts within the time-box** — `baseline-safety` → **fixpoint** (`Model checking completed. No error`) and `baseline-liveness` → **clean drain**. **Iterate `|msgs|` upward until Mut3 fires** (Mut3 is the binding leg). A bound that drains but at which any mutation goes **silent is a masked failure, not a certification** — the search continues. **The certified claim reads verbatim: "certified at (bounds): …; larger bounds: depth-clean, inconclusive"** — never "the protocol is verified". **M1 flips only on the COMPLETE six-leg receipt set** (quoted `verdict=` lines under `verification/ci-results/`, rule 16); a partial set flips nothing. **The `.tla` stays untouched (rule 13)** — the tightened constraint is carried by a *wrapper module* (`HydraCert.tla`, `EXTENDS HydraActivationCore`, adding only the `MaxMsgs` constant and `CertConstraint`), so the certified claim is about **the same model** and the July banked checkpoints stay valid. **⛔ RESULT (run 32597682154, 2026-08-22) — CRITERION NOT MET; see §7.21 F-UNBOUNDED-SEGMENT.** The sweep ran and produced its receipts. **Mut1, Mut2 and Mut4 fire `verdict=GREEN` (`violated=1`) at EVERY swept bound (|msgs| 8, 10, 12, 14, 16).** But `baseline-safety`, `baseline-live` and `mut3` **do not time out — they crash TLC**: `Error: TLC threw an unexpected exception … The specification contains one or more behaviors with 65536 or more states, but TLC can only handle behaviors of length up to 65535 states.` The cause is a model-level unbounded self-loop (`PrepareCandidate` → `CommitSegmentAndInstall` grows `installedCkpt`/`segCommitted` forever; the state constraint bounds only `|msgs|`). **So no `|msgs|` bound can ever certify — the obstacle is behaviour LENGTH, not state COUNT, and the long-standing "too big for free CI" diagnosis is corrected.** Nothing is certified; **M1 does not flip**; the model and the bounds were **not** touched. **Cert package (landed 2026-08-22):** `verification/HydraCert.tla` (wrapper: one constant `MaxMsgs`, one `CertConstraint`, `ASSUME MaxMsgs <= 20` so a search that needed a *larger* bound fails loudly instead of silently leaving the reduced-bound regime) + `verification/cert/Cert*.cfg.in` (six legs — **identical bounds at a given `|msgs|`, differing only in the mutation switch**, which is what makes a fire provably mutation-induced rather than bound-induced) + `.github/workflows/tlc-cert.yml`. The workflow **sweeps several bounds in one dispatch** (all six legs × all bounds in parallel) and a separate **`gate` job mechanically applies the §6 criterion** to the six quoted `verdict=` lines, emitting `cert-verdict=CERTIFIED msgs=N` or `NOT-CERTIFIED` — deliberately so that **nobody certifies a bound by eyeballing six check-marks** (rule 16 has caught exactly that twice, once at the agent and once at the design authority). The substituted `.cfg` is uploaded with every receipt: the bound defines the claim, so the bound is read off the evidence, never inferred from a filename. **Local pre-validation (within rule 7's permitted set — parse, Mut2, Mut4 only):** SANY parses the wrapper clean; `CertMut2Reset` and `CertMut4AbortFinality` at `MaxMsgs=10, MaxCrashes=1` both fired `Invariant Inv is violated` (419 / 3970 distinct states). The four CI-owned legs (`baseline-safety`, `baseline-liveness`, `Mut1`, `Mut3`) were **not** run locally — the thermal exemption stays spent. Container-2node + DST-marathon CI green weekly.

**🟢 STANDING-BOUNDS BREAKTHROUGH (run 32604092015, 2026-08-22) — `baseline-safety` REACHES FIXPOINT AT THE FULL STANDING BOUNDS.** Quoted (rule 16): `config=baseline-safety expect=clean violated=0 complete=1 verdict=GREEN`, and `config=baseline-safety-fast … verdict=GREEN`, and `config=mut1 … violated=1 … verdict=GREEN`. This is **not** a reduced-bound certification — it is `BaselineSafety.cfg` itself (`Stages={s1,s2}`, `MaxEpoch=1`, `MaxRId=1`, `MaxAttempt=2`, `MaxPos=1`, **`MaxCrashes=2`**, `|msgs| ≤ 20`, `MaxCkpt=3`) running to **fixpoint with no violation**, the M1 DoD item that has been open since the project began and that the record has spent six weeks calling unreachable. **`MaxCkpt` is what unlocked it** — the space was never too large, it was unbounded (§7.21). **`baseline-liveness` at standing bounds is still running at the time of writing — NOT claimed.** **Mut3 is MASKED-FAILURE here too** (§7.22), so **M1 still does not flip**: the DoD needs Mut3 fired-or-contingency-resolved, and that is now an escalation awaiting ratification. **Tier-2 note:** with the standing baselines going green, the reduced-bound certification has largely been **overtaken by events** — the honest claim available is now stronger than the one tier-2 was designed to buy. The authority may wish to re-scope tier-2 accordingly; that is a ratification call, not an agent call.

**🟢 STANDING-BOUNDS `baseline-liveness` GREEN — but on the PRE-repair model, so it is NOT the M1 flip receipt (2026-08-22).** Run **32604092015** (sha **`5a18049`**) landed `config=baseline-live expect=clean violated=0 complete=1 verdict=GREEN` at the **full standing bounds**, alongside `baseline-safety` GREEN. **This is the first standing-bounds liveness drain in the project's history** and it closes, on that model version, the clean drain **F-LIVENESS-FAIR (§7.13) has been waiting for since 2026-07-13**. **Why it is nevertheless NOT the flip receipt:** `5a18049` predates the Mut3 consistency repair (`f8badc3`, §7.22). The repair is **provably inert for this config** — `BaselineLiveness.cfg` sets `AttemptFencing = TRUE`, so `(TRUE => m.a = a)` is exactly the old `m.a = a`, and the transition system is identical; that equivalence was also checked empirically (Mut2/Mut4 state counts identical pre- and post-edit). **But an equivalence argument is corroboration, not a receipt.** Flipping M1 — the project's largest gate — on a run of a **superseded model version**, when the direct post-repair run (**32605432737**, sha `f8badc3`) is already in flight, would substitute reasoning for evidence in exactly the place rule 16 exists to prevent it, and would sit badly with rule 13's principle that a core change voids prior artifacts. **M1 stays unflipped pending the post-repair `baseline-live` receipt.** On GREEN: full ceremony. Not GREEN: escalate. — **✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-23: the post-repair receipt landed GREEN and M1 IS FLIPPED (FULL).** Run **32605432737**, sha **`f8badc3`**, verified unchanged through HEAD: `config=baseline-live expect=clean violated=0 complete=1 verdict=GREEN` **and** `config=baseline-safety … complete=1 verdict=GREEN`, with `mut1`/`mut3`/`mut3-fast` all `violated=1 verdict=GREEN`, and `mut2`/`mut4` `verdict=GREEN` on the same sha (run 32605434178). **The complete five-criteria set exists on one model version.** The pre-repair GREEN stands as corroboration only, exactly as recorded — the flip did not lean on it.

**⚠️ HISTORICAL RESTATEMENT (2026-08-22, one honest annotation — ratified).** July's deep runs (run 29222085672: `baseline-safety` 327.9M distinct at depth 133+; `mut3` 379.7M distinct; later 610.2M continuing) were **bounded exploration of what we now know was an INFINITE space** (§7.21 — the unbounded candidate loop). **The claims made from them stand exactly as written, because they never claimed more than they were:** *bounded-model-checked transition core*, *zero violations found across the explored space*, *mutations 1/2/4/5 confirmed firing*. Those remain true and remain evidence — a violation found in a bounded exploration is real, and none was found. **What is corrected is the DIAGNOSIS attached to them** — "the space is too big to drain on free CI", which implied that more CI time would eventually finish the job. It would not have. That line is corrected wherever it appears, citing §7.21. **No result is withdrawn; no claim is upgraded.**

**🟩 CLAIM CURRENTLY PERMITTED — UPDATED 2026-08-23 ON THE M1 FULL FLIP (this supersedes the 2026-07-13 wording retained below).** Verbatim, and no further:

> *The transition core is **model-checked to fixpoint at standing bounds**; **liveness holds under per-message-class fairness**; **all four mutations fire**; **six implementation mutations 200/200** in the randomized simulator.*

**The bounds are part of the claim and must travel with it:** `Stages = {s1, s2}`, `MaxEpoch = 1`, `MaxRId = 1`, `MaxAttempt = 2`, `MaxPos = 1`, `MaxCrashes = 2`, `|msgs| ≤ 20`, `MaxCkpt = 3`. **This is a BOUNDED model check** — it is not a proof about unbounded executions, and "fixpoint" means the bounded state space was exhausted, not that the protocol is verified in general. **Liveness is under a fairness assumption** (per-message-class weak fairness) — a modelling assumption about the environment, not a theorem about the network. **Three real defects were found by this layering and repaired:** TLC-1/I25 (protocol), F-UNSERVABLE (prose↔code correspondence), F-LIVENESS-FAIR (two model defects incl. a shipping-code fix); plus two verification-layer findings, F-UNBOUNDED-SEGMENT (§7.21, model-bounding omission) and F-MUT3-UNREACHABLE (§7.22, an inconsistently-encoded mutation). **Evidence:** `verification/ci-results/m1-flip-2026-08-23.md`, model sha `f8badc3`.

Local runs find no violation across 1.8M states; **the clean-drain + "Mut1 still fires" confirmation is CI-pending** — do not claim baseline-liveness green until CI drains it clean.

## 7. Findings & decisions log (chronological; the "why is it like this" record)

1. **Architecture (report):** pipeline sharding across LAN; TP only inside a machine/fast island — TP's ~160 collectives/token is infeasible over Wi-Fi/Ethernet latency. prima.cpp (ICLR 2026) is the closest prior art and the placement-algorithm reference.
2. **Protocol hardening v0.1→v0.9:** nine review rounds; every defect lived at a *transition boundary* (start, convergence, completion, state transfer, activation edge). Key machinery each round forced: token ledger + four watermarks; exactly-once via context-scoped `applied_pos`; input-vs-output position typing (I13); execution contexts/shards (I12); idempotent three-case BEGIN_RECOVERY (I11); restorable sampler snapshots + installation (I15/I17); atomic `GENERATION_COMMIT` (I19); PREACTIVE + stage-visible finalization (I20/I21); unservable-supersession closing the 2PC blocking hole (I22); truncating attempt reset (I23); candidate isolation (I24); unified INITIAL/RECOVERY activation with `activation_attempt_id`.
3. **TLC-1 → I25 (v0.10):** aborted activation attempts are permanently dead; restart classifies durable ABORT as attempt-terminal. Found by machine, missed by all nine human rounds — the justification for the layering doctrine.
4. **v0.10.1 package hardening:** six named TLC configs incl. Mut4; I25 made normative everywhere; authoritative `hydra-proto.fbs` + `WAL-FORMAT.md` (+ later `wal-records.fbs` with `INPUT_CHUNK_COMMIT` advancing `prefill_stable_pos`; version encoding `0x0100`); per-message-class fairness; M2 acceptance split into exact-tier vs mixed-backend-tier.
5. **M−1 spike findings (agent):** (a) llama.cpp already exposes boundary *extraction* (`t_layer_inp`/embeddings path) and *injection* (`batch.embd`); only loop-windowing needed patching — 47 lines, **per-architecture** (each model family has its own graph builder). (b) Two upstream bugs isolated: embeddings-context defaults to non-causal attention; `inp_out_ids` dangles when a shard isn't the final layer. (c) **The FP16 finding:** split mechanism is exact; *all* deviation is boundary-payload precision. Residual-stream massive activations (~1560, the attention-sink phenomenon) mean FP16's ulp ≈ 1.0 there → ~0.04 logit max-abs (argmax/top-10 stable). Not a bug; arithmetic.
6. **v0.10.2 ruling (design authority):** payload precision is per-session `{f32|f16|int8_blockq}`; **f16 production default** (within I8 semantic continuity); **f32 mandatory for golden-token/exact test tier** (FP16 boundaries would make that gate flaky); v1 model families pinned to the patched builders (llama + qwen2); spike sweep re-validation required on every submodule bump; M−1 DoD text ratified to the mechanism-at-F32 reading.
7. **Metal close-out:** F32 split-vs-unsplit bit-exact on Metal too (same-backend kernels — proves mechanism, *not* cross-backend equivalence, which remains M2's mixed tier); FP16 boundary cost ~4× lower on Metal (0.003–0.014) — accumulation-order artifact, noted, no action.
8. **Process precedent set:** the FP16 default change paused for ratification (retroactively at first occurrence; explicit rule thereafter).
9. **Upstream issue filed (agent, owner-authored):** the generic layer-window hook request is now [ggml-org/llama.cpp#25577](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/25577), option (a) per owner — an *enabling API primitive* filed standalone (not buried as a comment on the end-user features #22436/#23568, which it cross-references). The two prototyping bugs (non-causal embeddings default; dangling `inp_out_ids`) ride along as supporting evidence, offered as separate issues if maintainers prefer. Standing loop: check for replies at session start, draft responses, owner approves before posting.
10. **F-UNSERVABLE (2026-07-12, found at the sim layer, ratified + repaired):** the M1 item-2 work drove the sim's coordinator durability through the **real `hydra-wal` codec** (virtual disk); a restart cross-check (recovered WAL == coordinator's durable WAL) fired a divergence and pinned it to slice-4 code: `ProceedRecordUnservable` emitted `ACTIVATION_UNSERVABLE` as a `WriteWal` **effect** but never recorded it in `self.wal`, so `unservable_recorded()` was permanently false and §6.5's restart-superseding branch was dead. A coordinator crash in the window after the unservable decision but before the superseding `BEGIN_RECOVERY` misclassified to `ACTIVATION_COMPLETE` (reproduced at the coordinator level: `state after crash+restart = ActivationComplete`), reopening the I22 hole. **The TLA+ model `CoordRestart` was already correct** (evaluates `unservable` first, carries it `UNCHANGED` across `CoordCrash`) — a spec-text↔model correspondence gap, not a model defect. Repair (per ratified sequence, same commit): **spec §6.5 → v0.10.3** (branches now an explicit priority classifier, `ACTIVATION_UNSERVABLE` before `ACTIVATION_COMPLETE`; verification-plan note that the model is authority on order-ambiguous prose); **coordinator** records the unservable fact durably in `self.wal` (atomic, mirroring TLA+ `CoordRecordUnservable`); **TLA+** `CoordRestart` gets a load-bearing-priority comment (no semantic change → smoke only, not a rerun); **regression** `supersede.rs::f_unservable_crash_in_superseding_window_restarts_to_superseding` (restart resumes SUPERSEDING, never emits FINALIZE) + the sim reaches this window ~8×/seed (2499 window-restarts / 300 seeds). **Doctrine note:** a simulation layer caught what TLC could not have flagged (the model was correct — the defect lived only in the prose→code translation), the layering doctrine's "more mechanical layer catches the prose defect" in action.
11. **int8_blockq boundary characterization (2026-07-12, M2-prep, spike `Check E`):** the second half of M−1 item (f), long owed. Measured `int8_blockq` QK=32 (symmetric per-block) boundary payload across the 15-combo sweep (CPU + Metal spot). **F32 stays bit-exact (0.0); int8 peaks at ~1.8 logit max-abs at mid-network splits (k=4–18) — ~40× FP16's ~0.04 — with argmax stable but top-10 overlap dropping to 8/10, below M2(b)'s ≥9/10 bar.** Root cause is the massive-activation outlier (max|x|=1624.6 @ dim 62 dominates its QK=32 block → scale 12.79, crushing sub-6.4 dims to 0). **Backend-invariant** (CPU 1.805 ≈ Metal 1.834), unlike FP16 (Metal ~4× lower) — an outlier/quantization error, not accumulation-order. **Ruling: naïve int8_blockq fails the M2 mixed-backend tolerance → int8 boundaries stay FORBIDDEN for v1** (the v0.10.2 §1.3 constraint is upheld — this measurement is the evidence the gate was right). Future int8 needs outlier-aware requant (carry the massive-activation dims out-of-band) + a re-sweep clearing ≥9/10 at every split. `f16` production default / `f32` exact tier unchanged. Full data in `spike/FINDINGS.md`.
12. **Verification infrastructure gets no presumption of correctness (doctrine, from the `-recover` CI finding, 2026-07-12):** the TLC `long` job's checkpoint-upload had never been exercised end-to-end; on first observation it was found broken (job-timeout cancellation skipped `if: always()`, so slow-config checkpoints were silently lost and the `-recover` chain could never resume). Caught by *testing the link before trusting it*, not during a real incident. **Standing rule:** every untested link in the verification/CI chain (checkpoint round-trips, artifact uploads, resume logic, masked-failure guards) is **observed, not assumed** — it must be demonstrated working at least once before any gate leans on it. The layering doctrine applies to the harness itself, not only the protocol.
13. **F-LIVENESS-FAIR — BaselineLiveness `Progress` violated (found 2026-07-13 by reading the CI log, not trusting the job status; ESCALATED, paused for ratification):** `BaselineLiveness.cfg` reports `Error: Temporal properties were violated` — `Progress` (`RecoveryInProgress ~> (Serviceable ∨ Terminal)`, `.tla:546`) fails via a lasso: `CoordWriteIntent → CoordSendCommit → StageRecvCommitAt×2 → CoordAbortActivation → … → back to CoordAbortActivation`, i.e. the coordinator writes intent, both stages commit, then it **aborts instead of completing — forever**. The model's `Fairness` (`.tla:480`) has `WF_vars(CoordAbortActivation)` **and** `WF_vars(CoordWriteComplete)`; the lasso satisfies both because after each abort `CoordWriteComplete` is only *briefly* enabled (the next abort disables it), so its **weak** fairness never forces it, while abort's WF is met each cycle. **Analysis:** a **fairness-specification incompleteness** — the completion path needs **strong** fairness (`SF_vars(CoordWriteComplete)`, or an anti-livelock constraint on abort) to express "the scheduler eventually lets a fully-committed activation complete." This is the **documented §6 class** ("omitted `StageRecvAbortAt` WF … a fairness artifact that would masquerade as a protocol liveness bug"), **not a protocol defect** — the safety core is unaffected; the sim's I22 liveness watchdog + Mut1 parity independently exercise post-decision progress. **Also a record-integrity failure:** this was misrecorded GREEN (gate (e), §0b, §6, the handoff) by trusting the CI job's `success` (which only means "no step hard-failed") instead of reading the log — the §7.12 "observe, don't assume" rule now extends to CI **result classification**, not just links. **Owner ruling (2026-07-13):** fairness-artifact classification **accepted**; the SF-on-completion fix was **rejected as insufficient** (SF on a never-enabled action is vacuous); corrected diagnosis = **model-completeness (TLC-3 class): attempt exhaustion without participant loss has no defined outcome**. Ratified repair (contingent on trace confirmation): extend `SessionTerminate`'s guard with an attempt-exhaustion disjunct. **Repair attempt outcome (checker-disproven, RE-ESCALATED):** step-2 confirmation held for the first lasso (READY_ALL, attempt=MaxAttempt), but applying the disjunct and re-running BaselineLiveness surfaced **two more stuck families**, the third **contradicting the attempt-exhaustion framing**: (1) READY_ALL, attempt=MaxAttempt [ratified hypothesis — confirmed]; (2) RECONSTRUCTING, attempt=MaxAttempt, rId=MaxRId [broader cState, fixed by dropping the READY_ALL restriction]; (3) **RECONSTRUCTING, attempt=0, rId=MaxRId, post-supersession, a stage stuck in PREACTIVE** [the block is **rId** exhaustion, NOT attempt — an un-resettable stuck stage]. Per the owner's "if the trace contradicts, stop and escalate again," I **stopped and reverted the model to clean** (no unverified half-fix committed). The gap is a **family of model-bound-exhaustion states** (attempt OR rId), broader than a single attempt-exhaustion disjunct. **Owner re-ruling (2026-07-13): TWO defects, both repaired (v0.10.4).** (1) **Bound exhaustion** (families 1–2, TLC-3 model-completeness): `SessionTerminate`'s guard broadened to `cState ∈ {RECOVERY_STARTED, RECONSTRUCTING, READY_ALL, SUPERSEDING} ∧ (∃s LOST ∨ attempt ≥ MaxAttempt ∨ rId ≥ MaxRId)` — WF forces termination only on continuous enablement, so progressing runs (which leave the cState set) are never prematurely terminated. (2) **Model-fidelity gap** (family 3, the mirror of F-UNSERVABLE — spec right, model/impl incomplete): the §1.3-mandated PREACTIVE-revert arm was **missing from both** the TLA+ `StageRecvBeginAt` Case A *and* the Rust stage SM (`stage.rs` Case A accepted only `ActiveFinal|Frozen`); added to both + regression `recovery.rs::preactive_stage_reverts_on_begin_recovery`. **Acceptance:** parse ✅, Mut2/Mut4 fire ✅, workspace 55 tests green + all 5 sim parities 200/200 ✅; **BaselineLiveness: local runs find NO violation across 1.8M states / depth 25 (vs the immediate ~67 s violation pre-fix)** — post-fix it explores the full space (no early counterexample stop), so a clean *drain* is **CI-scale, not the 67 s the early-stop implied**; the authoritative baseline-live-green + "Mut1 still violates PostDecisionLoss" is CI (now hardened to FAIL on any lingering violation, 558af49). No new mutation owed (TLC-3). If a 4th family appears in CI → stop + escalate with full lasso dump. **Severity note (plain):** this was a **triple-layer** correspondence catch — the fix touched **shipping code** (`hydra-state/stage.rs`), not only the TLA+ model; a real stage would have ignored a spec-mandated `BEGIN_RECOVERY` while PREACTIVE and marooned itself post-supersession. **Standing corollary (from the stale-checkpoint reasoning):** TLC checkpoints are **model-version-bound** — any `.tla` change voids all prior checkpoints; the `-recover` chain must restart fresh (do not `recover=true` across a model edit).

14. **Record-integrity recurrence — "run 29222085672 all-green" was a misrecording; M1 full-flip blocked (found 2026-07-13 by the rule-12 log-read; corrected, no escalation to spec/model — the model is untouched).** The pre-C checkpoint commit (`fe30462`) recorded run 29222085672 as "COMPLETED, all five legs green/fired," explicitly "observed from run status." Reading the actual Classify `verdict=` lines (the 558af49-hardened semantic classifier) shows otherwise: **only Mut1 fired (`verdict=GREEN`); baseline-safety, baseline-live, and Mut3 are each `verdict=INCONCLUSIVE`** — the inner `timeout 320m` killed TLC before any of them drained (queues of 380K–758K states still outstanding; `-checkpoint 60` snapshots uploaded for `-recover`). The GitHub run page shows all legs ✓ **because a time-boxed leg exits `rc=0` by design** (so the `-recover` chain can continue) and the matrix job *labels* (`baseline-safety, ..., clean` / `mut3, ..., violation`) are the **expected** outcomes, not results. **This is the exact failure class as F-LIVENESS-FAIR (§7.13): trusting a CI job's green/label instead of reading the log.** The difference: last time the classifier itself was fooled (pre-558af49); this time **the classifier was correct and the transcription into §0b ignored it** — the receipt (`verdict=INCONCLUSIVE ... NOT green`) was printed in every one of the three job consoles. **Rule 12's "a milestone flip gets a human-readable receipt" (the owner's caveat (a)) is what caught this** — vindicating requiring the log-read *before* the flip rather than after. **Consequences:** (1) §4's M1 row stays **PASSED code-side**; the full flip is **BLOCKED** pending baseline-safety→fixpoint, baseline-live→clean drain, and Mut3→fire, none of which run 29222085672 delivered. (2) The two former "caveats" collapse: caveat (b)'s premise ("every leg drained <6h, so `-recover` is moot") is **false** — the legs did not drain; **continuing via `recover=true` is now the required mechanism** and simultaneously exercises the `-recover` round-trip for the first time (turning the untested link into an observed one, satisfying §8's owed infra-test in the same act). (3) No model/spec change — the transition core is untouched; this is a harness/record defect, repaired by reading + recording the truth (§11.5). Receipt: `verification/ci-results/run-29222085672.md`. **Standing reinforcement:** any future "run X is green" line in this file must cite the Classify `verdict=` (or the equivalent semantic log line), never the run-status ✓ or a matrix label.

15. **`-recover` cross-dispatch checkpoint fetch is unwired — the mechanical continuation is non-functional as built (found 2026-07-13 by inspecting the link before dispatching, per §7.12; static code fact, not yet observed live).** `tlc.yml`'s design intent (header comment) is: a `long` leg time-boxes, uploads its checkpoint artifact, and "resumed via `-recover` on the next dispatch (set input `recover=true`)." But the **Restore prior checkpoint** step is `actions/download-artifact@v4` with only `name: tlc-checkpoint-<leg>` — **no `run-id`, no `github-token`** — and `download-artifact@v4` is **scoped to the current run** (a v4 breaking change from v3). So on a fresh `recover=true` dispatch the artifact from the *prior* run (e.g. 29222085672) is invisible; the step no-ops (`continue-on-error: true`), `states/` stays empty, `RECOVER=""`, and TLC **starts from scratch** — another 320m time-box, ~22 CPU-h across four legs for nothing, and the `-recover` round-trip *still* never runs. This is the same doctrine as finding 12 (the 07-12 checkpoint-upload fix): **an untested verification link, inspected before being trusted, found broken.** It means run 29222085672 (a fresh `recover=false` dispatch — confirmed: its Run step shows `if [ "false" = "true" ]`) could never have been *continued*, only *restarted*; the "just re-dispatch recover=true" step in the mechanical protocol (§0e) and my own first correction were **premature** until this is fixed. **Fix — owner-ratified + LANDED 2026-07-13 (§12):** the restore step now passes `run-id: ${{ inputs.run_id || github.run_id }}` + `github-token: ${{ github.token }}`, plus a `run_id` `workflow_dispatch` input and `permissions: actions: read` on the `long` job; a `RECOVER-DECISION:` log line makes the resume-vs-fresh outcome unambiguous (rule 12 — the round-trip observation requires TLC's own "Starting recovery from checkpoint" marker, not a FRESH line). Then `recover=true run_id=29222085672` resumes the banked ~22 CPU-h AND exercises the `-recover` round-trip for the first time (closes the §8 owed infra-test on observation). **Gate strategy (owner-ratified, tiered):** baselines chain via `recover=true`, budget ≤3 more rounds each; if still inconclusive → propose a reduced-bound certification-config (that becomes the certified Tier-1 claim; the large-bound runs recorded as depth-bounded clean evidence — *certified-small beats forever-inconclusive-large*). **Mut3 is NOT chained** — a dedicated `Mut3Fast.cfg` at smaller sound bounds (MaxCrashes=1, MaxPos=1) makes the fencing counterexample shallow, paired with `BaselineSafetyFast.cfg` (identical bounds, must be clean) so a fire is provably mutation-induced (§7.16). **No model/spec change** (`.tla` untouched → run 29222085672's checkpoints stay valid, rule 13).

16. **Mut3 fast lane — a smaller-sound-bounds cfg to surface the fencing counterexample cheaply (owner-ratified verification strategy 2026-07-13, NOT a model change).** Mut3 (`AttemptFencing=FALSE`) never surfaced its designed `ServiceSafety`/`TupleSafety` violation in ~380M states at `MaxCrashes=2` — a deep-in-a-large-space problem, **not** a doubt that the harm is real (the randomized sim catches the identical sabotage at median 187 steps). A mutation check only needs the violation to **exist at some sound bounds**. So `verification/Mut3Fast.cfg` uses deliberately smaller sound bounds (`MaxCrashes=1`, `MaxPos=1`; `MaxAttempt=2` retained so a second attempt exists for a stale attempt-1 message to violate; `|msgs|≤20` unchanged — hardcoded in the `.tla`, not tightened, so no model edit) to make the counterexample shallow. **Paired with `verification/BaselineSafetyFast.cfg`** — byte-identical bounds, `AttemptFencing=TRUE`, must drain **clean** — so the *only* difference between the two is `AttemptFencing`, making any Mut3Fast fire provably **mutation-induced**, not bound-induced (verified by diff: the two fast cfgs differ solely in that constant). Both are new `long`-matrix legs. **Outcomes (owner-ratified):** Mut3Fast **fires** → receipt, Mut3 obligation closed (recorded per rule 16, `verdict=` only). Mut3Fast **drains clean to fixpoint** at these small bounds → *now* the drain-clean contingency ladder applies: escalate with the state-space stats before touching anything (never edit model logic). This is verification strategy, not model adjustment — `HydraActivationCore.tla` is untouched.

17. **THIRD `-recover` link broken — the resume path double-nests; run 29286795638 errored every big leg at 00s (found 2026-07-13 by the rule-16 verdict-read; M1 still BLOCKED, flipped nothing).** With the §7.15 fetch fixed, `recover=true run_id=29222085672` (run 29286795638) was dispatched. Reading the Classify `verdict=` lines (rule 16 — NOT the job status, which showed `completed/success`): **all four big legs `verdict=INCONCLUSIVE`** — and the logs show why: `RECOVER-DECISION: RESUMING from -recover states/ci-results` → `TLC threw an unexpected exception` → `Finished in 00s` (0 progress lines) on **every** big leg. **Root cause:** the Upload step bundles `verification/states` **and** `verification/ci-results/*` into one artifact whose common root is `verification/`, so the artifact holds `states/` and `ci-results/` at its root. The Restore step downloaded it into `verification/states`, **double-nesting** to `verification/states/{states,ci-results}`; then `ls -dt states/*/ | head -1` picked `states/ci-results` → `-recover states/ci-results` → TLC aborts instantly. So the §7.15 fetch delivered the checkpoint but the resume never ran. This is the **third** link in the same chain (after §7.14 misrecording, §7.15 unwired fetch), each found by finally exercising the next — the §7.12 doctrine holding. Rule 16 caught it a third time (job `success` ≠ GREEN). **Also:** the two `-fast` legs (`baseline-safety-fast`=failure, `mut3-fast`=cancelled, no artifacts) — they have no source checkpoint in run 29222085672, so recovering them just failed the download. **Fix (mechanical, landed §12):** Restore `path: verification` (extracts to `verification/states/<ckpt>` where the Run step looks); Restore skipped for `-fast` legs (`!endsWith(matrix.name,'-fast')` → fresh-only). Re-dispatched. **`-recover` round-trip STILL unobserved** (§8 owed item stays open); **M1 full-flip STILL blocked** (flip only on quoted GREEN verdicts — none exist). **Strategy note for the owner (Q2 tier-2):** if this genuinely-resuming round still doesn't drain baseline-safety→fixpoint, the reduced-bound certification-config is the escape. **And `Mut3Fast` is NOT shallow at `|msgs|≤20`** (that bound dominates the space, not `MaxCrashes`; the fast leg ran long, didn't fire) — making it genuinely shallow needs a tighter `|msgs|` via a **model constant** (currently hardcoded `StateConstraint == Cardinality(msgs) <= 20`), which is a certification-strategy change (owner's call) and would void the big-leg `-recover` checkpoints (rule 13) — flagged, not done unilaterally. No model/spec change here.

18. **Dev→regular crate-dep promotion: `hydra-coordinator` is now a regular dependency of `hydra-worker` (classified, 2026-07-19 — proceed+log per rule 5, NOT a package decision).** P1·1b seam B's `hydra-3node-wan` runner bin hosts the coordinator's `BoundaryStore` durability target on the compute path, so it needs `hydra-coordinator` at runtime, not only in tests. The edge is **one-way** — `hydra-coordinator` depends on proto/wal/state/tokenizer and **never** on `hydra-worker` — so there is no cycle (verified). This is a crate-dependency-graph change, which the standing process treats as a proceed+log finding (a package *decision* would be defaults/formats/scope; a dep edge is neither). No spec/model/format change. **Update 2026-07-19:** `hydra-tokenizer` **also** promoted dev→regular (same rationale — the `hydra-3node-kill` runner bin constructs an `Admission` for the commit stream at runtime; hydra-tokenizer deps are hydra-engine-sys only, no cycle). Both are classified crate-dep changes.

19. **Active-sampler-survivor re-activation on a middle-stage kill does not converge — ESCALATED (P1·1b seam C, 2026-07-19; NOT silently worked around).** The ratified seam-C design freezes the *downstream* survivor S_P via `BEGIN_RECOVERY` Case A on a middle-stage (S2) kill. Empirically, freezing then **re-activating the already-active sampler survivor** (the proven replacement-stage order: freeze → `INSTALL_SAMPLER_CHECKPOINT` → activate `Recovery`) **hangs** — the survivor's `COMMIT_ACTIVATION` never converges to `ACTIVATION_COMMITTED`/`FINALIZED` (a >60 s hang vs the S_P-kill case's clean 10 s). This is **distinct** from activating a *fresh* replacement stage (`recovery_start=true`), which the S_P-kill case does cleanly — so it is specific to re-activating a stage that is *already active* with a live sampler + KV. **The middle-stage demonstration is green regardless** because an *upstream* (S2) kill leaves the downstream survivor S_P's committed KV **intact** — nothing to truncate — so S_P legitimately **continues** (the Case-A freeze/truncate machinery exists to re-establish *invalidated* state; S_P's is valid). The three assertions all hold with S_P continuing. **OWNER RULING (2026-07-19): FIX THE WORKER — "downstream survivor continues" is REJECTED as spec-bending.** Ratifying survivor-continues would be adjusting semantics to make a hang pass — the exact move the standing rules forbid. The spec is unambiguous: `BEGIN_RECOVERY` Case A freezes **all** survivors (I10b global barrier), and S_P's freeze is **load-bearing** at a kill placement my demo happened not to hit — **if the middle stage dies while S_P holds provisional sampled-ahead state beyond `generation_durable_pos`, that tail (KV + samples + RNG effects) MUST be discarded (I7a/I7b) and the sampler restored from the committed checkpoint (I15)**, because the replacement S2 rebuilds only to the durable frontier. A continuing S_P keeps a provisional tail the rest of the pipeline can no longer justify. The byte-identical result is real **for that kill placement only** — not general. **Classification (rule 10):** the SM's survivor freeze→install→reactivate convergence is **proven** (M1 sim exercised Case-A-on-survivors extensively; model-checked I18) → the hang is **worker/orchestration-layer**, NOT `hydra-state`. Prime suspects: the activation handshake deadlocking against the down-link `tokio::sync::Mutex`, or the multi-conn serve loop's frame routing during re-activation (the newest concurrency). **Confirm the layer with the trace; if it implicates `hydra-state`, STOP and re-escalate (would contradict M1 evidence).** **Repair + regression bar:** fix the worker, then (a) a **looped ~100×** directed test — active S_P-survivor freeze→reinstall→reactivate converges (the §7.19 hang made deterministic); (b) the **adversarial middle-kill variant** — S2 killed while S_P is INSIDE the sampled-ahead window (the placement that makes survivor-freeze load-bearing) — full three-assertion bar. **Until (b) is green, the middle-kill claim in ALL records carries a "durable-frontier kill placement" qualifier** (added 2026-07-19; removed on (b)'s green). Trace: `tests/three_node_recovery.rs` `three_node_kill_middle_*` in-line FINDING comment. No spec/model change (worker-layer repair).

**RESOLUTION (2026-07-19) — root cause found, fixed; (b) PARTIAL, one sub-item remains.** **Rule-10 trace (control-plane, `tests/survivor_reactivate.rs`):** the hang localized to **`COMMIT_ACTIVATION`** — `Stage::step(RecvCommit)` (`stage.rs:226`) only accepts a commit in **`FrozenReady`**; a Case-A freeze leaves the stage **`Frozen`** (`stage.rs:163`), and `Frozen → FrozenReady` is reached via **catch-up** (`RebuildStep`, `stage.rs:204`). The recovery orchestration **skipped the catch-up** for the survivor and went straight to COMMIT → the SM correctly returned `Vec::new()` (no ack) → the coordinator's `activate()` blocked forever. **This is orchestration, NOT an SM bug** — the SM is correct and M1's sim exercised Case-A-on-survivors **with** catch-up (so it converges there); **no contradiction with M1 evidence** (the owner's escalation trigger did not fire). **Fix:** the survivor's recovery sequence now does **freeze → catch-up (`CATCH_UP_CONTEXT`, applied ≥ goal → immediate `FrozenReady`, re-applies nothing) → reinstall (I17) → reactivate**. Plus a **worker truncation fix** (proceed+log, effect-carrying not an SM-decision change): `on_frame` `BEGIN_RECOVERY` now mirrors the SM's `applied`-truncation into the engine KV (`kv_truncate`) and clears the `sampled_ring` (I7a/I7b) — a killed+replaced stage starts empty so it is a no-op there; load-bearing only for a **survivor**. **Regressions: (a) `survivor_reactivate.rs` — freeze→catch-up→reinstall→reactivate converges 100× (control-plane, CI-safe) ✅; the faithful-survivor-freeze middle-kill `three_node_kill_middle_*` ✅; (b) `three_node_kill_middle_with_sampled_ahead_survivor_truncates_byte_identical` ✅** (S_P holds a provisional **sampled** output q=m in its ring; the freeze clears it; resume re-samples identically, byte-identical — the d1_recovery SampledAhead semantics). **KV-ahead variant — CLOSED (owner-ruled 2026-07-19, TRUNCATE-AND-REPLAY, no new machinery, no spec change).** The stronger placement — a provisional *feedback* advancing the survivor's KV one position past `generation_durable_pos` — is where survivor-freeze is *observably* load-bearing: the provisional apply overwrites `latest_logits`, and a survivor never rebuilds, so `kv_truncate` alone leaves stale logits (resume samples the wrong position). **Ruling (rejected inventing logits-regeneration):** truncate one position **deeper** — roll the survivor's applied state to **goal−1** — then the normal catch-up re-applies position **goal** teacher-forced (boundary supplied by the rebuilt replacement, read from the durable `BoundaryStore`), and that last application **regenerates fresh head logits as a side effect** — exactly how Strategy A/B already guarantee `next_logits_ready`. Within-spec by construction (Strategy-B's premise; I7a's envelope is a floor on what may be discarded, not a ceiling; the truncate-and-replay primitive has been bit-exact since the spike). **Classification: implementation slice, proceed+log** (no spec/model change). **Regression `three_node_kill_middle_with_sampled_ahead_survivor_truncates_byte_identical` GREEN** — the KV-ahead middle-kill passes the full three-assertion byte-identical bar. **The "durable-frontier kill placement" qualifier is REMOVED — the middle-kill claim is now UNCONDITIONAL.** §7.19 fully closed. **Doctrine (the pattern held):** surfaced by the *hardest* kill placement (a control-plane rule-10 probe that made the hang deterministic), **root-caused to the orchestration layer** (a skipped catch-up — the SM and M1 model were correct, the escalation trigger never fired), and **closed within-spec by machinery that already existed** (catch-up for the hang; truncate-and-replay for the logits — no new primitives, no spec/model change). The layering doctrine catching its own gaps, one mechanical layer at a time.

20. **The M-1 layer-window patch had two latent null-weight derefs, invisible until weights were actually sharded (found 2026-08-22 at the engine layer during P2·10b; classified rule-10 IMPLEMENTATION → proceed + log, NO ratification pause).** The M-1 patch windows **compute** on a model whose weights are **all present**, so it could build the final norm + lm_head and merely *choose* not to expose them (`res->t_embd = (l1 < n_layer) ? hydra_boundary : normed`), and `build_inp_embd` could build **both** input paths (token lookup and injected-residual) and select between them at runtime. Both are harmless waste when every tensor is loaded. Under P2·10b's **load** window they are `nullptr` derefs, and both fired as `SIGSEGV` on first contact: **(a)** a boundary stage (`[0,12)`) has no `output_norm`/`output` — it crashed building the tail it would never expose; **(b)** a non-first stage (`[12,24)`) has no `token_embd` — it crashed building the token-lookup input path it can never take. **Repair (both arch-patched graph builders + one arch-agnostic site):** build the tail only when `model.output_norm != nullptr` (leaving `t_logits` null, which `llama_context` already guards — `if (logits.data && t_logits ...)`), and build only the embeddings input path when `tok_embd == nullptr`. **Nothing a boundary stage COMPUTES changed** — `hydra_boundary` is the same raw pre-norm residual either way — which is exactly why the bit-exactness gate still holds at `max_abs=0.000e0`, and why this is an implementation defect rather than a semantic one. **Not protocol-visible** (no spec/model/SM/wire change), so binding point 5's escalation trigger did **not** fire. **Regression:** the M-1 compute-window anchor `split_vs_unsplit_f32_bit_exact_through_crate_api` re-verified green after the change (the defect was latent, so the old path must be unaffected — observed, not assumed). **Doctrine note:** this is the layering pattern again — a patch that was *correct for every case it had ever been run against* was wrong for the case that made the next slice possible, and the failure was loud (SIGSEGV) rather than silent because the weights were genuinely absent rather than zeroed. **Maintenance liability grows:** the engine patch now carries two logically distinct changes (M-1 compute window, P2·10b load window), both tagged in-source (`[HYDRA M-1]` / `[HYDRA P2·10b]`) and both re-validated on every submodule bump (§8).

21. **⛔ F-UNBOUNDED-SEGMENT — the model admits behaviours of UNBOUNDED LENGTH, so `baseline-safety` can never reach fixpoint at ANY `|msgs|` bound. TLC does not time out — it CRASHES. (Found 2026-08-22 by the tier-2 cert sweep, run 32597682154; ESCALATED, PAUSED for ratification; the model and the cert bounds were NOT touched.)** **Quoted trace** (`verification/ci-results/…/baseline-safety-m8.log`, rule 16): `Error: TLC threw an unexpected exception.` … `The exception was a java.lang.RuntimeException : The specification contains one or more behaviors with 65536 or more states, but TLC can only handle behaviors of length up to 65535 states.` — with the dumped state showing `installedCkpt = 72288` and `segCommitted = { 1, 2, 3, …}`. **Mechanism (model-level, exact):** `PrepareCandidate` sets `candidateCkpt' = installedCkpt + 1` and is enabled whenever `cState = "SERVICEABLE" /\ candidateCkpt = 0`; `CommitSegmentAndInstall` then sets `installedCkpt' = candidateCkpt` and `segCommitted' = segCommitted \cup {candidateCkpt}`. **That pair is an unbounded self-loop** — nothing bounds `installedCkpt` or `|segCommitted|`, and `StateConstraint`/`CertConstraint` bound only `Cardinality(msgs)`. So every behaviour can be extended forever and TLC dies on its 65535-state behaviour-length ceiling. **Blast radius across the sweep (23 of 30 legs landed at the time of writing):** every leg that does **not** fire a counterexample early hits the ceiling — `baseline-safety` (m8/m10/m12), `baseline-live` (m8/m10), `mut3` (m8/m10/m12) all `exception=1`; `mut1`/`mut2`/`mut4` are unaffected at **every** bound (m8–m16) because they violate and stop first. **⚠️ THIS CORRECTS THE STANDING DIAGNOSIS.** Since 2026-07-13 the record has explained the baselines' `INCONCLUSIVE` as *"the big state space will never drain on free CI"* — a **capacity** story implying more CI time would eventually help. That is **wrong, or at best incomplete**: at `|msgs| ≤ 8, MaxCrashes = 1` the space is small (2.6 M distinct, explored in 30 min) and it **still** cannot conclude, because the obstacle is behaviour **length**, not state **count**. **No time-box would ever have worked.** **⚠️ AND THE HISTORIC READINGS CANNOT BE TRUSTED TO DISTINGUISH THE TWO:** the `Classify` step in `tlc.yml` mapped a TLC exception to `verdict=INCONCLUSIVE`, exactly like a time-box — so every past `INCONCLUSIVE` on a non-firing leg (including run 32002980633, Aug-17, quoted in §6) **may have been this crash rather than a time-box, and the receipts as written cannot tell them apart.** They must be re-read against the new `TLC-ERROR` verdict before any of them is cited again. **My own harness defect, classified and fixed (rule 12 — verification infra gets no presumption of correctness):** `TLC-ERROR` added to the classifier in **both** `tlc-cert.yml` and `tlc.yml`, with a non-zero `rc`; a crash may never again read as "needs a bigger box". This fix makes the harness **stricter, never weaker**, so it lands proceed+log. **What is NOT done, deliberately (rules 5, 6, 9):** the obvious repair — bounding `installedCkpt`/`segCommitted` via a new model constant or an extended state constraint — **is a model/certification-strategy change and is NOT taken unilaterally.** It changes what any certified claim covers, and rule 6 forbids adjusting the model to make a run pass. **PAUSED for owner/authority ratification** with three candidate paths, none preferred here: **(a)** add a `MaxCkpt` constant and conjoin `installedCkpt <= MaxCkpt` to the cert constraint (a *bounds* change of the same class as the authorized `|msgs|` tightening, but it must be authorized explicitly because it defines the claim); **(b)** treat the unbounded candidate loop as a **model fidelity defect** — the real protocol's segment checkpoints are bounded by a session's segment count, so an unbounded loop may be modelling something the implementation cannot do, which would make this a spec/model repair under the §7.11 sequence rather than a bounds tweak; **(c)** accept that Tier-1 certification of the baselines is unreachable under the current model and re-scope the M1 claim. **Until ratified: the tier-2 acceptance criterion is NOT met, nothing is certified, and M1 does not flip.**

**✅ RULED 2026-08-22 (design authority) — path (a), in the CORE model. RESOLVED IN CODE; RE-SWEEP DISPATCHED.** Classification made precise by the authority and recorded here: **a model-bounding omission of the TLC-3 class, original authorship the design authority's.** The `PrepareCandidate`/`CommitSegmentAndInstall` transitions are **faithful** — the protocol genuinely permits unboundedly many segments — but model checking requires every unbounded-in-reality dimension to carry a bounding constant, exactly as `MaxEpoch`/`MaxRId`/`MaxAttempt`/`MaxPos`/`MaxCrashes` do. Checkpoint count is the same class and was simply missed. **Not a spec defect** (the spec is untouched) **and not a cert-config tweak** (it belongs in the core, so every config inherits it). **Repair, landed:** `MaxCkpt` added to `HydraActivationCore.tla` as a `CONSTANT` with `ASSUME MaxCkpt \in Nat /\ MaxCkpt >= 1`, plus the guard `installedCkpt < MaxCkpt` enabling `PrepareCandidate`; a module changelog entry cites §7.21; the value is justified in-module (2–3 — two committable ids already exercise a candidate prepared against a **non-initial** installed checkpoint plus the drop and crash arms; a third adds a second succession without a new interleaving **shape**). **Values in force: standing configs `MaxCkpt = 3`, certification configs `MaxCkpt = 2`** — both inside the ratified range; the cert legs carry a drain obligation and an independent counter multiplies the space, so they take the smaller sufficient value. **This is not bound-shopping** — the §6 criterion is self-policing: all four mutations must still FIRE at the certified bounds, so a `MaxCkpt` too small to exhibit the harm **disqualifies** the bound instead of passing it. **Rule 13:** the core changed, so **every prior TLC checkpoint is void — moot by construction**, since `-recover` chaining is dead (§8, closed as superseded) and the cert configs are built to drain inside one time-box. **`HydraCert.tla` inherits the constant unchanged** (it still adds only `MaxMsgs` + `CertConstraint`). **Pre-flight verification before spending CI:** SANY clean on core **and** wrapper; the two rule-7-permitted mutations still fire on the core at `MaxCkpt = 3` (`CaseBPure` 834 distinct, `AbortFinality` 18 637 distinct) **and** through the wrapper at `MaxCkpt = 2, |msgs| = 8` (`Inv` violated, 419 / 3 922 distinct) — **the bound does not silence the mutations**; and an audit of every incrementing/growing component confirmed `MaxCkpt` closed the **last** unbounded dimension (`recTarget < MaxEpoch`, `rId < MaxRId`, `attempt < MaxAttempt`, `servedCount < 2`, `crashes < MaxCrashes`, and `complId` transitively bounded because `CoordWriteComplete` is gated on `~completeDurable`, reset only by rounds already bounded). **Anticipated side-benefit to verify in the re-sweep:** the unbounded counter was flooding BFS with semantically-irrelevant states, which is a plausible explanation for Mut3 never firing in 5.4 h of large-bound checking. **If Mut3 now fires at small bounds, that closes two mysteries with one constant and will be recorded here as the resolution.**

**✅ RESOLUTION — the repair works; the flooding hypothesis is CONFIRMED; the hoped-for Mut3 consequence is REFUTED (run 32603140844, receipt `verification/ci-results/run-32603140844.md`, 2026-08-22).** **Zero TLC exceptions across all 30 legs** (was 8 of 23). **Measured effect of the constant:** `baseline-safety` at `|msgs| ≤ 8` went from **2 601 589 distinct then CRASH at 30 min** to **6 098 distinct and FIXPOINT in 2 seconds**; `mut3` at `|msgs| ≤ 16` drained **70 361 distinct in 5 seconds**. The unbounded counter was inflating the reachable space by roughly **400×** with semantically-irrelevant states — **the authority's flooding hypothesis is confirmed.** **But the anticipated consequence did not follow:** with the noise gone, **Mut3 does not fire — it DRAINS CLEAN at every bound 8–16** (`violated=0 complete=1 verdict=MASKED-FAILURE`). That is **strictly stronger information** than the old "never fired in 5.4 h": the bounded space is now *provably free* of the designed counterexample, rather than merely unexplored. **TWO FIRSTS, never previously observed:** **`baseline-safety` reaches FIXPOINT** and **`baseline-liveness` DRAINS CLEAN**, both `complete=1 verdict=GREEN` at every bound 8–16 — the latter is the clean drain the **F-LIVENESS-FAIR (§7.13) repair has been waiting on since 2026-07-13**. Both hold at **reduced** bounds (`MaxCrashes = 1`, `MaxCkpt = 2`) and are stated as exactly that. **NOT CERTIFIED and M1 does NOT flip** — the criterion requires all four mutations to fire at the same bounds. **Ladder engaged (§0(b), first legitimate trigger ever — a genuine drain-clean, not a timeout):** rung **(a)** dispatched at `|msgs|` `[18, 20]` (the `ASSUME MaxMsgs <= 20` ceiling); the standing `tlc.yml` long job dispatched in parallel because the standing configs carry **`MaxCrashes = 2`**, and Mut3's designed harm is a *stale INITIAL attempt* — plausibly needing a crash to strand an attempt **and** a restart to resurrect it, a dimension the cert bounds pin at 1. A local diagnostic (declared **exceptional** per rule 7: `-workers 1`, `nice -n 19`) showed `MaxCrashes = 2` is a far larger space (3.5 M+ distinct and still growing at 5 min vs 70 K drained at `MaxCrashes = 1`); **that run was truncated by the reporting pipeline, so nothing beyond the space-size comparison is claimed from it.** Rung **(b)** (`MaxAttempt = 3`) is held until (a) and the standing run report, so two dimensions do not move at once. **Model logic is never edited to force a fire.**

22. **⛔ F-MUT3-UNREACHABLE — the Mut3 drain-clean contingency ladder is EXHAUSTED at rung (c); the model's Mut3 encoding does not express a harm the implementation demonstrably has. ESCALATED, PAUSED for ratification; model logic NOT touched (2026-08-22).** With F-UNBOUNDED-SEGMENT repaired (§7.21) the Mut3 space finally **drains**, and every drain is **clean** — `violated=0 complete=1 verdict=MASKED-FAILURE`, `0 states left on queue`. This is the §0(b) ladder's designed trigger, and it has now been walked to its end: **rung (a)** (larger `|msgs|`) swept **8, 10, 12, 14, 16, 18, 20** — all clean; **rung (b)** (`MaxAttempt = 3`) at `|msgs|` 12/16/20 — all clean; and **beyond the ladder**, the full **standing** config (`MaxCrashes = 2`, `|msgs| ≤ 20`, `MaxAttempt = 2`, `MaxCkpt = 3`) **exhausted completely** — `14 698 552 states generated, 4 239 954 distinct, 0 states left on queue, Model checking completed. No error has been found.` So `MaxCrashes = 2`, the most plausible "it needs a crash to strand an attempt and a restart to resurrect it" hypothesis, is **refuted by exhaustion**, not by a timeout. **State-space stats, as rung (c) requires (every row a COMPLETE exploration with zero counterexamples):** `|msgs|`≤8 → 6 118 distinct · ≤10 → 14 607 · ≤12 → 29 316 · ≤14 → 48 261 · ≤16 → 70 361 · ≤18 → 104 756 · ≤20 → 167 709 (all `MaxCrashes=1, MaxAttempt=2, MaxCkpt=2`); with `MaxAttempt=3`: ≤12 → 52 190 · ≤16 → 242 941 · ≤20 → 561 046; `Mut3Fast` (standing, `MaxCrashes=1, MaxCkpt=3`) → 202 161; **`Mut3AttemptFence` (standing, `MaxCrashes=2`) → 4 239 954.** **Twelve complete exhaustions, up to 4.24 M distinct states, zero counterexamples.** **The contradiction that makes this a finding rather than a curiosity:** the randomized simulator catches **the identical sabotage** (`mutation_no_attempt_fence`) at **median 187 steps, 200/200** (§6, §12) — so the harm is **real at the implementation layer** and reliably reachable there. A mutation that the implementation-level DST catches instantly and the model cannot reach *at all* after 4.24 M states is not a bounds problem; it is evidence that **the model's `AttemptFencing = FALSE` sabotage does not encode the same defect the code's does**, i.e. the TLA+ fencing guard is not load-bearing for `ServiceSafety`/`TupleSafety` along any path the bounded model admits. **Leading hypothesis, named but NOT acted on:** every config in the package pins **`MaxEpoch = 1` and `MaxRId = 1`**, and those two dimensions have **never been varied**. Mut3's designed harm is a *stale INITIAL attempt* being honoured after fencing is removed — staleness plausibly requires a **supersession (epoch bump)** or a **second reconstruction round** to exist at all, and at `MaxEpoch = MaxRId = 1` neither can happen. If so, the fix is a bounds change (`MaxEpoch = 2` and/or `MaxRId = 2`); if not, the model's mutation itself needs a documented strengthening. **Either way §0(b) rung (c) is explicit that this is "a package change requiring ratification", and rule 6 forbids editing model logic to force a fire — so nothing was changed.** **PAUSED for owner/authority ratification.** **Consequence for M1:** the tier-2 criterion requires all four mutations to fire at the certified bounds; Mut3 does not, at any bound tried, so **nothing is certified and M1 does not flip on the cert route.** **But see §6 — the standing-bounds baselines have meanwhile gone GREEN, which is a materially better result than the reduced-bound certification was ever going to be.**

**✅ RESOLUTION 2026-08-22 (authority-ruled; the diagnosis was neither of the agent's two options and was sharper than both). CLASSIFICATION: the sabotage was INCONSISTENT AS ENCODED.** `AttemptFencing = FALSE` disabled the **stage-side** checks (`StageRecvCommitAt`'s `m.a >= stAttempt[s]`, `StageRecvFinalizeAt`'s `m.a = stAttempt[s]`) but left the **coordinator-side** evidence sets `CommitAcks(a)`/`FinalAcks(a)` hard-coded to `m.a = a`. So a stale-attempt stage never yielded a **countable** ack, `AllCommitted` never held, `CoordWriteComplete` blocked, and **ack-counting alone reconstituted the guarantee the mutation was meant to remove** — the designed violation was unreachable **by construction**, which is why no bound could ever surface it. **TWO results, both kept:** **(1) THE POSITIVE ONE — a defence-in-depth theorem.** The twelve exhaustions are not wasted work: they prove that **at this abstraction, consistent ack discipline SUBSUMES attempt fencing.** Remove the stage-side fence and the coordinator's attempt-filtered ack counting still blocks every unsafe activation, across 4 239 954 distinct states. That is a genuine, machine-checked redundancy result about the protocol's layering, and it is recorded here as such. **(2) THE NEGATIVE ONE — the sabotage never modelled the implementation's defect**, which is *stale acks being counted* (TLC-1's class). That is exactly why the DST caught it at median 187 steps while the model could not reach it at all. **REPAIR (authorized under the mutation framework's own too-abstract clause — strengthening the DETECTOR, not adjusting the model to pass; rule 6 untouched):** gate the filter on the same switch — `(AttemptFencing => m.a = a)` in both `CommitAcks` and `FinalAcks`. `ReadyAcks` is **round-scoped (`r`), not attempt-scoped**, carries no `m.a` filter, and is therefore not part of the fencing surface — checked, not assumed. With fencing `TRUE` the implication is equivalent to the old equality, so **the faithful model is behaviourally unchanged**; rule 13 still voids all checkpoints (moot, as established under `MaxCkpt`). **PREDICTION VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED — `verdict=GREEN`, `violated=1`:** at the **standing** bounds `config=mut3 expect=violation violated=1 complete=0 verdict=GREEN` (49 062 distinct, **fires in 4 s**), `config=mut3-fast … verdict=GREEN`, and at cert bounds `|msgs| ≤ 20, MaxCrashes = 2` `verdict=GREEN`. **The 17-state counterexample walks the predicted path exactly**, ending `StageRecvFinalizeAt` ×2 → `CoordBecomeServiceable` with final state `cState = "SERVICEABLE"`, `attempt = 2`, **`stAttempt = (s1 :> 2 @@ s2 :> 1)`** — a stage left at attempt 1 while the coordinator is at 2, serviceable ⇒ **`TupleSafety`**. **Honest bound note:** at `|msgs| ≤ 8, MaxCrashes = 2` Mut3 still reads `MASKED-FAILURE` — the stale-`COMMIT`-survives-into-attempt-2 path needs message room, so that bound is simply too tight, and it is recorded rather than hidden. **Re-verified unaffected (observed, not assumed):** `mut1`/`mut2`/`mut4` `verdict=GREEN` at every bound run; `baseline-safety` and `baseline-live` `complete=1 verdict=GREEN` at cert bounds with `MaxCrashes = 2`; and the two rule-7-permitted local mutations returned **state counts identical to pre-edit** (Mut2 834, Mut4 18 637 distinct) — the strongest available evidence that the faithful model did not move. **§7.22 CLOSED.**

23. **⚠️ F-TPOT-OBJECTIVE — "TPOT" in the M3 DoD is ambiguous, and the two readings give different optimal placements. FLAGGED for ratification; both implemented, neither silently chosen (P2·3, 2026-08-22).** The M3 DoD says the cluster must "pick a placement within 15 % of **brute-force TPOT**". Building the solver forced the question of what TPOT *is*, and the two defensible answers disagree. **(a) Single-stream latency** — Hydra's actual interactive workload is autoregressive decode, where token *t+1* cannot start until token *t* has crossed every stage, so per-token time is a **sum**: `Σ(layers_i × ms_per_layer_tok_i) + Σ(boundary crossings)`. **(b) Pipelined throughput** — with several tokens in flight (batched serving, chunked prefill) the rate is set by the **single slowest pipeline element**, and a link is itself an element: `max({stage times} ∪ {link costs})`. **Why it matters, concretely:** under (a), "balance the stage times" is **not** optimal — with no other constraint the best placement puts *every* layer on the fastest device and pays no link cost at all. **P1·2's recorded 56/30/14 split is the (b) heuristic**, and it is only the right answer when the model does not fit on one device. **The binding constraint is therefore MEMORY, not balance** — which is precisely what P2·10b's sharded loading turned into a real, measurable per-device quantity. **Both objectives are implemented and selectable (`Objective`); the solver does not pick for the project.** **An uncomfortable result recorded rather than buried:** priced with the *real* recorded WAN links (25 ms Tailscale RTT per boundary) and a 0.5 B model that costs only ~24 ms on the Mac alone, **every split is worse than not splitting, under both objectives** — which matches what the real 3-node WAN run actually measured (~0.81 tok/s across the cluster vs tens of tok/s on the Mac alone, `docs/wan-run.md`). That is a true statement about small models on high-latency links, not a defect in the architecture, and the solver is not written to flatter the architecture. It is also why the M3 DoD's 15 % clause should name its objective. **✅ RULED 2026-08-22 (design authority): objective (a) `SingleStreamLatency` IS the M3 gate objective; (b) is retained for prefill and reserved for v2.** **v1's normative workload decides it:** one session per model instance, **no speculative decoding** — token *t+1* strictly waits for token *t*, so **there is never more than one token in flight within a session**, and single-stream latency simply *is* TPOT for everything v1 serves in decode. The solver optimises (a) subject to memory. **The corollary is embraced as a feature, not an embarrassment:** when the model fits on one device, the correct placement is **no split**. Hydra's value proposition has always been **correctness + running-at-all, not parallel speedup** — and the solver now *proves* that honestly instead of asserting it. **(b) stays implemented** for the two places multiple positions genuinely are in flight: **chunked prefill (P2·7 plans with the max-based objective)** and, reserved, **v2's multi-session / speculative modes**. **(b) is reported as an informational metric alongside every (a)-placement** (`Placement::other_objective_ms`); **a placement need not satisfy both.** **P1·2's deployed-split fixture stays valid — as a (b)-objective fixture, and is now labelled so** (`pipelined_objective_b_reproduces_the_p1_2_deployed_split_exactly`) so it can never be misread as validating the gate objective. **THE 15 % CLAUSE, RESTATED to mean something at this scale (recorded in the M3 gate criteria, citing §7.23):** enumeration is **optimal by construction** and that is already independently asserted in the tests, so comparing the search against itself is vacuous. The residual check is **CALIBRATION: the deployed placement's MEASURED TPOT must be within 15 % of the solver's PREDICTED TPOT on the same measured inputs.** The gate tests **the cost model against reality**, not the search against itself.

24. **⛔ F-COST-MODEL-INCOMPLETE — the solver's TPOT model is missing TWO real per-token terms; the calibration measurement is now sound and says so. ESCALATED, PAUSED for ratification; the model was NOT tuned to pass (2026-08-23, M3 gate row 8).** **The harness fault is fixed first**, exactly as diagnosed: `pair::time_teacher_forced_pipeline` opens both mTLS connections **before the first timer starts** and returns **one duration per token**, so connection setup cannot leak into a per-token number (the earlier 171 ms/token "link" was handshake ÷ 3, and the −50.95 ms/token marginal was two runs each paying their own setup). The measurement now applies P2·1's hygiene: **setup excluded by construction, 8 warm-up tokens discarded, 56 steady samples, median not mean**, on a 64-token prompt so per-token signal dominates. **Result — sound, and outside the bar:** predicted **17.61 ms/tok** (`24 layers × 0.7336 ms/layer-tok`, measured unsplit and independently); measured **32.97 ms/tok** (median, raw spread 18 %); **residual +15.37 ms/tok; error 87.3 % against a 15 % gate.** **DIAGNOSED rather than merely reported** (`crates/hydra-engine-sys/tests/fixed_decode_cost.rs`): the model prices compute as **purely proportional to layer count**, and that is **false**. Timing the same box's windowed contexts: full `[0,24)` = **16.88 ms/tok**; lower `[0,12)` = **10.68** (*63.2 % of full for 50 % of the layers*); upper `[12,24)` = **11.23** (*66.5 % for 50 %*). **`lower + upper − full = +5.02 ms/tok`** — a **fixed per-context decode cost** (graph dispatch, scheduler setup, output marshalling) that a 2-stage pipeline pays **twice** and the unsplit reference pays **once**. **So the two missing terms are:** **(1) a fixed per-STAGE decode cost** (~2.5 ms/stage here), and **(2) a per-CROSSING protocol cost** — the remaining ≈10.35 ms/tok, which the link model's `rtt + bytes/throughput` does not capture because it ignores flatbuffer encode/decode, BLAKE3 framing and mTLS crypto across the **four frames per token** (`APPLY_TOKEN`→S1, `FWD` out, `FWD`→S2, `APPLIED_ACK`), plus the coordinator relay hop. At 3.5 KB on loopback the *transfer* term is genuinely negligible; the *processing* term is not. **Both are legitimate, ratifiable model amendments** — `TPOT = Σ_i (fixed_i + layers_i × ms_per_layer_tok_i) + Σ_crossings (protocol + rtt + bytes/throughput)` — **and neither was applied.** Per the ruling, a missing per-token constant is a ratifiable amendment and a quiet fudge is not; **the calibration assertion is left in place and failing.** **Note the consequence for §7.23's ruling, which is strengthened not weakened:** a fixed per-stage cost makes splitting *more* expensive than objective (a) currently predicts, so the "when it fits on one device, do not split" conclusion holds **a fortiori**. **PAUSED for owner/authority ratification of the two terms.**

25. **⚠️ F-ANCHOR-WITNESS — the §7.24 residual is NOT a third model term; it is a harness artifact I was calibrating against. The production cost model needs no further term. ESCALATED for one judgment call (2026-08-23, M3 gate row 8).** **The amendment was implemented under the provenance rule and the residual persisted:** with `fixed` and `per_layer` from the **windowed-context decomposition** (engine-only — no pipeline, no mTLS, no coordinator, so it cannot be a residual of what it predicts) and `protocol = 0.438 ms/exchange` from the **zero-inference microbench**, the prediction still missed by **+10.50 ms/tok at k=12 and +10.37 at k=8**. **The residual's SHAPE was the clue:** essentially **constant across split points**, which rules out both amended terms — a per-stage cost scales with stage count, a per-layer cost with layers. Only work done **once per token regardless of the split** has that shape. **Found it (`digest_cost.rs`): the final stage's `APPLIED_ACK` witness — `logits_digest` converts the whole `n_vocab` logits vector to little-endian bytes and BLAKE3-hashes it every token. 151 936 floats ≈ 593 KB/token, measured at 9.607 ms/token** (1.387 ms of that in the byte conversion alone). That is **93 % of the residual**, with exactly the right shape. **⚑ THE POINT: that digest is a TEST WITNESS, not production work.** It exists so the rule-14 teacher-forced anchor has something to compare; **production sampling returns a token and computes no such hash.** So the calibration was timing `run_teacher_forced_pipeline` — the **correctness harness** — and charging the cost model for 9.6 ms/token of work the deployed path never does. **The measurement target was wrong, not the model.** **With the witness accounted for as a HARNESS term — added to the prediction of the harness path, never to the production model — calibration lands at 0.4 % (k=12) and 3.3 % (k=8) across two out-of-sample splits**, against a 15 % gate. **The §7.24 amendment's two terms are therefore SUFFICIENT; no third model term is needed**, which is a stronger result than a third term would have been. **What is escalated, and why I did not simply flip:** the authority's delegation says *calibration within 15 % with documented coefficient sources → flip*, and it also says *a third term would need its own physical story*. I have a third **quantity** with a physical story, but it is a **harness artifact rather than a model term** — and whether predicting the anchor path **plus** a harness constant satisfies a gate phrased as *"the deployed placement's measured TPOT"* is a **ratification call, not mine to make**. The strictest reading would instead have the gate measure a **production-shaped path** (sampling, no witness). **Both readings land within 15 %; the model is unchanged either way.** **PAUSED for that one judgment.**

26. **✅ M3 gate row 8 CLOSED on the production-shaped path — and a real per-token cost removed from the deployed decode loop (2026-08-23; executes the §7.25 ruling).** The design authority ruled that the calibration gate measures the **production-shaped** path — real sampling, no digest witness — with the anchor-path result banked as corroboration and the ~9.6 ms/token test-witness cost attributed in harness docs. **Executing that ruling required a shipping-code change, and it is the finding worth recording:** `Worker::retain_and_ack` computed the `APPLIED_ACK` `output_checksum` — a BLAKE3 over the whole `n_vocab` logits vector — **unconditionally**, on every final-stage apply, including autoregressive decode. So there was no production-shaped path to measure: the deployed decode loop was itself paying the harness's witness. **The witness is now emitted only for a teacher-forced (`NO_SAMPLE`) apply**, which is exactly the equivalence-harness case it exists for (the rule-14 anchor, the shard anchor, the chunked-prefill anchor, and rebuild/catch-up replay). On a decode apply the coordinator's token comes from `SAMPLED`, **nothing in the protocol reads the field**, and hashing 593 KB there was 9.607 ms/token of pure cost — **~39 % of the measured 24.7 ms/token TPOT on the dev box.** **Rule-10 classification: IMPLEMENTATION (proceed + log).** It changes what an effect *carries*, never what the SM *decides*; no state, no transition, no invariant moves; the `.fbs` field is untouched and still populated on every path that ever read it. Retention itself is unconditional as I14 requires — only the hash is conditional. **Every rule-14 anchor is green unchanged** (they are teacher-forced by construction), workspace **252/0**. **THE RESULT (receipt `verification/ci-results/m3-calibration-2026-08-23.md`, quoted):** coefficients from the windowed-context decomposition **position-matched to the pipeline's own decode range** `[64,136)` — `fixed = 4.714 ms/stage`, `per_layer = 0.5927 ms/layer-tok` — plus **three separately-measured crossings** (`0.463` A + `0.431` B + `0.154` C = `1.048 ms/token`; crossing C is ~2.9× cheaper than A/B because it carries no boundary, and assuming otherwise would have priced the cheapest crossing as the dearest). **`PRODUCTION SPLIT k=12/24: predicted 24.70 measured 25.41 → ERROR 2.9 %`; `k=8/24: predicted 24.70 measured 24.65 → ERROR 0.2 %`** — two out-of-sample splits, 64-token prompt, setup and prefill excluded by construction, 8 warm-up discarded, 64 steady samples, median. **Worst 2.9 % against a 15 % gate.** **The §7.24 two-term amendment is confirmed sufficient — no third model term, and the model was never tuned.** **Corroboration banked:** the same coefficients predict the *anchor* path (two crossings, witness attributed as a **harness** constant) at **0.8 % / 2.1 %** — one coefficient set predicting two differently-shaped paths. **An unforced cross-check:** measured minutes apart, production **24.65/25.41** vs anchor **32.91/33.34** ms/tok — a ~7.9–8.3 ms gap, which is the 9.607 ms witness net of the crossing and sampler work production adds. The two measurements agree without having been made to. **One honest limitation, recorded not glossed:** teacher-forced applies — production **prefill** and recovery **`REBUILD_APPLY`** — still pay the digest. That is TTFT and recovery-time cost, not TPOT, and it is out of this slice's scope; **owed in §8** as an optimisation, not a correctness item.

27. **⛔⛔ M4·1 AUDIT — ten real gaps found by making the audit a TEST rather than a reading; all fixed in the same slice (2026-08-23).** BLUEPRINT §3's M4 asks for "the reserved-hook audit (every `[RESERVED]` spec field exists in `hydra-proto` and is fenced)" and a security checklist. **Written as prose, both would have passed.** Written as executing assertions, they found ten defects — which is the finding: *a documented invariant that nothing checks is not an invariant, and the difference is invisible to a reading.* Full map: `docs/SECURITY-CHECKLIST.md`. **Rule-10 classification for every item below: IMPLEMENTATION (proceed + log).** Each changes what an effect carries or whether an input is admitted; **none changes what any state machine decides**; no spec, model or invariant moved; the `.fbs` is untouched. And none is a rule-5 package change — BLUEPRINT §1.9 already *fixes* "API auth token + Host/Origin validation, never bind 0.0.0.0 by default", spec §1.4 already fixes Option A, and the caps are already normative in the schema. **This slice implements ratified decisions that had never been implemented; it does not re-decide anything.** ‖ **(A) THREE RESERVED HOOKS WERE PRESENT BUT UNFENCED — worse than absent, because an unvalidated field is an accepted input on a code path nobody wrote.** (1) **`Fence.branch_id`** — schema says *"RESERVED, must be 0 in v1"*; it was **written as 0 and never read**, so a peer could set anything. (2) **`Tensor.block_scales`** — schema says *present iff `dtype == I8_BLOCKQ`*; it was never inspected, i.e. accepted-and-ignored on every boundary frame, leaving a field a future peer could use to smuggle state past a build that does not understand it. (3) **Option B (spec §1.4)** — *"one active session per model instance (Option A); Option B [RESERVED]"* — **was the DEFAULT**: the HTTP surface minted a fresh session on every POST without a matching `Idempotency-Key`, so a second client simply started a second generation against workers whose stage SMs, sampler and commit stream are single-session by construction. Option B was not unimplemented; it was reachable. Now a structured **409** that names the session holding the instance. ‖ **(B) FOUR OF FIVE NORMATIVE WIRE CAPS WERE NEVER CALLED.** Only `MAX_FRAME_BYTES` was enforced; `check_tensor_len`, `check_positions`, `MAX_SNAPSHOT_BYTES` and `MAX_STRING_BYTES` lived in `limits.rs` and were invoked from **nowhere in the tree**. *"The frame cap already bounds it"* is not an answer — it is a different cap on a different quantity: a legal 64 MiB frame could carry a 60 MiB tensor against a declared 48 MiB cap, and `n_positions` is a `uint16`, so a peer could declare **65 535 positions — 64× the cap — in a frame whose bytes look unremarkable**. All four now enforced **before** the copy that FlatBuffers' zero-copy access makes the actual allocation point (`wire_limits.rs`, each test asserting the *error kind*, since naming the quantity and the cap is only possible at the check). **⚑ AND ENFORCING THE CAP IMMEDIATELY EXPOSED A TENTH DEFECT — the audit's own near-miss, recorded because it is the most instructive one here.** `encode_fwd` had been putting `activations.len()` — the **float** count — into `n_positions`, a field the schema declares as `<= MAX_POSITIONS_PER_FRAME`; the line even carried a `// placeholder` comment. It was harmless only while nothing read the field.
