The moment the cap became real, the wrong value became live: the dev model's `n_embd` is **896**, which slips under the 1024 cap, so every test would have stayed green — but **every larger model would have had its boundaries REFUSED** (a 7 B has `n_embd = 4096`). A correct-looking security fix would have shipped and broken the product on the first real model. v1's pipeline is strictly position-at-a-time, so a `FWD` carries exactly one position — the value `encode_boundary_copy` had always used. Fixed, with `wire_limits.rs::a_real_boundary_wider_than_the_position_cap_still_decodes` driving a 4096-float boundary so the dev model's narrowness can never hide it again. **The general lesson, worth keeping: turning a documented-but-unenforced constraint into an enforced one is not a free change — it is a behaviour change against every producer that was quietly violating it, and the small dev model is exactly the wrong oracle for noticing.** ‖ **(C) THE LAN API HAD NO AUTHENTICATION AT ALL, AND NOTHING STOPPED A WILDCARD BIND.** Report Addendum 2 §E1's threat is not a malicious user but **a web page the victim is merely visiting**, executing in a browser that already holds the victim's network position — which is why "it is only on localhost" is not a defence, and why Ollama-class servers have been exploited exactly this way. Now: `ApiAuth` with a **required** bearer token (no `none()`, no `Option` — "auth not configured" is an unreachable state), digest-compared so neither length nor a matching prefix leaks; a `Host` allow-list with **missing `Host` refused rather than exempted**; an `Origin` allow-list that permits *absent* (a normal API client) but refuses *foreign*; and `hydra_transport::check_bind_addr`, which refuses any unspecified address **before the socket exists**, opt-in only via `HYDRA_ALLOW_WILDCARD_BIND=1`. **Deliberately an opt-in, not an opt-out: an opt-out is a flag someone forgets to set; an opt-in is a decision someone had to make.** Exactly one file in the tree opts in (the containerised CI runner, whose netns *is* the isolation boundary and whose port is published only on `127.0.0.1`), and that is asserted **repository-wide**, as is the absence of any raw `TcpStream`/`TcpListener` outside the mTLS module. **API break, stated: `AppState::new` now takes an `ApiAuth`** — an embedder must supply a token; there is no unauthenticated path to fall back to. ‖ **(D) TWO CVE-CLASS DEFECTS IN THE GGUF PARSER, both found by the new fuzzer within a second of first running it.** Report Addendum 2 §D1 names the class (the 2024 llama.cpp heap-overflow family). (1) **Allocation amplification from a declared count** — `Vec::with_capacity(kv_count / tensor_count / array_len)` reserved memory read from the header *before any element was parsed*, so a ~40-byte file requested **64 TiB** and **aborted the process**. Note that an allocation failure is an *abort*, not a panic: it is not even catchable, so this was an unconditional remote/file-triggered kill. Fixed by `Cursor::reserve_for` — a declared count may never reserve more than the remaining input could justify. (2) **Unchecked 64-bit shape arithmetic** — the dims product and the byte-length multiply were unchecked. **The debug panic is how the fuzzer saw it; the release behaviour is the actual vulnerability**: a silent wrap to a small length that then sizes a read against a real buffer. Fixed with checked arithmetic reported as `ShapeOverflow` — deliberately an *error* and not a `saturating_mul`, because a saturated length is still a number and a caller who does not look at it carefully will use it. Also hardened: `offset + len` checked on `u64` **before** either side narrows to `usize`, and a hostile `general.alignment` (0, non-power-of-two, `u32::MAX`) made survivable. **Both are directed regressions now** (`gguf_hardening.rs`), per the standing discipline that a fuzz finding becomes a named test that runs on every push, not a corpus entry a weekly job might notice. ‖ **THE FUZZER'S KIND IS STATED, NOT IMPLIED:** `hydra-fuzz` is a **deterministic structure-aware mutation fuzzer, NOT coverage-guided** (libFuzzer/AFL). It gives up coverage feedback; it gains reproduction from a `(seed, iteration)` pair alone — the same contract `hydra-sim` has run under since M1 — and a **stable-Rust** CI arm this project can actually watch run, which standing rule 12 requires of any verification link a gate leans on. Structure-awareness is what makes a blind mutator worth the CPU here: uniform random bytes fail the 4-byte GGUF magic with probability ≈ 1 − 2⁻³², so a naive fuzzer would spend 24 CPU-hours re-testing one `if`. `cargo-fuzz` over the same entry points is a strictly additive upgrade and is **owed in §8, not pretended away**. ‖ **Fast lane on every push** (`fuzz_smoke.rs`, fixed seeds — a randomly-seeded security test is flaky, and a flaky security test gets muted), **plus** a paired assertion that the *generator* produces varied non-empty inputs, so a green cannot mean "the fuzzer emitted nothing" (the same pairing principle as the D0 zero-traffic control).

28. **📒 M4·1 DEFECT LEDGER — the ten, each with severity and its named regression (design-authority directive 2026-08-23: *a defect fixed without a durable regression + record is a defect that returns*).** §7.27 is the narrative; this is the enumeration. **Severity key: 🔴 SERIOUS** = remotely or file-triggered loss of availability or of a normative safety property · **🟠 MODERATE** = an unenforced normative constraint, exploitable or breaking under inputs v1 intends to serve · **🟡 LATENT** = wrong today but not yet reachable, and certain to become reachable.

| # | Defect | Sev | Regression test |
|---|---|---|---|
| **D1** | **Option B was reachable by DEFAULT** — the HTTP surface minted a new session on every POST without a matching `Idempotency-Key` (full write-up below) | 🔴 | `hydra-coordinator/tests/session_http.rs::a_second_concurrent_session_is_refused_option_b_stays_reserved` |
| **D2** | **A 40-byte GGUF file could ABORT any worker** — a declared count reserved memory before any element was read (full write-up below) | 🔴 | `hydra-modelsvc/tests/gguf_hardening.rs::a_declared_count_far_beyond_the_file_does_not_allocate_for_it` |
| **D3** | **Unchecked 64-bit shape arithmetic** — the dims product and byte-length multiply. The debug panic is how the fuzzer *saw* it; the **release** behaviour is the vulnerability: a silent wrap to a small length that then sizes a read against a real buffer (the 2024 llama.cpp heap-overflow shape). Fixed as a reported `ShapeOverflow` **error**, deliberately not a `saturating_mul` — a saturated length is still a number, and a caller who does not look at it carefully will use it | 🔴 | `gguf_hardening.rs::an_overflowing_declared_shape_is_an_error_not_a_wrap` |
| **D4** | **`offset + byte_len` narrowed to `usize` before the bounds check** — a hostile offset could wrap back into range and read the wrong bytes **with no error at all**, the quietest version of D3 | 🟠 | `gguf_hardening.rs::a_wrapping_tensor_offset_is_refused_rather_than_wrapped_into_range` |
| **D5** | **`general.alignment` is attacker-controlled and was used as both divisor and multiplier** — `0` is a division hazard and a large value overflows `div_ceil(a) * a`. `align_up` now saturates, which the caller's length comparison then rejects | 🟠 | `gguf_hardening.rs::a_hostile_alignment_value_does_not_overflow_the_data_offset` |
| **D6** | **Four of five normative wire caps were never called anywhere in the tree** — `check_tensor_len`, `check_positions`, `MAX_SNAPSHOT_BYTES`, `MAX_STRING_BYTES`. A legal 64 MiB frame could carry a 60 MiB tensor against a declared 48 MiB cap; a "sampler snapshot" could be 64 MiB against a declared 1 MiB one. Now enforced **before** the copy that FlatBuffers' zero-copy access makes the real allocation point | 🟠 | `hydra-worker/tests/wire_limits.rs` — `an_oversized_tensor_is_refused_before_it_is_copied`, `an_oversized_position_count_is_refused`, `an_oversized_sampler_snapshot_is_refused`, `a_fixed_width_digest_field_is_capped_at_its_width` |
| **D7** | **The LAN API had NO authentication and no `Host`/`Origin` validation** — report Addendum 2 §E1's exact scenario, whose attacker is not a user but **a web page the victim is merely visiting**, executing in a browser that already holds their network position. Now `ApiAuth`: bearer token **required by the type**, digest-compared; `Host` allow-list with a **missing** `Host` refused rather than exempted; `Origin` allow-list permitting *absent* but refusing *foreign* | 🔴 | `session_http.rs::{the_api_refuses_an_unauthenticated_request, the_api_refuses_a_foreign_host_header, the_api_refuses_a_cross_site_origin_but_allows_a_plain_client, a_refused_request_starts_no_session_and_no_generation}` |
| **D8** | **Nothing prevented a `0.0.0.0` bind** — the precondition of the D7 attack. `check_bind_addr` refuses any unspecified address **before the socket exists**; opt-in only (an opt-out is a flag someone forgets to set), with **repository-wide** assertions that exactly one file opts in and that no raw TCP exists outside the mTLS module | 🟠 | `hydra-transport/tests/security_checklist.rs::{a_wildcard_bind_is_refused_and_an_explicit_interface_is_not, the_real_listener_refuses_a_wildcard_bind, only_the_container_ci_runner_opts_into_a_wildcard_bind, no_source_file_hardcodes_a_wildcard_listen_address}` |
| **D9** | **Two reserved hooks were present but unfenced** — `Fence.branch_id` (schema: *"must be 0 in v1"*) was **written as 0 and never read**; `Tensor.block_scales` (schema: *present iff `I8_BLOCKQ`*) was **accepted-and-ignored** on every boundary frame. An unvalidated reserved field is worse than an absent one: it is an accepted input on a code path nobody wrote, and a place a future peer can smuggle state past a build that does not understand it | 🟡 | `hydra-worker/tests/reserved_hooks.rs::{a_nonzero_branch_id_is_refused, block_scales_on_a_non_i8_tensor_is_refused_not_ignored, an_i8_blockq_boundary_is_refused_as_reserved, a_non_f32_boundary_is_refused_rather_than_widened}` |
| **D10** | **`encode_fwd` put the FLOAT count in `n_positions`** — invisible at the dev model's `n_embd = 896`, fatal for every larger model once D6's cap became real (full note at §7.29) | 🟠 | `wire_limits.rs::{wire_caps_hold_for_boundary_widths_the_dev_fixture_never_reaches, the_dev_fixture_is_below_the_position_cap_which_is_why_the_sweep_exists}` |

**🔴 D1 — Option B reachable by default (full write-up).** Spec §1.4 is unambiguous: *"one active session per model instance (Option A); Option B [RESERVED]"*, and §12 defers multi-session to v2. The reserved-hook audit's obligation for Option B was therefore to show the path **absent**. It was not absent — it was the **default**. `chat_completions` resolved a session by `Idempotency-Key` and, on a miss, **minted a new one and started a second generation**, unconditionally. **Why this is 🔴 and not merely a scope leak:** every layer beneath the HTTP surface is single-session *by construction* — the stage SMs carry one session's fence tuple, S_P holds one sampler and one SAMPLED ring, and the coordinator has one commit stream whose `generation_durable_pos` is a single watermark. A second concurrent generation does not fail cleanly at an admission check; it drives shared state that has no notion of a second tenant, which is a **correctness** exposure in the exact machinery M1's whole verification effort exists to protect. It also needed no hostility to reach — two ordinary clients, or one client that omitted the header, would do it. **The fix is an admission fence, not a queue:** `Registry::active_session()` finds any session that is not `done`, and a second request is **refused** with a structured `409` that **names the session holding the instance** (P2·4's discipline: a refusal that does not say what is in the way is not actionable). Reconnecting to the running session remains what `Idempotency-Key` is for, and the fence is a *concurrency* fence, not a one-shot — the regression proves all three: refusal while active, the live session still streaming to completion, and a fresh session admitted once it finishes. **What the audit-as-a-test contributed here specifically:** a prose audit asks *"is Option B implemented?"* and correctly answers *no*. The test asks *"can two sessions exist at once?"* — and the answers differ. **Classification: rule-10 IMPLEMENTATION** (an admission decision at the API surface; no state machine's decision changed), and **not** a rule-5 package change, because spec §1.4 had already fixed Option A — this implemented it.

**🔴 D2 — a 40-byte GGUF file could abort any worker (full write-up).** Report Addendum 2 §D1 names model files as untrusted input, and this is the canonical shape of that threat. GGUF is length-prefixed: the header declares `tensor_count` and `kv_count`, and each metadata array declares its own element count, all **before** the bytes that would justify them. The parser passed each straight to `Vec::with_capacity`. The fuzzer's first hit — within a second of the arm first running — was a **70 368 744 177 664-byte (64 TiB)** reservation from a file of a few dozen bytes. **Why this is 🔴 and worse than a panic:** an allocation failure in Rust **aborts the process**. It does not unwind, `catch_unwind` cannot see it, and no supervisor logic at any layer above gets a chance to classify it — the worker simply dies. It is also **unconditional**: no race, no timing, no second interaction. Any path that parses an attacker-influenced GGUF — and the whole point of `hydra-modelsvc` is that a cluster distributes community model files to every device — is a one-shot remote kill. **The fix is not a magic cap** (a cap invites the question "why that number?" and is wrong for the next model): `Cursor::reserve_for(declared, min_bytes_each)` clamps every reservation to `remaining_input / min_bytes_each`, i.e. **a declared count may never reserve more memory than the remaining input could possibly justify**. The count is not *rejected* there — the parse loop still fails on the genuine truncation, with a better error — it is simply not pre-allocated for. That keeps the invariant total and independent of any tuning. **What found it:** not review, and not a reading of the parser — the fuzzer, in under a second, because the generator is structure-aware enough to get past the four-byte magic and put a hostile number where a count belongs. **Classification: rule-10 IMPLEMENTATION.**

29. **📌 DURABLE NOTE — a limit must be tested against values the fixtures do not reach (design-authority directive, 2026-08-23; the M4·1 catch of record).** **The near-miss:** M4·1 turned `n_positions <= MAX_POSITIONS_PER_FRAME` from a documented constraint into an enforced one (§7.27 D6) — and `encode_fwd` had been putting `activations.len()`, the number of **f32s**, into that field, with a `// placeholder` comment sitting right there. **The dev model's `n_embd` is 896. The cap is 1024.** So the entire workspace stayed green while the change would have **refused the boundaries of every model above 0.5 B** — a 7 B has `n_embd = 4096`, a 70 B has `8192`. A correct-looking security fix would have shipped and broken the product on the first real model, and no test in the repository could have said so. **The general statement, which is what makes this durable rather than one bug's postmortem:** *a cap whose only test data sits comfortably below it is not tested — it is merely not triggered, and from a green suite those are indistinguishable.* Fixtures are chosen for cheapness (a 0.5 B model that fits an 8 GB box), and cheapness systematically means **small**, which systematically means **below every limit**. So the fixture's convenience is precisely correlated with its blindness. **Two consequences now standing:** **(1) Enforcing a documented-but-unenforced constraint is never a free change.** It is a behaviour change against every producer that was quietly violating it, and those producers are found by testing at the boundary, not by reading the constraint. Treat "make the cap real" as a compatibility change requiring its own evidence. **(2) Every limit gets a sweep across values the fixtures never reach** — `wire_limits.rs::wire_caps_hold_for_boundary_widths_the_dev_fixture_never_reaches` sweeps 896 / 1024 / 1536 / 2048 / 4096 / 8192, straddling the cap and running out to 70 B-class widths, on both the `FWD` and `BOUNDARY_COPY` paths. Paired with `the_dev_fixture_is_below_the_position_cap_which_is_why_the_sweep_exists`, which asserts the blindness itself, so if the cap or the fixture ever moves, the sweep is told it has lost its reason to exist rather than decaying into a test that proves nothing. **⚑ THE PAIRING IS A REUSABLE PATTERN, NAMED HERE BY THE DESIGN AUTHORITY (2026-08-23): the SELF-INVALIDATING REGRESSION — a test that asserts its own premise, so it fails loudly when its reason to exist disappears.** The failure mode it defeats is the one no green suite can report: a regression whose *justification* silently stops holding, leaving a test that still passes while covering nothing. Ordinary tests assert a behaviour; this pattern additionally asserts **the condition that makes the behaviour worth asserting** — here, "the fixture is below the cap, which is why a wider sweep is needed". **Where else it applies, as a standing prompt for future slices:** any test whose value depends on a fixture property (a model small enough / a bound large enough / a timeout longer than the operation), any control-pairing test (the D0 zero-traffic assertion is only meaningful while the D1 control counts non-zero — and that pairing already exists), and any assertion written *because* some other guard is absent. **The cost is one extra assertion; the return is that the test tells you when it has become theatre.**

30. **⚑ RULED 2026-08-23 (design authority) — the `APPLIED_ACK` digest removal is a DEFECT FIX, not merely a measurement enabler. Reclassified and recorded as such.** §7.26 introduced the change as what the §7.25 calibration ruling *required* — there was no production-shaped path to measure while the deployed decode loop was computing the harness's witness. **The authority's ruling sharpens it:** computing a full-vocabulary BLAKE3 witness on the production decode path was **itself a latent defect**, independent of anything to do with calibration, and removing it is a fix. That reading is correct and the record now carries it. **Severity: 🟠 MODERATE — a real, unconditional, per-token performance defect on the deployed hot path.** The numbers are measured, not estimated (`hydra-worker/tests/digest_cost.rs`): `logits_digest` converted the whole `n_vocab` logits vector to little-endian bytes and BLAKE3-hashed it **on every token**, 151 936 floats ≈ 593 KB, at **9.607 ms/token**. Against the measured production TPOT of **24.70 ms/token** on the dev box that is **≈ 39 % of the decode budget spent on a value nothing in the protocol reads** — the coordinator's token comes from `SAMPLED`, and the ack's `output_checksum` is consumed only by equivalence harnesses. **Why it is a defect and not an inefficiency:** the work is not merely suboptimal, it is *unnecessary* — there is no configuration, no mode, and no consumer for which a decode-path ack's digest is used. **Why it hid for so long:** the field was introduced for the rule-14 bit-exact anchor, where it is genuinely load-bearing, and the anchor is the most-run test in the project. Every test that exercised the ack **wanted** the digest, so nothing in the suite could distinguish "needed here" from "computed everywhere". It took a measurement of a *different* thing — the calibration residual's shape being **constant across split points**, which rules out both a per-stage and a per-layer term — to surface it at all. **That is worth keeping as a method note: a cost that every test wants is invisible to the tests, and only a measurement whose model does not fit will find it.** **The fix** gates the witness on `no_sample`, so it is emitted for teacher-forced applies (the anchor, the shard anchor, the chunked-prefill anchor, rebuild/catch-up replay) and never on an autoregressive decode ack. **Classification: rule-10 IMPLEMENTATION** — what an effect carries, never what an SM decides; `.fbs` untouched; I14 retention unconditional; every rule-14 anchor green unchanged, because they are teacher-forced by construction. **Regression:** the anchors themselves are the guard (they would fail immediately if the witness stopped being emitted where it *is* needed), plus `crates/hydra-worker/tests/calibration.rs::production_shaped_tpot_is_within_15_percent_of_the_solver_prediction`, whose 2.9 % result is only reachable because the decode path no longer pays it. **⚠️ The other half is still owed and is NOT closed by this ruling:** teacher-forced **prefill** and recovery **`REBUILD_APPLY`** still pay 9.607 ms/position. On a 4 k prompt that is ~38 s of pure BLAKE3 in TTFT, and it lands on the recovery path too — so the same defect survives, out of TPOT's scope but inside the user's. Owed in §8 (M4), and it should be closed by making the witness an explicit opt-in rather than inferring it from `no_sample`.

31. **⚑⚑ STANDING LESSON, RATIFIED FROM THE EXTERNAL AUDIT (2026-08-23) — a test whose name promises more than its assertion is WORSE than no test, because it terminates inquiry.** The audit's central thesis, accepted as a body: **the checklist's rows outrun their assertions.** M4·1 was built on *"a documented invariant that nothing checks is not an invariant"* (§7.27) and that was right as far as it went. The audit pushed it one level further, to the place the first lesson cannot see: **a row that claims more than its test proves is the same failure wearing the test's clothes.** **The example is C1, and it is worth stating exactly, because it is the shape and not the instance that matters.** `docs/SECURITY-CHECKLIST.md` read: *"A worker verifies the signature and every tensor hash before any shard weights are read and refuses on any mismatch."* Every clause of that sentence was **true**. A test proved it. And the signature was checked against `manifest.signer_pubkey` — **the key carried inside the artifact being checked** — so any attacker could generate a keypair, sign a manifest describing any shard bytes they liked, and satisfy the sentence completely. **The row was not false; it was under-specified in exactly the dimension that carried the security.** *"Verified"* silently meant *"internally consistent"*, and no reader of the row, the test name, or the passing suite had any prompt to ask *"verified against what?"* **Why this is worse than a missing test, precisely:** a missing test leaves a **visible hole** — the checklist would have said "no assertion", and the next person to look would have looked. An over-promising test **fills the hole with an answer**, and a reasonable reader stops. It converts an open question into a closed one **at the moment it is most valuable to keep open**, and the closure is invisible: everything is green, everything reads correctly, and the gap is legible only to someone who re-derives the claim from scratch — which is what an external auditor is for and what a suite is supposed to make unnecessary. **The operational rule, now standing:** every assertion must name **what would have to be true for its own name to be a lie**, and a checklist row must be **narrowed to what its test proves** whenever the two diverge. **Wave 5 is that sweep, deliberately scheduled as its own wave** rather than folded into the fixes, because tightening a claim is different work from fixing a bug and gets skipped when it rides along. **Related but distinct from §7.29's self-invalidating regression:** §7.29 defeats a test whose *premise* silently stops holding; this one defeats a test whose *conclusion* was always weaker than its name. Both are failures the green suite reports as success — the first over time, the second from birth.

32. **⛔⛔ AUDIT C1 — the shard-manifest signature was verified against the key EMBEDDED IN THE MANIFEST: a self-attestation, not a verification. FIXED (Wave 1a, 2026-08-23).** Banked audit: `verification/audit-2026-08-23.md`. **Severity 🔴 CRITICAL and, unlike most of this ledger, entirely independent of the honest-worker ruling** — the attacker here is not a compromised stage but **anyone who can place a file where a worker reads one**, which is the entire point of a signed-manifest supply chain. **The defect:** `Manifest::verify()` took no arguments and checked the Ed25519 signature against `self.signer_pubkey` — a field inside the signed bytes. The artifact carried its own answer key. `generate_keypair()` is a public function of the same module; an attacker calls it, re-signs a manifest naming whatever shard files and per-tensor BLAKE3s they like, and the manifest verifies **perfectly**. Every downstream check then runs faithfully **against the attacker's numbers**: the per-tensor hashes match (they wrote them), the layer-range map matches (they wrote it), the admission hashes match (they wrote them). **The refuse-on-fail posture was intact and pointed at the wrong oracle.** **Why it survived M4·1's audit-as-a-test:** this is §7.31's shape in its purest form. The checklist row said *"verifies the signature and every tensor hash before any shard weights are read and refuses on any mismatch"* — **all true** — and `shard_anchor.rs` proved a tampered manifest is refused, which it is, because tampering breaks the self-consistency. **What no test asked was "verified against WHOSE key", because the row never raised the question.** **The fix is structural, not a check added at the call site:** (1) `verify()` is **removed from the public API**, not deprecated — while it exists it is the shorter call, and the shorter call is the one that gets written; (2) `verify_against(&trusted_pubkey)` is the only verification, so "verified" cannot be reached without answering *by whom*; (3) `TrustedSigner` and `ShardManifestConfig { path, trusted_signer }` put the anchor **in the same struct as the path**, so `shard_manifest: Some(..)` cannot be written without it — the previous `Option<String>` made the anchor something a caller could simply not think about; (4) the bootstrap wire carries the key **beside** the path and **refuses a path without one** rather than defaulting, because there is no key this code could substitute that would be safe. **Regression: `shard_anchor.rs::a_manifest_signed_by_an_untrusted_key_is_refused`** — and note how it is built, because the construction is the argument: it re-signs the **real fixture manifest** with an attacker key, then **asserts the forgery passes the old self-attestation** (`verify_against(attacker_pub)` succeeds) before showing the cluster's anchor refuses it. The forgery is structurally perfect and internally consistent; **only the trust anchor distinguishes it**, which is precisely why an anchor that comes from the artifact is not an anchor. Paired with a genuine-manifest control so the refusal is provably caused by the signer. **⚑ H13 and H14 landed with it, because all three are the same sentence failing in three places.** **H13 — order:** `verify_shard`'s comment read *"Signature — before anything else is trusted"* while the code called `from_bytes` (a full structural walk of length-prefixed strings and counted arrays, on unauthenticated bytes) and only then checked the signature. **The comment described the intent; the code did neither the order nor the anchor.** `verify_and_parse` now splits the 64-byte trailer, verifies the remainder against the pinned key, and parses nothing until that passes; regression `an_unsigned_but_structurally_hostile_manifest_never_reaches_the_parser` asserts it **by consequence** — a manifest declaring `u32::MAX` shards is refused on the *signature*, not on a parse error, which is only possible if the parser never ran. **H14 — identity:** a signature says *"one of ours"*, never *"the one this session agreed on"*, because the cluster's key legitimately signs every model it publishes. `blake3(manifest)` is now bound to the fence tuple's `manifest_hash` (spec §1.1) — a value already in `WorkerConfig::keys`, so the binding introduced no new source of truth — and because architecture, `n_layer_total` and all three admission hashes live inside the signed canonical bytes, pinning that one hash pins the model's identity with them. Regression `a_genuine_manifest_that_the_fence_tuple_does_not_name_is_refused`. **Also in this seam:** the manifest reader's `Vec::with_capacity` on declared counts — **the §7.28-D2 defect, third instance, in the parser M4·1's fuzz arm never covered** — clamped via `Reader::reserve_for`, string lengths bounds-checked before the `as usize` cast, and a **`manifest` fuzz target added** (`hydra-fuzz`), which the CI classify step now requires to report or the leg is INCONCLUSIVE. **Rule-10 classification: IMPLEMENTATION for the code; the trust-anchor requirement is a BLUEPRINT §1.9 posture already ratified, now actually implemented.** Rule-14 anchors green, including `two_worker_anchor_is_bit_exact_with_shard_loaded_weights` on the real shard fixture.

33. **🧹 THE §7.28-D2 CLASS SWEEP — audit L2 was the FOURTH instance, and the class is now closed mechanically rather than by memory (2026-08-23; standing rule 17, ruled by the design authority from this very repetition).** Enumeration: `docs/UNTRUSTED-INPUT-PARSERS.md`. **The history is the argument for the rule.** `Vec::with_capacity(declared)` was found (i) in the **GGUF** parser by M4·1's new fuzzer, within a second of its first run; (ii) in the **manifest** parser while fixing audit C1 — the parser M4·1's fuzz arm had never covered; (iii) as audit **L2**, in the **bootstrap** blob. **Three discoveries of one class, each found by a different activity, and none of them found by anyone remembering the previous one.** That is the whole case: a class is not closed by fixing instances, it is closed when the check becomes **mechanical**. **⚑ THE SWEEP'S OWN FINDING, AND THE DESIGN AUTHORITY'S RULING THAT IT IS THE MORE VALUABLE HALF: the definition was too narrow, and the wording is what carries the rule.** "Untrusted input" had been read as *the network*. **Three of the four instances are reached from the DISK** — a downloaded model file, a signed manifest, a provisioning blob. **Under the original network-only reading this sweep would have inspected exactly ONE parser (the wire), found it already clamped, and closed the class** — producing a green sweep, a satisfied rule, and three unfixed instances. **The load-bearing wording is therefore: untrusted input is ANYTHING THE PROCESS DID NOT COMPUTE ITSELF.** A rule is only as good as the noun it quantifies over, and "the network" is the intuitive noun precisely because network input *feels* adversarial while a file on one's own disk does not — which is the intuition the 2024 llama.cpp GGUF CVEs exist to correct. **Recorded at the authority's direction so the near-miss is legible: the rule was one word away from being ceremony.** **Results — seven parsers enumerated, all now clamped, six fuzz targets (up from three):** GGUF ✅ · manifest ✅ · **bootstrap — FIXED here (L2)**, plus a `checked_add` in `Reader::bytes` so a hostile length cannot wrap the offset back into range · **WAL record — clean BY CONSTRUCTION** (it borrows and length-checks before every slice, never allocating) **but given a target anyway, because "clean by construction" is a claim and rule 12 grants claims about verification infrastructure no presumption**; its oracle asserts any record handed back is self-consistent with the buffer it came from · frame header ✅ · wire body ✅ · **HTTP — justified, not swept**: it is not a length-prefixed binary parser and nothing attacker-declared drives a reservation; a target is deliberately deferred to **Wave 4**, because the thing worth fuzzing is the auth/admission logic H15/H16/H21/M16 will reshape, and a corpus banked against code about to change is waste dressed as coverage. **⚠️ ONE ENTRY IS DELIBERATELY NOT A ✅, and writing it as one would have been the §7.31 failure in miniature:** `framed::Conn::recv` validates a 12-byte header and then **allocates `payload_len + 32` before the payload arrives** — **12 bytes reserve up to 64 MiB**, a ~5.6-million-fold amplification, held as long as a peer stalls the `read_exact`. That is **not** the D2 class (the cap is real and enforced pre-allocation, which is what `MAX_FRAME_BYTES` is *for*), but it is not "clean" either. **⚑ DISPOSITION RULED 2026-08-23: accepted-with-assumption is right for v1, but it is not a note — it IS audit finding M1, and it now has a binding home in WAVE 3.** The authority's ruling: **a read timeout is what converts "held while a peer stalls" from unbounded to bounded**, and that is cheap enough to land in v1 *even under* the honest-worker assumption, because a stalled read is as easily an accident (a wedged peer, a black-holed route) as an attack. Wave 3's M1 work is therefore **read timeout + per-peer and global connection semaphore**, not merely a recorded caveat. The honest-worker assumption excuses the *malice* case; it does not excuse leaving an unbounded hold in the accident case. **⚑ The enumeration is `crates/hydra-fuzz/Cargo.toml`'s dependency list**, so a parser whose crate is absent from it *is itself the finding* — the mechanism, not the memory. **CI now requires all six targets to report or the leg is INCONCLUSIVE**, so a silently-dropped target cannot read as a pass.

34. **⛔⛔ AUDIT C2 — mTLS authenticated peers but NOTHING bound them to a ROLE, so any certificate holder could send any message family. FIXED (Wave 1b, 2026-08-23).** **Severity 🔴 CRITICAL, and independent of the honest-worker ruling**: the attacker is any holder of a cluster certificate, which includes every legitimately-paired device — a stage could issue `COMMIT_ACTIVATION` (a coordinator's frame), the durability target could send `SAMPLED` (S_P's), and **the F1 fence passed all of it, because F1 checks SESSION identity, not SENDER role.** The cluster CA's signature was being read as an **authorisation** when it is only an **authentication**. **The fix binds at `accept()`, not per-message**, because a per-message check is one that can be forgotten on the next message type added; binding at the handshake makes the role a property of the **connection**, so every frame carries one by construction. **Fail closed, three refusals that are really one** (design-authority direction): no parseable certificate · a name matching **no** configured role · a name matching **more than one**. The third is why the binding **tests configured names against the certificate** rather than **enumerating the certificate's SANs and looking one up** — a first-match lookup would let a peer holding a multi-SAN certificate **choose its own role by ordering**, and an ambiguous authorisation is a granted one. **⚑ The enumeration-avoidance is also a rule-17 consequence worth naming: reading SANs would have introduced an X.509 parser, which is itself a parser of untrusted input and would need its own fuzz target and reservation audit.** Testing borrows rustls's own vetted path and adds no new parser at all. **An empty role table is refused at BIND, not at the first connection** — it denies every peer, which is the *safe* direction, but a worker that starts and then rejects everything is harder to diagnose than one that does not start: **fail closed AND fail loudly.** **The gate is an ALLOW-list of (role, family) pairs, deliberately**: a deny-list would mean a message family added later is silently granted to everyone, whereas an allow-list makes forgetting to state a sender produce a **refusal**. Asserted directly — `the_role_gate_is_an_allow_list_so_an_unstated_family_has_no_sender` walks 14 families v1 gives no sender and proves no role may send them. The gate runs on a `peek_body` **before** decode, because an authorisation check that runs after decoding has already let the unauthorised peer direct work (allocation, tensor copies, engine calls). **⚑⚑ THE FIRST THING THE FAIL-CLOSED GATE REFUSED WAS US, and that is recorded rather than quietly patched:** the rule-14 anchor `direct_worker_to_worker_fwd_is_bit_exact` went **RED** on the first run, because the harness dials S2 with a separate outbound identity (`worker-s1-client`) that the dev fixture never named — so S2 refused it at `accept()`. **That is the property demonstrating itself on the project's own traffic**, and it is why every dialling identity is now listed individually in `dev_role_table()` instead of the table being made permissive: the moment a fixture is widened to "whatever shows up", the binding stops being a binding. **Production does not use that fixture** — a real worker builds its table from its `Bootstrap` (`expected_peers`, append-only on the wire, with `role_table()` refusing an empty list), and the six fail-closed regressions in `hydra-transport/tests/role_binding.rs` drive a **real listener with a real table**, never the fixture, so the fixture's convenience never becomes a claim the tests do not make (§7.31 avoided deliberately). **Rule-10: IMPLEMENTATION.** Workspace **307 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored**, clippy clean, both rule-14 anchors green.

35. **⚑⚑ M12 — §7.31 AT THE DESIGN LAYER, and the pattern most likely to recur when v2 revisits worker trust (ratified 2026-08-23; the authority's phrasing, adopted verbatim as the canonical statement).**
>
> **A mechanism whose name promised authentication while providing misrouting prevention, thereby suppressing the question of where authentication actually lived.**
>
**That sentence is the finding.** §7.31 says an over-promising *test* is worse than no test because it terminates inquiry. This is the same failure one level up: an over-promising **mechanism**. `SessionKeys` — checked on every single frame, named for secrets, four fields deep — read like an authentication boundary, so **nobody asked where authentication actually lived**; and the answer, until audit C2, was **nowhere**. mTLS proved a peer belonged to the cluster and nothing bound it to a role. **The name did not merely mislead: it made the real gap uninteresting to look for**, which is a stronger and more durable harm than a wrong comment. **⚠️ Why it will recur:** v2's worker-trust work will introduce attestation, redundant recomputation or a stage quorum — mechanisms that *sound* like they answer "is this worker honest?" and will, at first, answer something narrower. **The question to ask of each is the one this finding took an external auditor to ask: what exactly does this authenticate, and what is it merely making tidy?** ‖ **The verdict, both ways as directed: the static-secret path is VESTIGIAL as a security control and LOAD-BEARING as a correctness control — and the two must not be conflated.** With C2's role binding in place, the four F1 fields split cleanly. **`cluster_id`: no security work left.** It asserted "same cluster", which mTLS now proves cryptographically and the role table proves *specifically*; a peer that reaches `on_frame` has already passed a CA-signed handshake, and a matching `cluster_id` adds nothing an attacker who got that far could not also copy. **`model_instance_id`: none** — RESERVED, constant in v1, validated-never-branched (§7.27). **`manifest_hash`: yes, and MORE than before — but not as a secret.** H14 made it the manifest identity binding; it is doing more work as a **public identity**. **`session_id`: no for authentication, yes for correctness** — it makes a stale frame from a *previous session* rejectable, which is an **accident-class** property (I4/F1), not an attacker-class one. **⚑ The honest statement, and the reason this was worth asking: `SessionKeys` was NEVER a secret.** It travels in cleartext inside every frame, so anyone who can read one frame can forge the tuple. It was doing **misrouting prevention that read like authentication** — which is §7.31's shape at the *design* level rather than the test level: a mechanism whose name promised more than its construction delivered, and whose presence therefore discouraged asking where the real authentication was. **What is vestigial is the implication, not the mechanism**: F1 keeps its real job (rejecting stale-but-honest traffic), the authentication is now the certificate, and the authorisation is now the role. **⚑ ACTED ON, not merely documented (authority direction): the type is RENAMED `SessionKeys` → `SessionFence`** across 36 files, the `WorkerConfig` field `keys` → `fence`, with a doc block that states in its first line that **it has never held a key** and tabulates what each of the four fields does *now*. A re-document alone would have left the misleading name at 208 call sites — and by §7.31's own logic **the name is the thing that terminates inquiry**, so leaving it would have been half a fix. The rename is mechanical and behaviour-preserving; the doc block ends by naming where the reader should actually go: *authentication is the peer certificate; authorisation is `roles::PeerRole`*.

36. **⚖️ AUDIT H1 + M13 — the two spec SILENCES, amended (spec + TLA+ + code + regression, 2026-08-23). ⚠️ RATIFIED AND LOCALLY SMOKE-VERIFIED ONLY — CI VERIFICATION IS PENDING AND THE AMENDMENT IS NOT VERIFIED UNTIL IT LANDS.** Draft + rationale: `verification/spec-amendments-H1-M13.md`. **These two differ in kind from every other audit finding: the rest fix code that disagreed with the spec; here the spec DOES NOT SAY, and two conforming implementations could choose incompatibly. The defect is the silence, so the repair goes to the document first** (rule 11). ‖ **H1 — the attempt fence was bounded below but not above.** §1.1's F2 said only *"rejects any … below its highest accepted attempt"*, and acceptance **adopts** the value as the new floor. So the floor was **unforgeable-past but forgeable-future**: one activation control message carrying a far-future id (`u32::MAX`) satisfied the rule, was accepted, became the floor, and **permanently fenced every legitimate attempt thereafter** — the session can never activate, never recover, and §6.4's bound-exhaustion path terminates it. **A silent, permanent denial of activation in which the stage behaves exactly as specified**, which is why nothing caught it: there was nothing to catch. **Under the honest-worker ruling this is not an attack — and that is precisely why it still had to be fixed**: it is an **accident** case (a corrupted field surviving the BLAKE3 tag, a buggy peer, a replay from a future epoch), and the assumption excuses malice, never accidents. **Amended to the window `{highest, highest+1}`** — sufficient because §6.4 gives the coordinator exactly one way to advance (`+1`), so a stage at *n* can only legitimately be offered *n* (an idempotent replay §6.6 step 2 *requires* it to re-ack) or *n+1*; necessary because the one-sided rule let a single message consume the entire remaining attempt space. **The regression asserts the property that matters, not the obvious one:** `an_attempt_above_the_window_is_fenced_and_does_not_move_the_floor` — **a rejection that still advanced the floor would reproduce the whole denial while looking like a fix** (frame refused, log says `Fenced`, session dead anyway); asserting only the rejection would itself be a §7.31 over-promise. Paired with a control proving the retry path still works and the lower edge is unchanged. ‖ **M13 — the reset's effect on the attempt space was undefined, and the MODEL had quietly chosen.** §6.4 says a reset increments `recovery_id` and **says nothing** about attempts; `HydraActivationCore.tla` carries `UNCHANGED << attempt, stAttempt >>` on every reset action. **That choice is self-consistent, so TLC had NOTHING to say — the model was not wrong, it was silently opinionated, and its opinion was invisible to anyone reading the spec.** A second implementation could read "a reset is a fresh reconstruction ⇒ attempts restart at 0" — coherent, and **fatal**, because F2 scopes the stage floor to **(session, epoch)** and a reset does **not** change the epoch: every post-reset activation would be fenced by the stage's own floor. **Two conforming implementations, one permanent deadlock.** Amended: the attempt space is per (session, epoch) and **only a new epoch begins a fresh one**; the model gains the **checked** property `ResetPreservesAttemptSpace`, so the choice stops being incidental — it must hold on the model **unchanged** (a first-run failure would mean the model was making the *other* choice and the finding is larger than a silence), and a future edit adopting the other reading now fails instead of passing quietly. ‖ **⚠️ VERIFICATION STATUS, stated exactly (rule 16 + the new rule 18).** **Local, through the project's own runner flags — the ONLY legs rule 7 permits locally:** SANY parses clean; **`Mut2Reset` fires** (`Invariant Inv is violated`, 834 distinct); **`Mut4AbortFinality` fires** (18 507 distinct). **NOT run, and therefore NOT claimed: `baseline-safety`, `baseline-liveness`, `Mut1`, `Mut3`, and `ResetPreservesAttemptSpace` — all CI-owned.** **⚑ The M13 property specifically requires a CLEAN BASELINE DRAIN to be meaningfully evaluated: a leg that stops early on an invariant violation may never reach the temporal-property check**, so a mutation leg's silence about it means nothing either way. **Mut3 must still FIRE** — narrowing a fence must not silence the mutation that exists to catch its absence; if it goes quiet, the amendment weakened the fence rather than tightening it and must be escalated, not explained. **Rule 13: the `.tla` changed, so all prior checkpoints are void** — moot in practice since the weekly schedule runs fresh, but stated because the rule does not bend for convenience.

37. **⛔ AUDIT WAVE 1c — C3 + M4 + H7 + M5, the boundary-tensor/FFI seam (2026-08-23). Rule-10 classification: IMPLEMENTATION, proceed + log — no state machine decides differently; `dims` becoming two-dimensional is a data convention inside an unchanged schema (`dims: [uint32]`, "row-major; product bounded by caps"), not a wire change.** **C3's substance:** a `FWD`/`BOUNDARY_COPY` frame makes **three independent claims** about its shape — `n_positions`, `Tensor.dims`, the byte count — and the codec **discarded the first two at decode** (`Msg::Fwd` had no shape at all), so the byte count *was* the shape: `hydra-engine-sys` derived `n = len / n_embd`, two positions' worth of bytes under `n_positions = 1` were applied as **two positions**, and a byte count that was not a multiple of four was **silently truncated** by `chunks_exact`. The FFI shim was the first and only component that compared a declaration to a length, and it compared the wrong two. **Now:** `wire::check_boundary_tensor` proves *internal* consistency engine-free (`dims == [n_positions, n_embd]`, `n_embd ≥ 1`, `len == n_positions × 4 × n_embd`, caps first) on **both** boundary bodies; `Msg::Fwd`/`BoundaryCopy` carry the declared shape; `worker::Engine::apply_boundary` proves the *external* half (declared `n_embd == engine.n_embd`, `n_positions ≤ n_batch` = **H7**) **before** `hydra_apply`; `Context::apply_boundary` takes `n_positions` **explicitly** and `Context::apply` holds `n ∈ [1, n_batch]`, `pos0 + n ≤ n_ctx`, buffers exactly `n × n_embd`; the shim repeats `n ≤ n_batch`, the position range, and `token < n_vocab`. **M4:** `n_positions == 1` refused otherwise (every encoder in the tree already wrote 1). **M5:** `Session::push_sampled` refuses `token_id ≥ n_vocab` **before the group committer owns it** — no buffer entry, no append, no watermark, no event (`PieceSource::n_vocab` makes the bound structural; the pump ends the generation loudly); the worker refuses before compute. **`i32::try_from`:** `worker::wire_pos` replaces every `as i32` on a wire `i64` (`input_pos`, `first_input_pos`, `truncate_to`), bounded `[0, n_ctx)`. **14 shim guards:** `HYDRA_GUARD_BEGIN/END` — a C++ throw can no longer cross the C ABI (inspection row: nothing can provoke a throw on demand without H6's hostile GGUF). ‖ **⚑ THE ENSHRINING CONTROL, and the blind oracle named (standing instruction).** `wire_limits.rs::an_oversized_position_count_is_refused` used as its *control* a frame with **3 584 bytes declaring `n_positions = 1024`** and asserted it decodes — i.e. it asserted the exact inconsistency C3 forbids was *legal*, and it would have gone **red on the fix**, reading as "the fix broke decoding". **The oracle was "the cap check", asked one question about one of three mutually-constraining claims, with a fixture chosen for convenience; an oracle that checks one claim in isolation cannot express a violation of the claims' *relationship*, so it cannot guard it, so its green meant nothing about it.** Same shape as the checklist rows (§7.31) and M6's hard-coded simulator fsync: **a harness that cannot produce the failure it nominally guards.** Rewritten; the write-up lives in the test's doc-comment so it travels with the code. **The sibling audit found one adjacent instance:** `docs/SECURITY-CHECKLIST.md` named `wire_limits.rs::a_real_boundary_wider_than_the_position_cap_still_decodes` — a test that had been **renamed** (to `wire_caps_hold_for_boundary_widths_the_dev_fixture_never_reaches`) — a row pointing at a test that does not run; corrected. `pre_ffi.rs` names its own blind oracle too: the rule-14 bit-exact anchor only ever sends consistent frames, so it is structurally blind to a peer that lies — its green was never evidence about this seam. ‖ **Rule 17 applied in-wave:** a boundary tensor parsed from the **disk** (`BoundaryStore::read`, D1 replay) is the same parser class as one parsed from the wire, and the sweep of 2026-08-23 had not listed it. Factored into `decode_boundary_record` with the same check, and made the **7th fuzz target (`boundary-record`)**; `fuzz.yml`'s classify step now requires all seven. ‖ **Tests:** `wire_limits.rs` +3 (`more_than_one_position_per_boundary_is_refused_in_v1`, `a_boundary_whose_bytes_disagree_with_its_declared_shape_is_refused`, `…copy…`), **`pre_ffi.rs`** (4, engine-gated: wrong-width self-consistent boundary; positions `n_ctx`/`−1`/`2³²+3`/`i64::MIN`/`i64::MAX` on both ingest paths; `151 936`/`u32::MAX` token ids; the wrapper's `n_batch`/shape/range bounds with controls), `session_http.rs::an_out_of_vocabulary_token_is_refused_before_anything_is_written` (absence-proven, with the last-legal-id control), `fuzz_smoke.rs::the_boundary_record_parser_survives_a_seeded_hostile_corpus`. ‖ **Housekeeping in the same commit:** `7cf1445` had accidentally committed **11 macOS "` 2`" duplicate files** (`HydraActivationCore 2.tla`, nine `* 2.cfg`, `hydra-session-protocol 2.md`, `stage 2.rs`) — byte-identical to the **pre-amendment** originals. Inert (no `mod`, `run-tlc.sh` names its files explicitly) but a stale copy of the un-amended model sitting next to the amended one is exactly the kind of thing a future reader checks by mistake; removed with `git rm` (no history rewrite, rule 4).

38. **⛔ AUDIT WAVE 1d — C4 + M9 + H2 + H3 + H4 + H19; and the finding *about the harness* that runs through all of them (2026-08-23). Rule-10 classification: H2/H3 are the CODE CATCHING UP WITH THE MODEL (the SM decides *less* than before, in exactly the direction the TLA+ actions already required — no spec, model or invariant moved); C4/M9/H19/H4 are IMPLEMENTATION (proceed + log).** ‖ **C4 — the data plane was unfenced.** Epoch, stage generation and serve-eligibility were checked on **control** frames only; `APPLY_TOKEN`/`FWD` went straight to the engine behind nothing but F1's *session* identity. A frame from a **superseded epoch** — an in-flight boundary from before a recovery, a retransmit from a peer that has not learned of the new epoch — was applied to the KV the recovery had just rebuilt. No forgery: C2 binds the sender's role, and this is a legitimate sender's **stale** frame, i.e. the accident case the honest-worker assumption explicitly does not excuse. Fixed in `Worker::data_plane_fence`, with eligibility following the frame **class** exactly as spec §1.1 F1 states it — `SAMPLE` (decode) requires `ACTIVE_FINAL`; `NO_SAMPLE` (teacher-forced / `REBUILD_APPLY`) is the rebuild class and is admitted while `FROZEN`/`REBUILDING`/`FROZEN_READY`; **`PREACTIVE` and `LOST` serve neither** (I20 names `PREACTIVE`). Reply `ERR_FENCED`. ‖ **M9 — no per-position idempotency.** I1 is *exactly-once apply, shard-scoped* and R2 says *"shard-scoped idempotency; `ERR_GAP` on gaps; in-shard recomputation never authorized"*; the worker had neither, so **the R1 retransmit the protocol itself prescribes applied the position a second time** — position p twice in the KV, every later position attending over a history that never existed, silently, because both applies "succeed". A dropped ack on a healthy link was enough. Now: `p == frontier+1` applies; `p == frontier` re-sends the **cached** replies byte-for-byte (never recomputed — R2 forbids it, and a recomputed boundary is not guaranteed identical once the KV has moved); anything else is `ERR_GAP` carrying the frontier for §2.3d's resume rule; the frontier follows a `BEGIN_RECOVERY` truncation. **Limit stated, not hidden:** a duplicate *older* than the last position is refused, because answering it would need recomputation (forbidden) or an unbounded reply cache (the H20 shape). ‖ **H2/H3 — the code was strictly weaker than the model it mirrors, and that is the interesting part.** `StageRecvFinalizeAt` has always required `m.tgt = stEpoch[s]`; the code checked only `state == Preactive && attempt == self.attempt`, so **a legitimate coordinator's stale finalize still landed** (attempt ids recur across epochs, so the attempt check does not save it). `SendBeginRecovery` emits `base |-> recTarget - 1` **by construction**, so the model can never generate a `target ≠ base+1` frame; the code accepted any `(base, target)` pair and **any** `truncate_to`, including negatives — and `truncate_to = -1` discards the entire context through `applied.min(truncate_to)`. **Neither defect is one TLC could ever have reported: the model never produces the frame.** That is a standing lesson about model-checking's reach — *a model that constructs only well-formed messages cannot test a receiver's parsing of ill-formed ones* — and it is why the audit found these and the verification did not. Both bounds now live in `hydra-state` (rule 11: the layer that decides is the layer that checks), with `n_ctx` passed in as the **stage's own fact** rather than a wire field. ‖ **H4 — the quorum's rank had no source.** `ACTIVATION_COMMITTED` carries **no rank on the wire at all**, so the coordinator's ack set was keyed on whatever the driver supplied: one peer could fill a quorum of three. **The model gets one-ack-per-stage free from set semantics — a modelling artifact, not a protocol guarantee** (the auditor's SUSPICIOUS flag was exactly right: no production coordinator driver exists yet, so this was a latent defect the driver would have inherited). Closed **structurally and explicitly**, as the audit asked: `hydra_state::AuthenticatedRank` has a private field and no constructor in `hydra-state`; the only mint is `hydra_transport::roles::BoundPeer::authenticated_rank()`, reading the rank out of the C2 role binding; a coordinator or durability-target peer yields **`None`, not rank 0**. New one-way dep `hydra-transport → hydra-state` (classified; no cycle — `hydra-state` stays pure and never learns how identity works). ‖ **H19 — `CATCH_UP_CONTEXT{goal}` was an unbounded loop**: `goal = i64::MAX − 2` spun the single-threaded worker ~2⁶³ times. Bounded to `[0, n_ctx]` **before** the loop, which also runs only while the stage is in a rebuild state. ‖ **⚑ THE BLIND ORACLE FOR THIS WAVE, NAMED (standing instruction) — and it is one oracle, not six.** *Every driver in the harness was a perfect coordinator.* It sent each position exactly once, in order, in the epoch it started in, to a stage it never bothered to activate. So the harness could not produce a retransmit, a gap, a stale epoch, or an unactivated serve — and **a failure you cannot produce is a failure you cannot guard.** The sharpest case is the **rule-14 bit-exact anchors**: the project's standing regression gate, green throughout, and *structurally incapable* of noticing that `APPLY_TOKEN` was applied twice on a retry, because they never retry. Making C4 real forced the harness to become a coordinator: `pair::activate` now runs the activation transaction in every driver; `d1_recovery`, `d1_two_stage`, `three_node*` gained epoch-aware drivers; **frozen survivors are put through the §7.19 choreography (catch up → reinstall → re-activate) instead of being fed while frozen**; and the **middle stage of the 3-node chain is activated at all** (the coordinator's data path never touches it, so it had simply never been). **Two harnesses were also found freezing only *some* survivors of what spec §6.1 defines as a session-wide epoch transition** — invisible while the data plane ignored epochs. ‖ **Three harnesses were sending `BEGIN_RECOVERY{base = target = 0}`**, a degenerate transition the coordinator cannot produce — the shape H3 forbids, enshrined the way 1c's control enshrined C3's. Corrected to real `0 → 1` transitions. ‖ **Tests +9** (`idempotency.rs` ×5 engine-gated — the retransmit test compares **digests against a clean run**, because the KV is the oracle and the ack is not; `role_binding.rs` ×2; plus the harness corrections above), and one pre-existing test (`panic_vector_concurrent_sample_next_during_an_inflight_fwd`) was found decoding over positions it had never applied.

39. **⛔ AUDIT WAVE 2, seam 1 — H8 + H9, and what the oracles could not see (2026-08-23). Rule-10: IMPLEMENTATION (proceed + log) — no state machine decides differently; the WAL's normative contract (`WAL-FORMAT.md` §3.4: partial-tail discard vs mid-stream corruption) is unchanged, and this is the code finally implementing it.** ‖ **RULE 19 FIRST — the harness, then the fix.** ‖ **H8's oracle gap:** `torn_write.rs` corrupted **payload** bytes and **tag** bytes only. Both leave the frame header intact, so `read_record` still parses the declared length and returns `BadChecksum{total_len}` — which the scanner can step over to ask "is anything valid after this?". **No case ever corrupted a frame HEADER**, and that is the one the scanner could not handle: bad magic or a bad declared length yields `BadFraming`, the scanner had no length to step over, and it therefore concluded *"nothing valid follows"* and reported a torn tail. Adding three header-byte cases made the defect immediate and quantified: **one flipped magic byte discarded 3 of 5 durable records** (`durable_len` 176, records 4–5 checksum-valid and on the platter) — and `WalWriter::open_append` would then **truncate the file to that offset, destroying them permanently**. A silent, total loss of durable data from a single bit. ‖ **H8's fix:** `find_valid_record_after` no longer needs intact framing — **every record starts at an 8-byte-aligned offset** (`FILE_HEADER_LEN = 72`, and `record_size` is always a multiple of 8), so the scan walks aligned offsets probing for the record magic and parses there; a record that parses **and** passes BLAKE3 is durable data by any standard the format has. If one exists after the damage → `CorruptMidStream`, refuse to open. Probes are capped (256) and **exceeding the cap refuses** — fail-closed, because the alternative is to silently discard however much lay past the point we stopped looking. **And the tail is now bounded to one max record** (`tail_is_bounded`): a torn tail is at most one partially-written record, since the writer appends one at a time with an `fdatasync` between, so a longer trailing region is damage rather than a tail. The control that keeps the fix honest is explicit — the *same* corruption in the *last* record is still a discardable tail, because turning every ordinary crash-during-append into an unopenable log would be a worse failure than the one being fixed. ‖ **H9's oracle gap:** the chaos sink returned `Err` and **kept the payload to itself**, as though a failing write left the platter untouched. So the post-failure *state of the log* was inexpressible and every assertion in `chaos_disk.rs` was about watermarks — never about what a later reader would find. A sink that persists bytes on failure (a short write; an `fdatasync` that failed after partial write-back) makes the real question askable. ‖ **H9's answer, before the fix: the next append was accepted**, placing a checksum-valid record immediately behind a partial one — **manufacturing exactly the mid-stream corruption H8 refuses to open.** One transient `ENOSPC` would therefore cost the entire session's ledger rather than the single write that failed. **Fix:** `WalWriter` and `CommitStream` are both **poisoned by the first I/O error** and refuse every later append **without touching the sink**; the first cause is kept (it is the one that explains the file); recovery is `WalScan` + `open_append`, the only path that re-establishes what is durable. `CommitError::Poisoned` is deliberately distinct from a fresh `Wal(Io)`: *"this write failed"* and *"this stream can never be written again"* are different facts, and a caller that cannot tell them apart retries the first forever and mistakes the second for bad luck. Every commit-stream append now goes through one `durable_append`, so a new record type inherits the check by construction rather than by review. ‖ **Tests +6** (`torn_write.rs`: header-magic mid-log, header-length mid-log (both grown and shrunken), last-record header corruption still a tail, trailing region > one max record refused; `chaos_disk.rs`: poisoning after a partial write, and the control proving poisoning is caused by the failure and not by the check — a "poison everything" implementation would pass the first test and destroy the product).

40. **⛔ AUDIT WAVE 2, seams 2–4 — H10, M6, H5, M7 (2026-08-23). Rule-10: H10/H5/M7 are IMPLEMENTATION; M6's coordinator half is THE CODE CONFORMING TO THE MODEL (see the correspondence note below) — no spec, model or invariant moved.** ‖ **H10 — `CommitStream::open`, and the most complete oracle blindness there is: the function did not exist.** A restarting coordinator could only `create` (which refuses an existing file) or build a fresh stream over a new sink, so the restart-and-continue path had **no implementation and therefore no test to fail**. `recovery::read` reconstructs the *token ledger*; nothing reconstructed the *stream's own* state, so a resumed coordinator would have appended with `generation_durable_pos = -1` (every durable position looks un-emitted — the emit-after-commit gate re-opens), `prefill_stable_pos = -1` (the monotonicity check has nothing to compare against, so a chunk moving the input frontier **backwards** is accepted), and `next_commit_id = 1` (**re-using commit ids already on disk**, breaking the `previous_commit_id` chain that makes the ledger self-linking). `open` now restores all four from the durable prefix and performs the **durable discard**: `WalScan` decides where the prefix ends — and since H8 **refuses** damage that is not a tail — then `open_append` truncates and `fdatasync`s *before returning*, so a second crash cannot resurrect the discarded bytes. Three tests, including one asserting the on-disk length actually shrank (a bookkeeping-only discard would leave the tail to be re-read after the next crash) and one asserting a damaged ledger refuses to reopen at all. ‖ **M6 — two oracles agreeing with each other and with neither the spec nor a disk.** The sim called `vwal.fsync()` the instant an `ACTIVATION_UNSERVABLE`/`SESSION_TERMINATE` record was written, *"to keep the virtual disk's durable set == self.wal"* — it was keeping itself consistent with the coordinator's own mistake, which was to push the record into its durable set **in the same step that emitted the write**. Between them, **no schedule in 10M+ steps could place a crash between the write and its durability** — blind by construction to the exact window the record exists to survive (§6.5's restart classification reads it; F-UNSERVABLE is the finding that it must be durable to be read). Spec §6.7 step 1 says *"C **fsyncs** ACTIVATION_UNSERVABLE"* and §1.2 lists it under WAL-before-wire. Both halves fixed: the sim's fsync is gone and those records take the ordinary `pending_wal → WalDurable` path; the SM gains `UnservablePending` and applies the record only on `WalDurable(Unservable)`. **⚑ TLA+ correspondence, and why the model does NOT change (BLUEPRINT §4.3): the model's `Wal(r) == wal' = wal ∪ {r}` makes every write atomically durable — its `wal` *is* the durable set.** A crash "in the write→fdatasync window" is therefore indistinguishable in the model from a crash *before* the action: the record is simply absent, which is what a real disk gives too. The defect was the **code violating that abstraction**; `UnservablePending` maps onto the model's pre-action state exactly as `IntentPending`/`CompletePending` already do. **Model semantics did not move ⇒ per rule 11 no full TLC rerun is owed** — this is the same shape as H2/H3. And the two supersede tests that asserted the atomic transition were themselves the enshrining shape (they asserted the window does not exist); rewritten, plus a **new** test for the window that was previously unreachable: a crash before the record is durable must leave **no** unservable fact, and the coordinator must not restart into SUPERSEDING on a write that may never have reached the platter. `WalKindTag::Terminate` is written down but **unreached** — nothing emits `SESSION_TERMINATE` yet, and that is stated in the code rather than counted as covered. ‖ **H5 + M7 — four defects in three lines, and a driver that could not tell two functions apart.** The durability tests all fed one boundary per position, in order, every write succeeding — and under that driver **a `max()` frontier and a contiguous frontier return the same number for every input**, so nothing could distinguish them. The difference is the finding: the frontier is a `DURABILITY_ACK`, R3′ releases the upstream retain buffer up to it, so **acking 5 when 4 never landed frees the boundary a D1 rebuild replays**, permanently — *acking durability over holes*. Fixed: (1) the frontier advances **by exactly one contiguous position**, never `max()`; (2) the durability-target serve loops no longer write `append_boundary(..).unwrap_or(-1)` **and carry on** — a target that cannot persist stops, loudly (with `max()` that swallow acked straight over the failure); (3) a retransmitted position is **idempotent** — acked from the frontier with nothing written, instead of a second copy; (4) every record is **fenced to (cluster, session, epoch)** — a boundary from another session or a **superseded epoch** is refused before it is stored, the C4 accident on the durability plane. An append error **poisons** the store (the H9 shape): after a failed write the file's tail, and therefore the meaning of the frontier, is unknown. `read` enforces contiguity too, because the file is what a *different process* recovers from. ‖ **Tests +11 across the three seams** (`commit_stream.rs` ×3, `supersede.rs` ×1 new + 2 rewritten, `boundary_durability_h5.rs` ×4, `chaos_disk.rs`/`torn_write.rs` counted in §7.39). **Verification: 339 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored across 82 suites, exit 0; clippy clean.**

41. **⚑⚑ THE THREE DEGREES OF ORACLE FAILURE — the refinement of standing rule 19 (design authority, 2026-08-23, ratified from Wave 2's §2).** Rule 19 says an oracle must be able to produce the failure it guards. Failures to do so come in **three kinds, in increasing harm**, and naming them is what makes the third one huntable:

    1. **SILENT** — *the oracle cannot produce the failure.* It is quiet about a defect it never generates. Cost: the defect ships undetected. Instances: the torn-write contract never corrupting a frame **header** (H8); the fuzz generator capping nesting at depth 2 (H12) — **the fuzzer structurally excluded the bug it existed to find**; the chaos sink discarding bytes on failure (H9).
    2. **ASSERTING** — *the oracle encodes the defect as intended behaviour.* Worse, because **the fix turns the test red, and a red test reads as "the fix broke something"** — the oracle actively defends the defect during review. Instances: `an_oversized_position_count_is_refused`'s enshrining control (C3); the two `supersede.rs` tests asserting the UNSERVABLE transition is atomic (M6); the sim's hard-coded `fsync`, which carried a comment explaining why it was right.
    3. **INDISTINGUISHING** — *a driver under which the correct and the incorrect implementations are **extensionally equal***. **The worst kind, and the one to hunt deliberately:** running it more never helps, adding seeds never helps, and **coverage metrics look fine** — the line is executed, the assertion passes, and the test is measuring a function it cannot separate from its replacement. Instance: every durability driver fed one boundary per position in order, and under that input `max(frontier, pos)` and `frontier + 1` **return the same number for every call** (H5/M7).

    **⚑ A FOURTH SHAPE OF THE THIRD DEGREE, added 2026-08-23 (§7.47): A NORMALISATION STEP UPSTREAM OF A CHECK ERASES THE EVIDENCE THE CHECK EXISTS TO FIND.** `recovery::read` **sorted** the recovered positions and *then* looked for problems — so an out-of-order ledger, which is itself the symptom of a damaged log, was silently repaired into a well-ordered one before anything examined it. The check and the absent check return the same answer for every input, because the input can no longer be malformed by the time it is examined. **The generalisation, and it is worth a targeted sweep of its own (§8): anywhere the code SORTS, DEDUPES, CLAMPS, or COERCES before validating is a candidate** — each of those verbs destroys a distinction, and if a validator runs downstream of one, ask what it can still see.

    **⚑ A FIFTH SHAPE, and it has NO GREP SIGNATURE (added 2026-08-24, §7.49): A TEST THAT PASSES FOR A DIFFERENT REASON THAN IT CLAIMS.** H15 raised the API-token minimum, which invalidated the bearer literal three Host/Origin tests had hard-coded. Those tests **kept passing** — they still refused their requests, but now on **auth** rather than on **host**, so three checks silently stopped being tested while the suite stayed green. Unlike the other four shapes there is nothing to grep for: the test is well-named, its assertion is true, and its subject has moved. **The only detector is the one that found it: a change that SHOULD have moved a test and didn't is evidence, not relief.**

    **⚑ THE TWO HUNTING HEURISTICS, both binding. They are the only two ways an indistinguishing oracle announces itself, and BOTH LOOK LIKE GOOD NEWS.**
    1. **Definitional (Wave 2):** *a fix changes a function's DEFINITION but no test changes colour* → ask **which driver made the two versions equal**.
    2. **Preconditional (Wave 4):** *a fix TIGHTENS A PRECONDITION and no test turns red* → **find out why before assuming the suite is robust**. Something that used to satisfy the old precondition either no longer reaches the check, or is being refused earlier by something else — and in both cases a test has quietly changed subject.

    A green suite across a tightening is an unanswered question, not a reassurance. A green suite across a semantic change is not reassurance — it is an unanswered question about the drivers. (This is rule 12's doctrine pointed at *equivalence* rather than at correctness: the harness is infrastructure, and "the tests still pass" is a claim about the harness before it is a claim about the code.)

42. **⛔⛔ THE AUDITOR'S ORIGINAL REPORT IS NOW IN THE REPO — and reading it corrected the record in three ways (2026-08-23).** Banked verbatim at **`verification/audit-2026-08-23-AUDITOR-REPORT.md`** (transcribed from the owner's `Hydra-Penetration-Audit.docx`; auditor's scope: commit `a025d9b`, llama.cpp `13f2b28b` + layer-window patch, read-only). Since Wave 1 this project has carried the note that `audit-2026-08-23.md` is *the design authority's directive, not the auditor's report*, with **H11/H12 named only by ID and most mediums and all 12 lows named only by count**. **That gap is now closed: every one of the 57 findings has its severity, confirmation status, file:line references, exploit and proposed fix.** ‖ **(a) The wave grouping matches exactly** — the auditor's own closing recommendations (C1+H14+H13 · C2 · C3+M4+H7+M5 · C4+M9 · H8+H9+H10+M6+M7 · H6+H12+H11 · H17+H18+M1+M2+M3 · H15+H16+H21+M16 · M20) are the waves this project has been executing. The directive transcribed them faithfully. ‖ **(b) The directive's per-finding GLOSSES differ from the auditor's titles, and the difference is not cosmetic.** Wave 1c is "C3+M4+H7+M5" in both documents, but the directive glossed them as *shape cross-check / `n_positions == 1` / reject `n > n_batch` / `token_id < n_vocab`*, whereas the auditor's four are *position count from payload length (C3) / silent `i64→i32` narrowing (M4) / out-of-vocab token id crossing `extern "C"` (H7) / no exception barrier at the 14 shim entry points (M5)*. **All four were in fact implemented** — the glosses shuffled the labels, not the work — but the lesson stands: *a summary that renames findings makes "H7 is fixed" a claim about the summary rather than about the audit.* ‖ **(c) AND ONE GENUINE GAP IN MY OWN WAVE 2, found by reading the primary source: M7 is not what I implemented.** The directive said only *"M7 (contiguity)"*; I implemented contiguity on the **boundary store**. The auditor's M7 is **`GENERATION_COMMIT` contiguity**: *"a failed commit drops the batch and creates a gap that recovery mis-aligns; verify's monotonicity check runs after the sort"*, with the fix *"reject `first != durable+1` and non-dense entries on write; reject gaps on read; check monotonicity before sorting; **retain or fail (I9) on commit error**"*. The boundary-store work was real (it is H5's dedupe/fence half) but it was **aimed at the wrong record type**, and `Session::commit_group` still does `self.group.take()` **before** the durable append — so **a failed commit silently discards tokens S_P already sampled**, leaving a permanent hole in the ledger. **Recorded as an incomplete Wave 2 item rather than quietly folded into Wave 3** (§8), because "M7 fixed" was a claim I could not have checked against a document I had not read.

43. **⛔ AUDIT WAVE 3 — H6: the worker never ran the hardened GGUF parser, and the vendored one ABORTS. Rule-10: IMPLEMENTATION (proceed + log).** ‖ **The finding, in the auditor's own words:** *"The Rust `Gguf::parse` — and the entire 24-CPU-hour fuzz budget — protects the offline splitter only. The parser on the worker's load path is the vendored C++ one, which is not fuzzed by this project."* Two `open()`s of the same **name**: `std::fs::read(path)` to hash, then `llama_model_load_from_file(path)` to map. ‖ **⚑ THE ORACLE FAILED IN TWO OF THE THREE DEGREES (§7.41).** **SILENT** — nothing in this project had ever driven the parser the worker loads through, so 24 CPU-hours of green said nothing about it; the new `vendored-gguf` target **found a `SIGABRT` inside `gguf_init_from_file_ptr` on its very first sweep** (seed 1, iteration 350: GGUF v2 declaring `n_kv = 4 294 967 295` in a 371-byte file). An **abort is uncatchable** — the shim's `catch (...)` never sees it, which is why the M5 exception barriers do not help here. **INDISTINGUISHING** — the hash and the load are two opens of the same name, and every test fed them a file nobody was modifying, under which *"hash the bytes you load"* and *"hash some bytes, then load whatever that name resolves to now"* return the same answer for every input the harness could produce. ‖ **The fix, in three parts.** (1) **Verify over a descriptor:** `O_NOFOLLOW` (a symlinked shard path is refused outright — a symlink is a name that can point elsewhere a microsecond later), `fstat` regular-file (a FIFO would let `llama.cpp` read attacker-streamed bytes forever, and "the bytes I hashed" would mean nothing), hash **through the fd we hold**, and the fd is **kept for the lifetime of the `VerifiedShard`** so the hashed inode cannot be recycled. (2) **`(dev, ino, size, mtime)` compared across both opens — the audit's stated minimum — at BOTH ends of the load**, before and after; a mismatch after means the mapping may be of unverified bytes, so the model is **dropped rather than used**. Loading from the held descriptor would settle it outright, but `llama_model_load_from_file` takes a path and an fd-taking entry point means another patch to the vendored submodule, which **L1 already names as a release-integrity problem** — compounding it is the wrong trade today, and that reasoning is recorded rather than left implicit. (3) **⚑ THE WORKER NOW RUNS THE HARDENED PARSER** — the finding's literal title. `Gguf::parse_metadata` is new because the existing `parse` ends in `bytes[data_start..].to_vec()`: fine for the splitter, impossible on a worker where a shard is gigabytes — **the hardened reader was shaped for the wrong caller, which is a large part of why the worker never ran it.** The new entry point parses header + KV + tensor-info (**the region every GGUF parsing defect lives in**) from a bounded 64 MiB prefix, copies nothing, and validates each tensor's extent against the **real file length**, which a prefix alone cannot establish. ‖ **What that composition buys, stated precisely: it does not make the vendored parser safe.** It means the only files it is ever handed are files a fuzzed, clamped parser already accepted. The `vendored-gguf` fuzz target asserts exactly that composition — it probes the vendored parser **only for cases the hardened one accepts**, which is the load path — because a raw sweep's abort takes the whole harness with it and every other target's result becomes unobservable. The raw probe remains reachable for reporting upstream. **The upstream abort is owed to `ggml-org/llama.cpp`** (§8). ‖ **⚑ THE RECEIPT CORRECTION (binding, from the directive):** both banked fuzz receipts said *"the GGUF parser"* and meant *the splitter's*. Corrected in place, in both files, naming which parser the CPU-hours protected and which they never touched — **overstating by silence is §7.31's shape in the evidence layer.** And `vendored-gguf` is deliberately **excluded from CI's required-target list**, because **CI never builds the real engine at all (L1)**, so its CI status is **unavailable, never green**. ‖ **Tests +3** (`shard_anchor.rs`: symlink refused before hashing; a shard swapped between verification and load refused with the model dropped; the hardened parser running on the load path — with the control that a real shard still parses, and the assertion that the case which SIGABRTs `llama.cpp` is refused cheaply and structurally before the engine sees it) **+ an 8th fuzz target.**

44. **⛔ AUDIT WAVE 3 — H12 + H11, the supply-chain parser pair (2026-08-23). Rule-10: IMPLEMENTATION (proceed + log).** ‖ **H12 — the fuzzer structurally excluded the bug it existed to find (rule 19, SILENT degree, and the auditor named the line).** `gen.rs` read `let elem = if depth >= 2 { 4 } else { … }`, so **no generated case could ever nest an array deeper than two levels**, while the parser's unbounded recursion needs *thousands* to matter.
