Twenty-four CPU-hours of green verdicts were, on this question, a report about the generator. **The auditor marked it "CONFIRMED BY READING, NOT EXECUTED"; it is now executed** — I built the input and ran it: **600 053 bytes at depth 50 000 overflowed the main thread's 8 MiB stack**, `fatal runtime error: stack overflow, aborting`. That is the same **uncatchable** class (SIGSEGV/abort) as the `reserve_for` amplification this parser was hardened against — `catch_unwind` never sees it. **Fix: nested arrays are REFUSED (`GgufError::NestedArray`), not depth-capped**, because **upstream GGUF has no nested-array support at all**, so no real model contains one: a cap preserves a shape nothing produces, a refusal removes the recursion. The ordinary flat array (`tokenizer.ggml.tokens`) is untouched — asserted, because a fix that refused all arrays would refuse every real model, a worse failure than the one being fixed. The generator's cap is now **64**, far above what the parser accepts, so a regression that re-admits nesting produces cases that reach it — and there is a test asserting **the generator can still reach that depth**, because a generator that stops where the parser stops can only confirm the parser agrees with itself. ‖ **H11 — `general.architecture`, read from a community model file, became an output PATH.** `format!("{arch}-stage{stage}-…")` for shards, and in the CLI `out_dir.join(format!("{arch}.signing.pkcs8"))` — and **`Path::join` discards the left side entirely when the right side is absolute**, so a model declaring `general.architecture = "/etc/cron.d/x"` writes shards, the manifest, **and the private signing key** wherever the operator can write. No character validation existed anywhere. **Rule 19, INDISTINGUISHING degree:** every splitter fixture had `arch = "llama"` or `"qwen2"`, and under those inputs the sanitised and unsanitised implementations produce **identical output for every call** — no amount of running the existing tests could separate them. **Fix:** an **allow-list** (`^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+$`, no leading dot, ≤ 64) validated **at the source** where the value enters the program rather than at each of the three join sites (a check at the use site must be remembered three times; at the source, once) — deliberately an allow-list, because a deny-list of `..` and `/` invites the next encoding. Plus `safe_join`, which requires exactly **one `Normal` path component**, used for every shard/manifest/key write **and for `verify`'s read (M19)**: a validly signed manifest can still *name* `../../etc/passwd`, and signature validity is not path safety. **Tests +2** (nine hostile architecture strings refused with six real ones still accepted; the nesting refusal at depth 50 000 and at depth 1, on both `parse` and the H6 metadata path, with the flat-array control).

45. **⛔ AUDIT WAVE 3 — the platform group: H17 · H18 · H20 · M1 · M2 · M3 · L1 (2026-08-23). Rule-10: IMPLEMENTATION (proceed + log) throughout — no state machine decides differently and no wire shape changed.** ‖ **⚑ ALL SEVEN ARE THE SAME ORACLE FAILURE (rule 19, INDISTINGUISHING degree), and naming it once explains all of them: every transport and provisioning test drove a WELL-BEHAVED PEER.** It completed the handshake, it sent the payload it declared, it bound to `127.0.0.1`, it finished long before a certificate could expire, and it asked for output positions in order. Under that driver *"handshake with a timeout"* and *"handshake without one"* accept the same connections; *"read the payload"* and *"read the payload or give up"* return the same frames; `is_unspecified` and `is_wildcard` classify the same addresses; a 397-day certificate and one valid until the year 4096 are the same certificate; and a bounded ring and an unbounded map return the same cached token. **Seven pairs of functions the entire suite could not tell apart** — which is why adding seeds, runs or coverage would never have surfaced any of them. ‖ **H17 — a bootstrap blob is a PRIVATE KEY FILE and was written like a log.** `File::create` (follows symlinks, default umask ⇒ **0644**) to **predictable names in a shared `/tmp`**, containing `key_pkcs8_der` **and** the cluster CA cert; cleanup was `Drop`-only, so a `kill -9` left the key behind. Any local user could read it, and **via C2 a stolen worker key is a worker**. Now `create_new` + **0600** inside a **0700** directory: `O_EXCL` also refuses a planted symlink, and refusing to clobber means a stale key is an error someone looks at rather than a silent overwrite (`write_to_replacing` is the explicit re-run path). ‖ **H18 — one silent TCP connection stalled the listener for everyone.** `accept()` performs the handshake and every caller `await`s it **sequentially**, spawning only the *post-handshake* connection — so a peer that connects and **sends nothing** parks the accept loop forever, **no certificate required**, which is why C2's role binding does not help. Bounded at 10 s, and the refusal **names the peer**, which the old code discarded: an unbounded stall that is also unattributable is twice as hard to diagnose. **Stated honestly: the structural fix is moving the handshake into the spawned task; this bounds the damage and the residual is named in §8 rather than implied by a green test.** ‖ **M1 — the hold is now bounded in TIME as well as size.** The 64 MiB cap is enforced pre-allocation (which is why this is not the D2 class); what was unbounded was the *duration* — 12 header bytes reserve up to 64 MiB and then wait forever, so 16 idle connections from one certificate holder commit a gigabyte. Under the honest-worker ruling that is an **accident** (a wedged peer, a half-open NAT mapping), and **the assumption excuses malice, never accidents**. 60 s default, per-connection settable so the push-lane test asserts the bound in 250 ms instead of paying 60 s on every run forever. ‖ **M2 — `check_bind_addr` had a third spelling it could not see.** `is_unspecified` is **false for `::ffff:0.0.0.0`**, and on Linux an `AF_INET6` socket bound to it with `IPV6_V6ONLY=0` accepts on **every IPv4 interface** — exactly the state the check exists to prevent. The listen address is a runtime string from the bootstrap blob, so the repository-wide grep could not cover it and the unit test only knew the two spellings it was written with. New `is_wildcard` covers all of them, and **the grep test now looks for the mapped form too**. *(The guard caught my own new test file and I added it to the allow-list with its reason — that is the guard working, not a nuisance.)* ‖ **M3 — certificates never expired and the CA could mint sub-CAs.** `rcgen` defaults are **1975 → 4096**, so a key leaked through H17 was valid **forever**, with no CRL and no rotation to make it otherwise. Leaves now 397 days (the CA/Browser Forum maximum, as a familiar bound), the CA 10 years, and `BasicConstraints::Constrained(0)` says what is true: this CA signs leaves. **A bounded lifetime is not revocation and is not claimed to be** — it is the difference between a leak that expires and one that does not; CRL/OCSP and a re-pair flow stay owed (M4·2). The regression **parses the DER and reads the dates back out**, because asserting the constant is set is not asserting the certificate carries it. ‖ **H20 — `sampled_ring` was unbounded and keyed by an attacker-chosen `output_pos`**, cleared only on an accepted `BEGIN_RECOVERY`, retaining ~1 MiB per entry. Now evicted below a 256-position window: the ring exists for **I14 idempotence**, and idempotence only needs the recent window because the coordinator never re-requests a position below its own committed watermark. **And the installed penalty window is capped at `penalty_last_n`** — a 1 MiB snapshot carries **262 144 entries** that `serialize()` re-emitted into *every* later snapshot and `apply_repeat_penalty` walked on *every* sample, so one oversized install made every subsequent token slower and every subsequent snapshot bigger, permanently. Anything beyond `penalty_last_n` cannot affect a sampled token, so keeping it was pure cost. ‖ **L1 — RAISED, and correctly: this is release integrity, not a low.** The M−1 layer-window patch — the change that makes pipeline sharding possible at all — exists **only as an uncommitted working-tree modification** of the submodule (7 files, matching `spike/llama-cpp-layer-window.patch`), and `build.rs` proceeded on `llama.h.exists()` alone. **The oracle failure is at its most literal here: the only machine that ever builds the engine is this one, and on this machine the patch has been applied since M−1** — so every green engine-gated result in this repository is evidence about a working tree no other checkout reproduces. `build.rs` now checks for the patch's own marker and **degrades to a stub with a named reason** rather than building an ABI-mismatched engine, and `patch_integrity.rs` asserts (a) a linked engine implies the patch is present and (b) the committed patch describes every modified vendored file — a patch that has drifted from the tree is worse than none, because it reads as reproducibility. **This does not make the patch durable; pinning a fork SHA is the real fix and is owed (§8), since creating a fork is an owner action.** ‖ **Tests +6** (`platform_hardening.rs` ×4 — a silent peer, an undelivered payload, four wildcard spellings with four controls, certificate dates and basic constraints read from the DER; `patch_integrity.rs` ×2). **Verification: 353 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored across 84 suites, exit 0; clippy clean.**

46. **⚑⚑ THE CONVERGENCE, AND A PROPOSAL — the coordinator driver is not a residual, it is the missing half of the architecture rule (2026-08-23; the design authority asked for this when Wave 3 closed). ⏸️ PROPOSAL ONLY — this is a scope decision (rule 5) and it pauses for the owner.** ‖ **What I checked before proposing, rather than reasoning from the residual list:** (a) **`hydra_state::coordinator::Coordinator` is constructed in exactly one place in the workspace — `hydra-sim`.** No shipping binary owns one. (b) **`hydra-coordinator` has no `hydra-transport` dependency at all**, so the crate that holds sessions, the commit stream and the event log **cannot open a worker link**. (c) **There is no `hydra-coordinator` binary.** The binaries are `hydra-worker` plus demo/harness drivers (`hydra-wan`, `hydra-3node-{wan,kill}`, `hydra-2node-ci`, `hydra-local-pair`). (d) Every activation in a non-test path is a hand-rolled two-line sequence in `pair.rs` and those demo binaries: `send(COMMIT_ACTIVATION)` → `send(FINALIZE_ACTIVATION)`, **`activation_attempt_id` hard-coded to `1`**, no ack collection, no `ACTIVATION_COMMIT_INTENT`, no `ACTIVATION_COMPLETE`, no abort, no `ACTIVATION_UNSERVABLE`. ‖ **⚑ So the activation transaction is implemented OUTSIDE `hydra-state` on the coordinator side, and BLUEPRINT §2 says in as many words: *"Any protocol behavior implemented outside `hydra-state` is a defect."*** The **stage** half is genuine — `Worker::on_frame` steps the real `Stage` SM, which is why C4/H2/H3/H19 were fixable *in the SM*. The **coordinator** half is a script. **Stated precisely, and no wider: the coordinator SM is VERIFIED BUT NOT DEPLOYED, and the deployed coordinator behaviour is not the verified SM.** M1's TLC and DST evidence is real evidence about `hydra-state`; it transfers to the shipping coordinator only when the shipping coordinator *is* `hydra-state`. **The M2/M3 demonstrations stand exactly as recorded** — the kills, the byte-identical resumes, the three-assertion bars all happened — and this does not re-open those gates; what it says is narrower: *those runs prove the choreography works when driven correctly, and say nothing about what happens when it is driven incorrectly under crash, loss or supersession, which is precisely what the SM exists for and what TLC covered.* ‖ **The residuals are not four items, they are one absence reported four times** — plus three more found while checking: **H2** (completion evidence: nothing produces a `completion_id` to compare against) · **`WalKindTag::Terminate`** (written-but-unreached: nothing emits `SESSION_TERMINATE`) · **H4** (the auditor's own note — *"a latent SM defect the driver will inherit"*; Wave 1d hardened the SM with `AuthenticatedRank`, and **no production code path feeds it**) · **H10's residual** (no recovery/truncation record is ever written by `hydra-coordinator`) · **M12** (CSPRNG session ids must be *minted by the coordinator*) · **M8** (binding a WAL file to its session needs a component that owns the session) · **M13's sender half** (`RESET_RECOVERY_ATTEMPT` has no wire decode arm **and** nothing to send it). ‖ **➡️ PROPOSAL: an M4 slice, sequenced FIRST within M4 — not a v1.1 item.** **Three reasons, in order of weight.** **(1) Without it there is no v1 coordinator to ship.** M4's own DoD is *"a non-author can set up a 3-machine cluster from the README in under 30 minutes"* — today that person has no coordinator binary to run, so M4's DoD is unreachable, and the pairing UX and dashboard slices both assume a process that runs sessions. This is why it cannot be deferred: v1.1 would be the release that first makes v1 runnable. **(2) The architecture rule is binding and is currently violated.** Shipping the hand-rolled path ships the one defect the blueprint names explicitly, and it is the defect that makes M1's verification not apply where it matters most. **(3) The deferral cost is correctness, not features:** ship without it and `ACTIVATION_UNSERVABLE` is never written by a real coordinator, so §6.7's I22 recourse exists only in the model; H4's quorum stays latent in whatever driver eventually appears; and every crash window §6.5 classifies is classified by nobody. ‖ **The fair counter-argument, stated rather than buried:** the driver is substantial (session lifecycle, ack collection over real mTLS keyed on authenticated identity, WAL-before-wire on every transition, recovery orchestration, supersession), and M2/M3 did demonstrate the choreography end-to-end — so one *could* ship v1 as a single-session demo runtime and call the driver productionisation. **I think that is the wrong call, and the reason is the same sentence as above:** the demos prove the choreography works when driven correctly; the SM exists for the case where it is not. **Shipping the demo driver ships precisely the part TLC never covered.** ‖ **Shape of the slice, so the estimate is not hand-waved:** it is **not a new binary from scratch** — it is the missing SM-owning layer inside the existing `hydra-coordinator` crate: add `hydra-transport` as a dependency; own one `Coordinator` per session; execute its `Effect::WriteWal` against the real commit/control WAL and its `Effect::Send` over real mTLS; feed `StageCommitted`/`StageFinalized` from `AcceptedConn`'s **authenticated** peer identity (closing H4 by construction); and add a `hydra-coordinator` binary. Every one of the seven residuals above closes as a consequence rather than as separate work.

47. **⛔ WAVE 4 SEAM 1 — the real M7, plus three more gloss drifts found by the rule-20 re-verification (2026-08-23). Rule-10: IMPLEMENTATION throughout.** ‖ **M7 (the auditor's), fixed properly.** Three parts, and the third is the one that lost data. **(a) Retain-or-fail (I9):** `Session::commit_group` called `group.take()`, which **drains the buffer**, and *then* appended. On failure the `?` returned with the batch **already gone** — tokens S_P had sampled, that no durable record mentioned, and that the next group's `first_pos` skipped straight past. The error was reported, so it did not look like silent corruption; what was silent was the **hole**. Now `peek` → append → `confirm`: the buffer clears only after the write is durable. **(b) Contiguity on write:** a group must begin at `generation_durable_pos + 1` and its entries must be dense, refused **before** the disk. **(c) Gaps and order on read, checked BEFORE the sort** — `recovery::read` sorted first, which *quietly repaired the very disorder that would have been evidence*; records are appended in order, so an out-of-order ledger is a damaged one, and sorting made it look fine. A gap matters because those tokens are replayed as the session's history: a missing position shifts every later token one place, and the recovered stream is silently a different generation from the one the client already saw. ‖ **⚑ HOW M7 AND H9 COMPOSE, recorded because the test found it and my first expectation was wrong:** after a failed commit the batch is **retained** (M7) *and* the stream is **poisoned** (H9), so the retry on that stream is **refused** rather than succeeding. Both are right and they say different things — *the tokens are not discarded* and *this file is not written to again*. Recovery is reopen-then-recommit via `CommitStream::open`, which discards the partial tail durably and restores the watermarks. The poison check is ordered **before** the contiguity check, because a poisoned stream's `generation_durable_pos` no longer reliably describes the file, so reasoning about contiguity against it would be reasoning from a number we do not trust. ‖ **⚑ AND THE DOUBLE-COMMIT TEST HAD TO MOVE BELOW THE API** — it built its corrupt ledger *through* `CommitStream`, which the write-side guard now refuses. It constructs the record with a raw `WalWriter` instead, which is the read-side guard's real reason: **the file is what a DIFFERENT process, or an older build, recovers from.** ‖ **THREE FURTHER GLOSS DRIFTS (standing rule 20's first harvest).** **M11** — `bytes_to_f32_le` still truncated a trailing partial word via `chunks_exact`. C3 makes that unreachable *from the boundary call sites*, which is exactly the argument the audit says not to accept alone: a function that truncates will truncate for the next caller. Now returns `None`, refused as a `Shape` error. **M13** — the directive framed it purely as the **spec silence**; the auditor's M13 has a **second half**: *"`RESET_RECOVERY_ATTEMPT` has no wire decode arm"*. There was none — an inbound reset fell to `UnsupportedBody`, so **spec §6.4's reconstruction-invalidating restart was unreachable over the wire**, while the stage SM has implemented `RecvReset` since M1. Added (decode arm, encoder, worker handling that truncates the engine KV and the M9 frontier together). *Its spec half is CLOSED and correct:* the amendment ruled a reset does **not** reset the attempt space and a stage **retains** its highest accepted attempt, which is what the SM already did — the auditor wrote before the amendment. **M5** — the gloss said "the 14 shim entry points" (done in 1c); the auditor's text adds *"`text_len` narrowed `usize` → `i32`"*. A ≥ 2 GiB prompt wrapped negative. Now `i32::try_from`.

48. **✅⛔ THE UPSTREAM FILING WAS APPROVED, AND I AM NOT FILING IT — the bug is already reported and already fixed upstream (2026-08-23). Reported back rather than posted.** The owner approved filing the `gguf_init_from_file` abort. **Before posting I searched the tracker, and the premise turned out to be false.** ‖ **What I found:** the abort is **`GGML_ASSERT(!key.empty())` at `ggml/src/gguf.cpp:143`**, reached by a GGUF whose metadata key has length zero. Minimised from the fuzzer's 371-byte case to **40 bytes** with a standalone C probe, which also gave the exact line. It is reported upstream **at least four times** — #20873 (*"malformed GGUF metadata can trigger empty key assert"*), #22410, #24263, #24884 (*"Empty GGUF metadata key aborts loader instead of returning a load error"*) — plus PRs #24445/#24446 (*"gguf: reject empty KV keys with a clear error"*). **And it is fixed:** upstream `master`'s `gguf.cpp` now carries `if (ok && key.empty()) { GGML_LOG_ERROR(...); ok = false; }` in the reader. **Our vendored pin `13f2b28b` does not have that guard** — verified by diffing the same region of both files. **So this is not an upstream defect we found; it is a stale submodule pin.** Filing would have duplicated four closed issues and spent maintainer time on a fixed bug. **The approval was given on a premise that did not hold, so the right action is to report that, not to post.** ‖ **Reclassified in §8: from "report upstream" to "bump the submodule".** The cost is real and blueprint-mandated: **BLUEPRINT §1.2 requires every `vendor/llama.cpp` bump to re-run the M−1 spike sweep as part of the M2 golden-token gate**, and **L1** means the layer-window patch must be re-ported to the new base. **Not urgent-blocking, and the reason is the composition fix, not luck:** since §7.43 the worker runs the hardened parser first, so a file with an empty key is refused before `llama.cpp` sees it — Hydra is protected *whatever* the pin. What changes is the classification: the pin is now **security-relevant staleness**, not hygiene. ‖ **The general lesson, and it is rule 12's shape again: an approval carries its premise.** "Post it" was approval to report a finding; it was not approval to report a *duplicate of a fixed bug*, and the difference was one search away.

49. **⛔ WAVE 4 — the HTTP surface: H15 · H16 · M16 · H21 (partial), before any binary serves it (2026-08-24). Rule-10: IMPLEMENTATION. ⚑ THE ORDERING IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT INCIDENTAL: M4·0's coordinator binary is the FIRST thing that will serve this router**, so every defect below is fixed while the surface is still library code and the blast radius is a test. ‖ **H15 — an EMPTY configured token authenticated every anonymous request.** `check` collapsed a missing `Authorization` header to `""` and compared `hash("") == self.token_digest`; with an empty token (an unset env var, an `unwrap_or_default()`, a blank config field) those are **equal**. The M4·1 checklist row said the token was *"required by the type"*, and that was true of the **argument**, not of a **non-empty secret** — `ApiAuth::new` could not be called without passing something, and `""` is something. **§7.31's exact shape in a checklist row.** Fixed at both ends: construction returns `Result` and refuses tokens under `MIN_API_TOKEN_LEN` (16), and an **absent header is refused before anything is hashed**, so "no header" and "wrong token" stop being the same code path. **The oracle, per rule 19:** the suite used **one fixed 14-character token** — the auditor named the number — and a valid token exercises the happy path while saying nothing about what the check does when the *configured* token is empty. ‖ **⚑ AND FIXING IT EXPOSED A SECOND, QUIETER INSTANCE.** The bearer header was spelled as a **literal in five places**. Raising the minimum invalidated the old literal, and three tests that exist to check **Host** and **Origin** kept passing — because they now failed the request for the **wrong reason** (auth, not host). *A test that passes for a different reason than it claims is the indistinguishing degree again*, and it is invisible precisely while the suite is green. All five now derive from `API_TOKEN`, so a token change cannot re-aim a test at a different check. ‖ **H16 — one panic wedged the coordinator until restart.** `done` was set on **exactly one exit path**, the generator channel closing cleanly. Every other exit — a panic in `make()`/`gen()`, a panic inside the pump, a runtime that fails to build — left it `false` **forever**, and `active_session()` treats `done == false` as live, so **every later client got 409** with nothing reporting why. Now a `DoneOnDrop` guard: it fires on the normal path, the early return and the unwinding panic alike, and **cannot be forgotten the way a `break` arm can**. A poisoned mutex is recovered with `into_inner` rather than `unwrap` — the state behind it is a log, a sender and a flag, none of which a mid-update panic can leave unsafe to read, and panicking again in a `Drop` would abort the process. **The oracle:** wedge states are unreachable from a happy-path driver, so the new test **panics the generator on purpose**. ‖ **M16 — `client_drained` had no caller outside its own unit test.** The unit test proved the function decrements a counter; **nothing proved anyone called it, and nobody did.** So `pending_emit` only ever grew, `commit_group` returned `Paused` forever after `emit_capacity` events, the pump stopped committing, and the stream **ended with HTTP 200 and no error** — a truncated generation that looked like a completed one. The SSE task and the pump are different tasks on different threads, so the acknowledgement now travels through `SessionState::client_acked_through`, and it is an **id rather than a count**: with `Last-Event-ID` resume a client can reconnect and re-read, so *how far anyone has got* is monotonic while *how many were handed over* is not. Also bounded: the session registry and the idempotency map are **pruned** (they retained every event of every generation for the life of the process), and `Idempotency-Key` is length-capped and **refused rather than truncated**, since truncating would silently merge two distinct keys. Constant-time digest comparison via `blake3::Hash` — a small hole fixed for a small reason: the checklist claims a digest comparison, and `[u8; 32]`'s `PartialEq` is not constant-time while `blake3::Hash`'s is. ‖ **H21 — PARTIAL, and named as partial.** The router still has **no TLS layer**, so the one link carrying user prompts and the API bearer token is plaintext HTTP. What is fixed here is the *claim*: `ApiAuth::loopback` allow-listed `https://` origins the server **cannot serve**. Serving the API over the cluster's own rustls material is the real fix and is **owed (§8), landing with M4·0** — which is the binary that will first serve it. ‖ **Tests +4.**

51. **⚑ THE CRATE GRAPH ENFORCED THE VIOLATION (2026-08-24, promoted from §7.50's seam-A note; the origin of standing rule 21).** §7.46 reported that the activation transaction was implemented outside `hydra-state` on the coordinator side and called BLUEPRINT §2 violated. That was true and it was **not the whole diagnosis**. The codec lived in `hydra-worker`; `hydra-worker` depends on `hydra-coordinator` (a deliberate, documented one-way edge); therefore **a coordinator that encoded a single frame would have closed a dependency cycle and failed to compile.** The correct placement was not merely unchosen, it was **unreachable** — so the behaviour went where the build permitted, which was the test drivers. ‖ **Why this matters more than the fix:** read as a discipline failure it produces a review comment and the cause survives. Read as a **structural** failure it produces `hydra-wire`, and the misplacement cannot recur. **The rule a toolchain makes unfollowable gets broken silently, repeatedly, and looks like negligence every time.** This is rule 12's doctrine pointed at *architecture*: the build is a layer between an intention and its realisation, and it gets no presumption of neutrality either.

52. **✅ M4·0 SEAM C — the `hydra-coordinator` BINARY: the verified machine now drives production (2026-08-24).** ‖ **The binary exists and runs.** `--dev` mints a throwaway cluster CA, **mints a session id from the system CSPRNG (M12)**, creates a commit stream and control WAL **bound to that session (M8)**, and serves the OpenAI-compatible API **over TLS**. Verified by running it: no token → **401**, correct token → **200**, and **plaintext HTTP on that port → connection refused**. ‖ **H21 CLOSED PROPERLY, not via the fallback.** The router is served over the cluster's own rustls material (`api_server_config`: presents the coordinator's identity, **does not ask the client for a certificate** — a browser or SDK has none, and its credential is the bearer token). The handshake is bounded and runs **in the spawned task, not the accept loop** — H18's lesson applied where it is being written rather than after an audit finds it again. **Stated for the operator:** the cluster CA is self-signed at pairing, so a client must be told to trust it; that is a real setup step and it is the price of not shipping a plaintext API. ‖ **M12 + M8 closed**, exactly as predicted — cheap once a real coordinator mints sessions. `SessionFence::mint` replaces `dev(seed)` (which filled every identity vector from **one byte**), and `WalScan::open_for_session` finally **compares** the `cluster_id`/`session_scope` the file header has carried since M0 and nothing ever read. Regression: another session's ledger is refused on open, with the control that the owning session's opens. ‖ **⚑ THE ACCEPTANCE ORACLE, and what it caught.** `coordinator_drives_production.rs` runs the transaction **coordinator SM → real mTLS → real `Worker` → real `Stage` SM**, with every frame produced by `hydra_state::Coordinator`'s own effects; and a second test where a stage is **lost after the decision** and the *coordinator* — not the harness — decides the recourse (`ACTIVATION_UNSERVABLE`, durable before the transition, then SUPERSEDING), with the on-disk record set asserted so a different process would classify the restart identically. **This is the evidence that the SM drives production rather than tests.** ‖ **⚑ AND IT IMMEDIATELY CAUGHT THE FAILURE THE DESIGN AUTHORITY NAMED WHEN RATIFYING H2.** The ruling said `completion_hash()` belongs in `hydra-state` because *"divergent reimplementations of a hash are a classic silent-mismatch source"* — and I had left a **second** implementation (`control_wal::tuple_hash`) in the coordinator, so the driver signed with one function while the stage checked against another. **The finalize was silently refused and the test HUNG.** Deleted; there is now exactly one definition. **Two lessons recorded rather than one:** (a) the warning was correct and I reproduced the exact defect it described **while implementing the fix for it** — a duplicate introduced during a refactor is invisible to review precisely because both copies look correct in isolation; (b) **the failure mode was a HANG, not an error** — a refused control message produces *silence* from the stage, which reads as a network problem. That is **audit M10** (`ERR_*` never emitted on the wire) showing its cost: still owed, and now with a concrete instance attached.

53. **⚑⚑ THE DIVERGENT-HASH INCIDENT — two lessons, both promoted (2026-08-24, from §7.52; design-authority ruling).** ‖ **What happened:** the H2 ratification said `completion_hash()` belongs in `hydra-state` **because divergent reimplementations of a hash are a classic silent-mismatch source**. Implementing exactly that, I left a **second** implementation (`control_wal::tuple_hash`) in the coordinator — so the driver signed with one function and the stage checked against another. ‖ **⚑ LESSON 1, and it generalises: THE FIX FOR A DIVERGENCE CLASS IS THE HIGHEST-RISK SITE FOR THAT CLASS.** A duplicate introduced *during a refactor* is invisible to review **because both copies are correct in isolation** — there is no wrong line to find. The moment you move a definition to a shared home is precisely the moment a copy is left behind at the old one, and the review that would catch it is reading the new code, where nothing is wrong. **Binding consequence: after consolidating any definition, grep for the name you just moved and prove there is exactly one.** ‖ **⚑ LESSON 2: THE FAILURE WAS A HANG, NOT AN ERROR — and that promotes M10 from a category to a measured cost.** The stage refused the finalize by returning no effects, so it simply **did not reply**; the acceptance test waited forever. **A hang reads as a network fault and is debugged as one**, so the debugging cost of silence scales with the size of the system while the cost of a reply is one frame. **M10 was raised in priority and is now DONE (below).**

54. **✅ AUDIT M10 — a refused control frame now SAYS SO (2026-08-24). Rule-10: IMPLEMENTATION (proceed + log) — the codes are already in `hydra-proto`'s `ErrCode` and §1.3 already names Case C as `ERR_TRANSITION`; this is code catching up with the spec, and the TLA+ model does not model error replies at all, so nothing there moves.** Every `return Vec::new()` in the stage SM's refusal paths was a decision the sender never heard: a wrong-epoch `COMMIT_ACTIVATION`, a `BEGIN_RECOVERY` whose `target != base+1` or whose `truncate_to` is out of range, a `COMMIT_ACTIVATION` in a state that cannot take it, a `FINALIZE_ACTIVATION` for another epoch, and — **the one that cost real time** — a finalize whose completion evidence does not match. They now emit `StageEffect::Refused { code, detail }`, which the worker encodes as `ERR_FENCED` / `ERR_TRANSITION` and logs. A free `refused()` helper means every site reads identically and **a new refusal cannot accidentally be written as a bare `Vec::new()`**. ‖ **⚑ TWO TESTS TURNED RED, AND BOTH WERE THE ASSERTING DEGREE (§7.41).** `mixed_epoch_commit_is_rejected_by_f1` and `a_finalize_whose_evidence_names_a_different_activation_is_refused` both asserted **`effects.is_empty()`** — i.e. they encoded *"a refusal produces silence"* as the intended behaviour, so the fix for the silence turned them red. Rewritten to assert the refusal **message**, keeping the separate assertion that it is not an ack. **Exactly the predicted shape, and it arrived as good news.**

55. **✅ M4·2 — PAIRING: the ceremony that creates a cluster's trust (2026-08-24).** `hydra-cli pair` opens a window, mints a **CSPRNG** six-digit PIN, provisions the **coordinator's own identity** (the artifact deferred in seam C), and issues per-device identities; `hydra-cli status` reports what it can observe, with each line carrying **its source**, so a reader can tell a measurement from an assumption. Every file is written **0600 inside a 0700 directory** — H17's lesson applied where the code is being written rather than after an audit finds it. ‖ **⚑ "THE CA PRIVATE KEY NEVER LEAVES THE COORDINATOR" IS NOW A PROPERTY OF THE API SHAPE, NOT A RULE TO REMEMBER.** `ClusterCa::save_private` is the **only** method that puts the key in a buffer, it writes straight to a 0600 file, and it returns `()`. There is no accessor; `IssuedIdentity` has nowhere to put a CA key. Persisting it *on* the coordinator is **required**, not a compromise: a CA that vanishes on restart cannot issue a replacement when a device's key leaks, which is exactly H17's recovery path. Asserted structurally — after a real pairing, the device's directory is scanned and **must not contain the CA key's bytes**, and the key's mode is asserted `0600`. That is the test that fails if someone later adds a well-meaning `to_bytes()`. ‖ **⚑ THE ORACLE, named before the tests were written (rule 19): pairing is a ONE-SHOT CEREMONY, and its natural test is "open a window, read the PIN, type the PIN, get an identity" — which passes forever and proves almost nothing.** Every interesting failure is a path that driver cannot reach. So: **a wrong PIN** is refused, counted, and **burns the window** — and the correct PIN then **still fails**, because a counter a correct PIN can reset is decorative; **an expired window** refuses even the right PIN (with `now` injected, since a test that waits three real minutes is a test nobody runs); **a dead window is not a PIN oracle** — burned/expired are checked **before** the PIN, so probing after the fact cannot distinguish "wrong PIN" from "right PIN, too late"; and **re-pair after compromise** issues a *different* certificate over the same cluster CA, so one leaked key does not mean re-pairing every machine. ‖ **⛔ M3's REVOCATION RESIDUAL: RE-STATED AS OWED, not closed.** Re-pairing issues a new credential; **it does not revoke the old one.** There is no CRL/OCSP distribution, so the previous certificate stays valid until it expires, and what bounds the exposure is Wave 3's **397-day leaf lifetime — a ceiling, not a remedy.** Stated in the re-pair test itself so the next reader meets it there rather than inferring safety. §8 carries it with its condition.

56. **✅✅ M4·0b — THE RECOVERY AND SESSION-LIFECYCLE HALF: crash-safe sessions is now a property of the PRODUCT, not of the harnesses (2026-08-24).** ‖ **⚑ THE FINDING THAT SIZED THE SLICE:** `hydra_state::Coordinator` **had no action for entering recovery at all.** The TLA+ model has had `CoordBeginRecovery`, `SendBeginRecovery` and `CoordResetAttempt` since **v0.9**; the Rust SM implemented **none** of them. So the coordinator could activate a session it had started and **could not recover one** — which is exactly why D1 recovery lived in test drivers. Rule-10 / rule-11 classification: **the code was a strict SUBSET of the model, and this makes it match** — the same "code catching up" shape as H2/H3, so **the model does not move and no TLC rerun is owed**. ‖ **Added, mirroring the model action-for-action:** `ProceedBeginRecovery` (durable `BEGIN_RECOVERY`, then the epoch advance), `ProceedSendBeginRecovery` (the message, **after** the record), `ProceedResetAttempt` (M13's **sender half** — the wire arm landed in Wave 4 and the stage SM has implemented `RecvReset` since M1, and nothing ever decided to send one), and `ProceedTerminate` (the `SESSION_TERMINATE` path that had a record, a `WalKindTag`, and no emitter). **And `ProceedStartSuperseding` now writes its `BEGIN_RECOVERY` record, which the model always did** — the code advanced the epoch, told nobody and recorded nothing, so a crash in the superseding window left §6.5 with no `BEGIN` to classify from, **in the one place I22 depends on**. ‖ **⚑ THE ACCEPTANCE RUN THE README'S CENTRAL CLAIM RESTS ON.** `a_killed_stage_is_recovered_by_the_real_coordinator_with_the_three_assertion_bar`: a **real `hydra-worker` OS process**, a **literal `kill -9`**, a replacement on a fresh port, and **every decision taken by the coordinator SM** — that a recovery is needed, that the record is durable before anyone is told, what epoch it targets. The test supplies only the failure. **Three-assertion bar, all green:** (1) **disk truth** — the durable record set is what §6.5 would classify from (`BeginRecovery{base:0,target:1}` and `ActivationComplete` both present); (2) **no position committed twice** — `[0,1,2,3,4]`, dense and ascending; (3) **byte-identical to an uninterrupted seeded run**. ‖ **Two more tests turned red and both were the ASSERTING degree again** (§7.41): `post_decision_loss_supersedes_and_recovers` and `f_unservable_crash_in_superseding_window_restarts_to_superseding` asserted supersession lands in `Reconstructing` **atomically** — encoding the missing record as intended behaviour. Rewritten to step through `WalDurable(BeginRecovery)`. **Third occurrence of the same shape in three slices, each arriving as good news.** ‖ **H10's recovery record CLOSED** — `a_recovery_resumes_from_the_durable_frontier_and_never_re_commits` asserts the reopened stream resumes at the last position **actually on disk** and that re-committing an already-durable position is refused (M7), so a recovery cannot silently re-show the client output it has already seen. ‖ **All three convergent items closed: `Terminate` ✅, H10's recovery record ✅, M13's sender half ✅.** ‖ **Residual, reported rather than folded in:** the driver decides *that* a recovery begins and *that* stages are told; the **strategy path** between (catch-up `REBUILD_APPLY` drive, `INSTALL_SAMPLER_CHECKPOINT`, re-activation of the rebuilt stage) is still executed by the pipeline drivers in `pair.rs`, not by the coordinator. §8 carries it.

57. **✅✅ M4·0c — THE STRATEGY PATH MOVES INTO THE DRIVER: the coordinator now owns recovery end to end (2026-08-24).** ‖ **Correction to §7.56's wording first:** the residual said the strategy path lived in `pair.rs`. **It did not** — `pair.rs` never drove a recovery. It lived in the **demo/CI binaries** (`hydra-wan`, `hydra-3node-kill`, `hydra-2node-ci`) and in five test files, each hand-sending `CATCH_UP_CONTEXT` and `INSTALL_SAMPLER_CHECKPOINT` in the right order. The substance was right and the location was wrong; recorded because a residual that misnames its own site is a residual someone will look for in the wrong place. ‖ **`ActivationDriver::reconstruct` is the whole path**, in the spec's order (§6.2/§6.3): **rebuild the KV** with the strategy's frames → **drive the control-plane frontier** with `CATCH_UP_CONTEXT{goal}` and require `CATCH_UP_READY` → **install the sampler checkpoint** (I17: installation precedes activation, always). Re-activation is then the ordinary transaction, because §6.6 is deliberately **one mechanism** for INITIAL and RECOVERY. ‖ **`RecoveryStrategy` carries A and B**: `BoundaryReplay` (Strategy A, §6.2, the D1 default — replay the durable `BOUNDARY_COPY` records, which is what makes D1 faster than a recompute) and `TokenReplay` (Strategy B, §6.3, D0 — replay committed tokens as `REBUILD_APPLY`). **The strategy is a property of the session's durability mode, fixed at admission, not a per-recovery decision** — so the SM decides *when* each phase happens and the strategy decides only *what the rebuild frames carry*, which is an effect and not a transition. ‖ **⚑ THE STRUCTURAL CLAIM, and how the acceptance test makes it checkable: the test holds NO CONNECTION.** Every link is *moved* into the driver at construction, so the harness's only powers are to supply a failure (`kill -9` of a real OS process), hand over a replacement link, and read state back. **There is no `conn.send` available to it.** A future edit wanting to hand-send a recovery frame would first have to take a connection back out of the driver — a visible, deliberate act rather than a slip. That is the difference between "the harness does not participate" as a promise and as a property. ‖ **Three-assertion bar, green:** disk truth (`BeginRecovery` + `ActivationComplete` both durable); no output position committed twice (`[0,1,2]`, dense); recovery changed no committed token. ‖ **⚑ ENGINE-GATED, and stated as such: a rebuild is DATA-PLANE work.** A worker with no engine answers `EngineUnavailable`, so this test's CI status is **unavailable, not green** — the same distinction the `vendored-gguf` fuzz target carries under audit L1. **M4·0b's control-plane acceptance test runs everywhere and covers the decisions; this one covers the rebuild.** Two honest failures found on the way: a control-plane fixture cannot replay tokens, and a hand-built sampler snapshot is refused by `install`'s checksum recomputation — **both are the code being right**, and the test now uses the real `initial_checkpoint_bytes`. ‖ **No further residual: the coordinator decides every step of a recovery**, and the README may state the claim without qualification.

58. **✅ M4·3 — PACKAGING, DOCS, AND A README HELD TO GATE-TABLE DISCIPLINE (2026-08-24). ⚑ WRITING THE QUICKSTART FOUND THREE REAL DEFECTS THAT READING IT WOULD NOT HAVE.** ‖ **The README leads with the verification story, not tok/s**, and every number carries its bounds inline: the permitted TLC claim **with its eight bound values**, the DST's 10M steps / ≥1,000 seeds / six mutations 200/200, and the recovery claim **split by the reach of its evidence** — control plane **CI-covered on every push**, data-plane rebuild **a local engine-gated run whose CI status is *unavailable*, not *green***, in those words. WAN numbers are labelled `WAN/Tailscale` and never compared to LAN; wired-LAN and 70B are named as **targets, not measured**; the fuzz budget says **2.4 of 24 CPU-hours accumulating**. ‖ **The boundaries are stated as load-bearing, not as disclaimers:** the honest-worker assumption and what rests on it; that the formal guarantees cover a **protocol abstraction** and not the transport, FFI, or vendored engine; and the proportions, **measured with `wc -l` on this tree rather than quoted from memory** — ~21.7k hand-written Rust `src/`, ~12.2k tests, ~9.9k generated, ~400 C++ shim, against **~729k lines of vendored `llama.cpp`**, with which layer is verified stated beside each. *(The design authority's brief said ~18k/~676k; I used the measured figures and named the counting basis, per the standing honesty rule.)* ‖ **⚑ THE THREE DEFECTS, all found by EXECUTING the documented steps:** **(1)** pairing wrote the CA as **DER only**, and `curl --cacert` **refuses a DER file outright** (`error setting certificate file`) — a PEM is now written beside it, `0644`, because *a trust anchor nobody can read is not a trust anchor*; **(2)** the coordinator's `--dev` minted **its own throwaway CA**, unrelated to the one pairing had just created, so a client trusting the paired CA could not connect and **the pairing was meaningless to the coordinator** — `--pairing-dir` now loads the paired CA and issues this run's leaf from it; **(3)** the API certificate's only SAN was **`DNS:coordinator`** while clients dial **`127.0.0.1`**, so TLS failed with *"no alternative certificate subject name matches target host name"* — `issue_api` adds the addresses an endpoint is actually reached at. **Rule 19's oracle note on (3):** every transport test dials **by device name**, because that is how cluster *peers* connect; under that driver a DNS-only SAN and one with IP SANs are **indistinguishable**. The client API is the only caller that dials an address, and it had no test. It has one now. ‖ **The quickstart's status is stated precisely:** every command has been **executed as written**, the final call returns `200` with verification on and `401` without a token — and **the DoD itself is still unverified**, because M4 requires a *non-author* on a *fresh account* in under 30 minutes and that has not happened. **The 30-minute claim is therefore made nowhere in the README.** ‖ **Packaging:** `packaging/systemd/` (coordinator + worker units — `UMask=0077`, `StateDirectoryMode=0700`, `ProtectSystem=strict`, and the API token deliberately **not** in the unit file but in a 0600 override), `packaging/launchd/`, plus `docs/BUILD.md` (the two build modes and why a skipped engine-gated test is *unavailable* and not *passing*) and a real `LICENSE` with third-party attribution.

59. **✅ M4·4 — THE READ-ONLY DASHBOARD (2026-08-24).** ‖ **It is another CLIENT of the API surface, not a privileged path**: same router, same `ApiAuth`, same TLS material, same `Host`/`Origin` checks, and the auth check runs **before any state is read**. A status page reachable without the token would be a second, weaker front door to a user's session state — and it is exactly the endpoint that gets added "just for debugging" and forgotten. Verified live: `/dashboard` returns **401 without a token, 200 with**, over real TLS. ‖ **⚑ THE PROVENANCE DISCIPLINE SURVIVES THE LAST INCH.** P2·6's collector never invents a sensor — a platform that does not expose thermals reports `Unavailable`, and **container CI, the standing multi-node verifier, exposes neither thermal zones nor a battery**. The dashboard renders `Unavailable` as **`unknown`**, `BestEffort` with the qualifier *"estimated"*, and only `Measured` plainly, so a reader can always tell **a measurement from an estimate from an absence**. A stage with missing sensors is labelled **"unobserved… which is not the same as healthy"**, and there is deliberately **no cluster-average number to hide behind** — one hot stage in a two-stage cluster must not be averaged into comfort. **A page that rendered `Unavailable` as "Nominal" would undo the whole discipline at the point it finally reaches a human.** ‖ **No control actions in v1, and the page SAYS SO** rather than merely having no buttons: a page that happens to have none invites someone to add one; a page that states the constraint makes adding one a visible decision. Asserted structurally (no `<form>`, no `<button>`, no `method="post"`). ‖ **The oracle, named first (rule 19): a dashboard's natural test renders a HEALTHY cluster** — every sensor present, every number reassuring — and passes forever while saying nothing about the case an operator actually opens it for. So the four tests are **a degraded cluster** (its real numbers shown, per stage), **a stage whose sensors are unavailable**, **an absent session** (rendered as "no active session", not a session at position zero — an empty table would read as a healthy quiet cluster), and **an unauthorized request** (refused without leaking a watermark into the error body). ‖ **One test assertion was wrong and the page was right:** a blunt substring check for "healthy" flagged the very sentence that *denies* healthiness. Rewritten to assert on the structured readings — no unavailable sensor may carry a value — which is the property that actually matters. ‖ **`docs/FRESH-ACCOUNT-TEST.md`** written: the protocol for the one measurement the builders cannot take (fresh account, clean clone, stopwatch from opening the README to a `200`, every confusion recorded **verbatim** because the wording is the data, no contact with maintainers during the run). It states that **nothing in it tests the tester** — every confusion is a documentation defect — and that **repeating it with the same person is not a second measurement**.

60. **⛔ THE CLEAN CHECKOUT DID NOT COMPILE, AND THE GREENEST ORACLE IN THE PROJECT COULD NOT SEE IT (2026-08-24). ⚑ RULE 19 AIMED AT THE LOCAL TEST SUITE ITSELF.** ‖ **The defect.** `crates/hydra-engine-sys/src/lib.rs` ends with `pub use imp::{gguf_probe, Context, Model};`. In the real arm `gguf_probe` is a free function in `imp`; in the **stub** arm (`cfg(engine_unavailable)`) H6 placed it **inside `impl Model`**. The re-export therefore does not resolve whenever the engine is absent — which is every machine that does not have a **built and patched** `vendor/llama.cpp`, i.e. **every clean checkout and all of CI**. `error[E0432]: unresolved import 'imp::gguf_probe' — no 'gguf_probe' in 'imp'`. Introduced by `3df0424` (Wave 3, H6, 2026-08-23 15:28:05 −0500); the first red run is **32664500211 at 2026-08-23T20:28:12Z**, the same push. ‖ **What it cost: 28 hours and every run of two workflows.** `container-2node` — which §9 names **the standing multi-node verifier** — and `fuzz` were red on every attempt from that push onward, including the **scheduled Monday leg 32702306568**. Meanwhile PROJECT_STATE's header still read *"container-2node ✅ + DST-marathon ✅ green weekly"*. **The first scheduled weekly fuzz leg had never yet fired** (the cron was added 2026-08-23; both banked legs were push/dispatch), and the first one — **Tuesday 2026-08-25 04:15 UTC, the next day** — would have banked **zero** CPU-hours against the 24-hour M4 DoD while appearing to be a normal weekly leg. ‖ **⚑ WHY THIS IS RULE 19 AND NOT MERELY A TYPO — the oracle the agent trusts most is the one that could not see it.** `cargo test --workspace` on the dev box reported **390 / 0 / 7, clippy clean**, before and after the break, because **this machine always has the real engine and therefore compiles the real arm and only the real arm**. The stub arm had no oracle of its own: it was compiled solely as a side effect of `container-2node` and `fuzz`, where a compile break presents as *"the workflow is broken"* rather than as *"the project does not build"*, and nothing in the record distinguishes those. §6.0's engine row already said the engine suites *"only ever run on ONE machine"*; **the unstated converse is that the no-engine configuration also only ever runs somewhere else, and nobody owned it.** ‖ **The fix is a driver, per rule 19(b), not a note.** (a) **`HYDRA_FORCE_ENGINE_STUB=1`** in `build.rs` — an explicit, self-announcing switch that compiles the stub arm on demand, so the clean-checkout build is reproducible **locally in one command** instead of only observable in CI. It reproduced the exact `E0432` before the fix and passes after it — the failure was **produced**, not reasoned about. (b) **`.github/workflows/clean-checkout.yml`**, deliberately **not path-filtered** (any crate can break the stub arm) and with **no submodule init** — check + clippy `-D warnings` + the full test suite, exactly what `git clone` gives a new contributor. **It is also the first CI home the workspace's unit tests have ever had**: before it, all 390 ran only on the developer's laptop. ‖ **Two more defects the new switch immediately exposed, both invisible to the real arm.** (i) **`clippy::drop_non_drop`** on `shard_load.rs`'s deliberate `drop(ctx)` RSS discipline — the real `Context`/`Model` own C handles and implement `Drop`, the stub implemented neither, so a caller's drop was meaningful under one arm and a lint under the other. Fixed **at the asymmetry** (the stub now implements `Drop`) rather than by `#[allow]`, because a stub that differs structurally from what it stands in for is the same class as the bug above. (ii) **`single_worker.rs::data_plane_apply_token_echo_over_mtls` failed on a TLS `UnexpectedEof`** — its skip guard checked for the **model** while its own message promised *"the engine + GGUF"*, so on a box with the model and a stubbed engine the worker failed to load, dropped the connection, and the test panicked **accusing the transport layer for a build-configuration cause**. Per **rule 17** the class was enumerated rather than the instance patched: `shard_anchor.rs` carried the identical understatement and was fixed too; `real_split.rs` is pure-Rust and correctly needs no engine check. ‖ **Also closed in this seam:** the §8 **`fuzz.yml` push-path drift** (it still named `crates/hydra-worker/src/wire.rs`, deleted by M4·0) — and fixing it surfaced a **rule-17 register gap**: rule 17 says `crates/hydra-fuzz/Cargo.toml`'s dependency list **is** the parser register, and `hydra-wire` — which owns the parser `wire-body` actually drives, via `pub use hydra_wire as wire` — appeared there only **transitively through `hydra-worker`**. A parser reachable but not **named** is absent from a register whose whole purpose is to be read. Now a direct dependency. ‖ **Verification, both arms, sequentially (rule 18 — no overlapping cargo):** stub arm `clippy -D warnings` **EXIT=0** and **390 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored across 94 suites, EXIT=0**; real arm identical. ‖ **⚑ One honest note about the measurement layer (rule 12 turned on this session's own tooling).** The real-arm run first summed to **387 / 93 suites**, and the discrepancy was **my summariser, not the tree**: Metal's teardown printed into a result line (`test result: okggml_metal_free: deallocating`), so the `grep`/`awk` count silently dropped that suite. 387 + 3 = 390, 93 + 1 = 94. **A log parser is infrastructure and gets no presumption of correctness either** — the number was re-derived before it was published rather than after it was doubted.

61. **⛔ THE PROJECT'S "CLIPPY CLEAN" WAS MEASURED ON A CLIPPY FIVE VERSIONS OLD — AND NO CI JOB HAD EVER RUN CLIPPY AT ALL (2026-08-24). ⚑ FOUND BY THE §7.60 WORKFLOW ON ITS FIRST RUN, AGAINST ITS OWN AUTHOR.** ‖ `clean-checkout`'s first run (**32793964366**) **passed `cargo check`** — the §7.60 defect was genuinely fixed — and then **failed `cargo clippy -D warnings`** on a lint this machine cannot produce: `clippy::unnecessary_min_or_max`. **Cause: the agent's local toolchain is `rustc 1.93.1 (2026-02-11)`; CI's `stable` is `rustc 1.98.0 (2026-08-18)`.** Every "clippy clean, zero warnings" line in this record — including M4·3's, M4·4's and this session's own — was true **only against a clippy roughly six months stale**, and nothing in the record said so. **The gap was invisible because no workflow ran clippy**: `container-2node`, `fuzz`, `tlc` and `marathon` never did, so CI's clippy had never once been consulted about this tree. ‖ **Rule 24 is the governing rule and it applies to the agent's own numbers first:** *a number arriving from any source is re-measured before it is published, and the basis is stated with it.* "Clippy clean" is such a number, and its **basis is the toolchain version** — which had never been stated. ‖ **The oracle gap was closed by RUNNING CI's clippy, not by reasoning about it (rule 12).** `rustup toolchain install 1.98.0` as a **named, non-default** toolchain — deliberately not `rustup update`, so the 390/0/7 baseline stays measured on the toolchain it was measured on — then `cargo +1.98.0 clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` on **both** arms. Iterating one lint per CI round-trip was rejected as an oracle: the first run without `-D warnings` enumerated **all** remaining findings at once. ‖ **Three real findings, none of them cosmetic.** (a) `recovery.rs:190` — `0_usize.max(state.last_commit_id as usize)` where `last_commit_id` is a **`u64`**, so the `max` was a true no-op **that read as a guard against a negative count while guarding nothing**. The hazard it gestured at is real and was never addressed: **`u64 as usize` truncates silently on a 32-bit target**. Replaced with `usize::try_from(..).unwrap_or(usize::MAX)` — the project's standing answer to an unproven narrowing (Wave 1c applied it to every network-derived position), and this value is read **off the disk**, which rule 17 counts as untrusted input for exactly the same reason. **§7.31's shape in an arithmetic expression: a construct whose form promised more than it delivered, thereby suppressing the question of where the narrowing was actually checked.** (b) + (c) `explicit_counter_loop` in `pair.rs::time_generation_pipeline` and in the **two golden-reference generators** (`three_node.rs`, `three_node_recovery.rs`): a `mut pos` counter that was always `prompt.len() + step`. Rewritten as a derived expression — **in a golden generator an invariant somebody has to maintain is one that can be broken**, and a single early `continue` would silently desynchronise the position from the step while the test still reported a byte-identical-looking comparison. ‖ **⚑ AND ONE DEFECT IN THIS SESSION'S OWN MEASUREMENT LAYER, which is why the counts in §7.60 wobbled.** `ggml`'s Metal teardown writes to **stderr** while `libtest` writes results to **stdout**; with `2>&1` they interleave and **corrupt the result line itself** — the log literally contains `test result: ggml_metal_free: deallocating`, a suite whose verdict is **unreadable**. That is how a run summed to `93 suites / 387 passed` when 94 started and `EXIT=0`. **The fix is to stop merging the streams** (`> out 2> err`), after which the same tree reports `94 / 390 / 0 / 7` with **zero** mangled lines. **The numbers this project quotes into gate tables come out of these logs**, so a parser that can be silently defeated by a C library's teardown is verification infrastructure under rule 12 — and it was defeating itself in the same session that wrote rule 12 into a commit message. **Every future full-suite receipt separates the streams and reports the mangled-line count as part of the result.** ‖ **Verification after the three fixes — the full matrix, sequentially (rule 18):** `cargo +1.98.0 clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` **EXIT=0 on the stub arm AND EXIT=0 on the real arm**; full suite **94 suites / 390 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored, EXIT=0, 0 mangled lines** on **each** arm, streams separated. Rule-14 anchors green within the real-arm run.

62. **⛔ A FUZZ TARGET REPORTED `verdict=GREEN` FOR A PARSER IT HAS NEVER ONCE CALLED (2026-08-24). ⚑ THE RECORD SAID "UNAVAILABLE, NEVER GREEN"; THE LOG SAID GREEN; RULE 16 QUOTES THE LOG.** ‖ **The defect.** `Target::VendoredGguf` exists to drive `gguf_init_from_file` **inside llama.cpp** — H6's whole point being that the 24-CPU-hour budget had been protecting the *offline splitter's* Rust reader while the worker loads through the vendored C++ one. Its body is gated `if hydra_engine_sys::ENGINE_AVAILABLE && hardened_accepts`. **Audit L1 means CI never builds the real engine**, so in CI the arm evaluates the **hardened Rust parser** — already covered by the `gguf` target — and then does **nothing**. The driver computed each target's verdict as `crashes.is_empty()`, and **a no-op never crashes**, so the log printed: `target=vendored-gguf seed=1000003 iterations=127886336 cpu_seconds=112.5 crashes=0 verdict=GREEN`. ‖ **Scale, measured on run 32795673202 (the first leg ever to include the target): 868 410 368 iterations and 900.0 CPU-seconds — 0.250 of the leg's 2.000 CPU-hours — spent on a parser that was never invoked**, and counted toward a DoD that reads *"GGUF parser fuzzed for 24 CPU-hours without crashes"*. For scale in the other direction: with the engine actually linked the same target manages ~21 k iterations/second, because materialising a file and crossing into C++ is real work. **~100 M iterations of nothing per shard is what the emptiness looks like as a number.** ‖ **⚑ THE PART THAT MATTERS MOST — THE RECORD WAS RIGHT AND THE CODE WAS NOT.** §0(b) and the Wave-3 directive both state, correctly and repeatedly, that `vendored-gguf`'s **CI status is "unavailable, never green"**. **Nobody had checked whether the program agreed.** Rule 16 says a CI outcome enters this record *only* as a quoted `verdict=` line — so the discipline that exists to stop unearned claims would have **transcribed this one faithfully**, and a future session banking the leg per rule 16 would have written GREEN into a receipt in perfect procedural compliance. **A prose caveat cannot protect a machine-readable claim: the caveat lives in one file and the verdict travels in every log.** This is rule 12 one layer deeper than it has been applied before — not "the classifier may be wrong", but *"the thing being classified may be lying, and the classifier has no way to know"*. ‖ **§7.31's standing lesson, now in its third layer.** *A test whose name promises more than its assertion is worse than no test, because it terminates inquiry* — first found in test names (§7.31), then in a mechanism name (§7.35), now in a **verdict token**. And rule 19's definition applies exactly: an oracle that cannot produce the failure it guards. `vendored-gguf` in CI could not have failed. ‖ **The fix, in three places, because one would have left the class open.** (a) **`Target::unavailable_reason()`** — a target that cannot do its job names *why*, and the driver prints `verdict=UNAVAILABLE reason="engine not linked (audit L1): gguf_init_from_file is never invoked, so this target proves nothing"`. **UNAVAILABLE is not GREEN and cannot be mistaken for it by grep or by eye.** (b) **Unavailable targets are not run and take no budget** — the runnable targets now split the whole allocation, so a leg's `cpu_seconds` is time spent on parsers that were actually driven, and the summary line carries `unavailable=N` so it **states its own gap instead of omitting it**. (c) **The classify step refuses two new things:** a **required** target reporting UNAVAILABLE (it was named but not driven — "reported" and "ran" were previously the same claim to it), and `vendored-gguf` reporting **GREEN in CI**, which now stops the run because it means either L1 is fixed — promote the target to required — or the target is again claiming an unearned green. ‖ **Verified by producing both outcomes (rule 19):** stub arm → `target=vendored-gguf … iterations=0 cpu_seconds=0.0 verdict=UNAVAILABLE`, `FUZZ SUMMARY targets=7 unavailable=1`; real arm → `target=vendored-gguf … iterations=21504 … verdict=GREEN`, `FUZZ SUMMARY targets=8 unavailable=0`. ‖ **Accounting consequence, applied in the receipts:** leg 3 banks **1.750 CPU-hours, not 2.000**, and the cumulative total is **4.150 / 24** — legs 1 and 2 need no adjustment, both having run `targets=3` before this target existed. **And a cadence correction found alongside it: no SCHEDULED fuzz leg has ever run.** The cron was added 2026-08-23; every leg to date is dispatch or push. "~10 more weeks" is really "~10 more legs", and those are not the same thing until a scheduled leg has been observed to complete.

63. **✅ THE SUBMODULE BUMP IS VALIDATED AND DELIBERATELY NOT APPLIED — BOTH BINDING CONDITIONS DISCHARGED (2026-08-25). ⚑ AND THE SWEEP FOUND SOMETHING THAT IS NOT ABOUT THE BUMP AT ALL.** ‖ Candidate `f280b269` (2026-08-25) vs pin `13f2b28b` (2026-07-11): **642 commits, six weeks**. Receipt: `verification/ci-results/submodule-bump-2026-08-25.md`; raw sweep log `spike-sweep-bump-f280b269.log`. **All validation ran in an isolated `git worktree`; `vendor/llama.cpp` was never touched** — its patched working tree is what every green engine result in this session rests on, and §0(b) warns it must not be cleaned. ‖ **Condition (ii) — the diff review.** Both per-arch graph builders (`models/{llama,qwen2}.cpp`), where most of the ~47-line patch lives, have **zero** upstream commits. Strict `git apply` fails on **context drift only** (`include/llama.h:319`, `llama-model.cpp:2309`); 3-way applies all seven cleanly, and re-extracting the diff afterwards gives **added/removed lines identical to the canonical patch**. **The finding the condition exists to catch:** `llama_model_params` lost `use_mmap`/`use_direct_io`/`use_mlock` and gained `enum llama_load_mode load_mode` + `bool load_mtp`. **The shim is insulated** — it only calls `*_default_params()` and assigns named fields, verified by grep — **but the patch's own hunk carried `/*.use_mmap =*/ true,` as context, which is precisely why strict apply failed there. A clean 3-way would have hidden it.** Because both defaults functions are **positional aggregate initialisers with comment labels**, where a wrong ordinal silently assigns to the wrong member, field order was audited member-by-member post-merge: `llama_model_params` **18/18 aligned**, `llama_context_params` aligned (`/*.sampler =*/` vs field `samplers` is an **upstream label typo**, not a misalignment). All three semantic hook sites — `build_inp_embd` injection, the `load_tensors` window filter, `done_getting_tensors(partial)` — landed in unchanged context. **The patched candidate builds: EXIT=0, zero errors.** ‖ **Condition (i) — the full 15-combination sweep, CPU (`-ngl 0`, the DoD backend): 15/15 `=== M-1 DoD: PASS ===`.** Check A `max_abs=0.000e+00` ×15; **Check C (the DoD test) `max_abs=0.000e+00` ×15 — bit-exact, argmax identical, top10=10/10 everywhere**; Check D exact ×15; FP16 2.832e-02…5.664e-02 argmax-stable; int8 6.421e-02…2.298e+00, consistent with §7.11's standing FORBIDDEN ruling. **⚑ The three prompts are NAMED in the receipt because the original sweep's were not** — `FINDINGS.md` says "× 3 prompts" and never records which, so a sweep BLUEPRINT §1.2 makes **binding on every bump** could not be re-*run*, only re-*performed with different inputs*. ‖ **⚑⚑ THE FINDING THAT IS NOT ABOUT THE BUMP.** The Metal spot-check (`-ngl 99`, k=12) **fails Check D** on prompt P3 (`def fibonacci(n):`, 4 tokens, truncate at pos 2) with `max_abs=4.059e-04` — **and fails byte-identically on the current pin**. **That control is the point: the bump introduces no regression.** What it does establish, in three parts: (1) **on Metal, KV truncate + replay is not bit-exact** for that case — deterministic, stable across runs and engine versions, while **all 15 CPU combinations are exact**; (2) **`FINDINGS.md`'s Metal close-out claims "KV truncate+replay: exact" across the full 15-combination Metal sweep, and that claim does not hold for P3** — whether the original sweep used prompts where it happens to hold or simply overstated **cannot be determined, because the prompts were never recorded**; (3) **⛔ every one of the project's 31 engine-test configurations sets `n_gpu_layers: 0`** — the rule-14 bit-exact anchors, the shard anchor, `d1_recovery`, three-node recovery and the M3 calibration receipt are **CPU-ONLY evidence, and Metal is exercised by nothing in the suite**. So a non-exactness on the exact operation teacher-forced recovery is built from is **invisible to every gate the project has**. **Stated precisely and no wider:** 4.059e-04 sits well inside the 1e-3 tolerance and did not move the argmax; this is **not** a demonstrated token divergence. It is a demonstrated non-exactness **on the backend the product will actually run on**, underneath claims phrased as *byte-identical* — which are true of the evidence that exists, and the evidence is CPU. Rule 19 in its purest form: the oracle cannot produce the failure because it never runs on the backend where it occurs. ‖ **Why the pin did NOT move.** §8 sequences the bump **with L1's fork pin so the patch is re-ported once**; the patch is still an **uncommitted working-tree modification**, so bumping today would reproduce L1 at a newer SHA and force a **second** re-port when the fork lands — the exact outcome the sequencing exists to prevent. **Creating `PavanManchikatla/llama.cpp` is an owner action, stated as such in §8.** The agent-side work is complete and banked; the remaining step is mechanical.

64. **✅ THE V1-BLOCKER IS CLOSED — AND CLOSING IT FALSIFIED THE §8 ROW THAT DESCRIBED IT (2026-08-25). ⚑ THE "ONE-LINE DELETION" WAS LOAD-BEARING FOR THE PROJECT'S OWN CI.** ‖ **`ACCEPT_LEGACY_ZERO_COMPLETION_HASH` is deleted, not set `false`** — its own §8 row called itself *"its deletion notice"*, and its unblocking condition (*"no peer in the cluster predates H2"*) is satisfied now that M4·3 pins a single build and no fleet is deployed. Evidence is now compared exactly, with **no special values**. ‖ **⚑ THE ROW SAID IT WAS A SINGLE NAMED CONSTANT AND A ONE-LINE CHANGE. IT WAS NOT, AND THAT GAP IS THE FINDING.** Deleting the allowance turned three things red: **(a)** `commit_then_finalize_reaches_active_final` — **the canonical "activation succeeds" test** — was finalizing with `complete_record_hash: [0u8; 32]`, so **the project's happy-path activation test had never once supplied genuine completion evidence and would have gone on passing had the evidence check been broken outright**; swept per rule 17, `recovery.rs` carried the identical shape with the comment *"ACTIVE_FINAL with evidence"* sitting directly above a line passing zeros. **(b)** `multiconn.rs::a_second_connection_is_served_while_the_first_is_held_open` failed — not a fixture problem, it traced to the encoder. **(c)** **`hydra_wire::encode_finalize_activation` itself wrote `[0; 32]`.** The shipping coordinator (`hydra_coordinator::driver`) was already on the evidence-carrying encoder — **but `hydra-worker::pair` and the three demo/CI binaries `hydra-wan`, `hydra-2node-ci`, `hydra-3node-kill` were not**, and `hydra-2node-ci` is precisely what `container-2node` runs. **A flip-to-false would have turned the standing multi-node verifier RED for a reason having nothing to do with rollout compatibility — five hours after this same session finished restoring it from exactly that kind of failure (§7.60).** ‖ **This is rule 22 earning its keep in a direction it had not been used before.** Rule 22 says *verify an owed item's SITE before its substance*; the site here was real and correctly named. What was wrong was the row's estimate of its own **blast radius** — *"a single named constant… a one-line change with a compiler-visible effect"* described the deletion accurately and the consequences not at all. **A residual can name its location correctly and still mislead about its cost**, and the only thing that surfaced it was doing the work and reading what broke. ‖ **Fixed at the encoder, not at its thirteen call sites:** `encode_finalize_activation` now delegates to `encode_finalize_activation_with_evidence` with the tuple's own hash, so **no caller can emit a finalize the stage will refuse for want of evidence**; callers naming a *specific* durable `ACTIVATION_COMPLETE` record still use the explicit encoder. Choosing the encoder over the call sites means the class is closed rather than its instances. ‖ **The oracle that could not previously exist (rule 19):** `an_all_zero_completion_hash_is_refused_now_that_the_rollout_allowance_is_gone`. **While the constant stood, the exact case H2 was about was the one case the suite was structurally unable to refuse** — an oracle blinded not by an oversight but by a *deliberate allowance*, which is a shape the register had not recorded before. ‖ **Verified both arms: clippy 1.98.0 `-D warnings` EXIT=0; 94 suites / 392 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored, EXIT=0, 0 mangled lines, rule-14 anchors green within the real-arm run.**

65. **✅🔶 THE GPU GATE ROW — MEASURED. EVERY BYTE-IDENTITY ASSERTION THAT COULD RUN ON METAL PASSED, AND THE TWO THINGS THAT DID NOT RUN ARE NOT CORRECTNESS FINDINGS (2026-08-25, owner Decision 2).** ‖ The owner ruled the CPU-only evidence base a **gate row**, not an accepted limitation: *re-run the engine suite at `n_gpu_layers: 99`; if the byte-identity assertions survive the claim strengthens and the row closes; if not, the recovery claim gets a per-backend qualifier before v1 ships.* Receipt: `verification/ci-results/gpu-backend-2026-08-25.md`; raw table `gpu-sweep-ngl99-2026-08-25.txt`. ‖ **The lever first, because it is infrastructure (rule 12).** All 31 configurations hard-code `n_gpu_layers: 0`; editing 31 literals would have to be undone, and **a lever that must be reverted is one a future session cannot re-pull**, so `HYDRA_TEST_NGL` intercepts at the single `hydra-engine-sys` load boundary — covering every caller including binaries, leaving **0 (the DoD backend) as the default**, opt-in by explicit env var and self-announcing, the same contract as `HYDRA_FORCE_ENGINE_STUB`. **Verified before anything was concluded from it:** `load_tensors: MTL0_Mapped model buffer size` appears **only** at 99, so layers demonstrably moved; and both anomalies below are **absent at `ngl=0`** on the same suites (0 aborts, 0 OOM in the control). ‖ **RESULT: 88 passed / 5 failed / 5 ignored across 30 engine-gated suites.** **Every byte-identity and argmax assertion that ran to completion PASSED on Metal** — `split_vs_unsplit_f32_bit_exact_through_crate_api`, **both rule-14 anchors**, `direct_worker_to_worker_fwd_is_bit_exact`, `two_worker_anchor_is_bit_exact_with_shard_loaded_weights`, `shard_loaded_weights_are_bit_exact_with_the_unsplit_model`, `chunked_prefill_is_bit_exact_with_unchunked_prefill`, `d1_recovery_three_kill_windows_are_byte_identical_to_an_uninterrupted_seeded_run`, `three_node_kill_middle_s2_…_byte_identical`, `three_node_chained_direct_fwd_is_byte_identical_to_unsplit_greedy`. **⚑ NOT ONE OF THE 5 FAILURES IS AN EQUALITY ASSERTION** — every one died on `peer closed connection` or `EngineError code 5`, each log carrying **8–17 GPU out-of-memory messages**. ‖ **RESIDUAL 1 — a Metal TEARDOWN abort, a real defect that is not a correctness defect.** `ggml/src/ggml-metal/ggml-metal-device.m:622: GGML_ASSERT([rsets->data count] == 0)` fires in **9 of 30 suites AFTER they report `test result: ok`** — `chunked_prefill_is_bit_exact_with_unchunked_prefill ... ok`, then `5 passed; 0 failed`, *then* SIGABRT. **The assertions have already been evaluated and reported when it fires.** Consequence stated plainly: **a Metal-backed worker would abort on shutdown** — operational, not computational; nothing it touches produced a wrong value. It is upstream code, and the bump candidate `f280b269` carries heavy Metal churn (its tip commit is a Metal change), so **whether the bump fixes it is worth checking when the fork lands — not checked here**, because this receipt reports the pinned engine. ‖ **RESIDUAL 2 — GPU OOM on this 8 GB M2, which is the row-1 class of limit.** `recommendedMaxWorkingSetSize = 5726.63 MB`, and the three failing suites each run **two or three workers all mapping the full model into that working set**: `d1_two_stage` (2 failures, 16 OOM), `three_node_recovery` (2, 17), `sampler_pipeline` (1, 8 — `seeded_sampling_is_reproducible_across_two_full_runs` runs two full pipelines). **All three pass on CPU; the models simply fit.** **Owed, not waived** — the multi-worker recovery suites stay unverified on Metal until a larger GPU runs them. ‖ **WHAT THE ROW NOW ESTABLISHES:** the split/recovery machinery is **bit-exact on Metal wherever it could be measured**, so the byte-identity claim is materially stronger than it was that morning, when it rested on 31 configurations that all pinned the CPU. **WHAT IT DOES NOT:** the multi-worker recovery suites on Metal; and it does **not** retire §7.63's Check D — Metal's KV truncate+replay is still not exact for a 4-token prompt truncated at position 2 (`4.059e-04`, deterministic, identical on pinned and candidate). **That case is narrower than anything the recovery suites exercise, and the recovery suites that ran were byte-identical — but it is not thereby excluded**, which is why `spike/FINDINGS.md` was corrected in the same commit rather than left standing. **And nothing here says anything about CUDA**, which no machine in this project has ever run. ‖ **⚑ THE FINDINGS.md CORRECTION WAS MADE EVEN THOUGH WE LANDED IN THE "SURVIVE" BRANCH.** The owner attached it to the *failure* branch; the claim *"KV truncate+replay: exact"* is nonetheless **something I measured to be false**, and a known-false claim does not become acceptable because the ruling that would have forced its correction was not triggered. It now reads *not exact*, with the reason the overstatement **cannot be adjudicated** — the original sweep's three prompts were never recorded (§8).

66. **✅✅ AUDIT L1 IS CLOSED — THE ENGINE IS A PINNED FORK, AND A FRESH CLONE PROVED IT RATHER THAN A CLAIM (2026-08-25).** ‖ `vendor/llama.cpp` now points at **`PavanManchikatla/llama.cpp`**, branch **`hydra-layer-window-f280b26`**, pinned at **`c00bcebf79cbf95be22aa711d485a287914672c5`** — exactly **one commit** (7 files, +130/−6) on upstream **`f280b269`**. Receipt: `verification/ci-results/L1-closed-2026-08-25.md`. ‖ **Branched off `f280b269`, NOT off the fork's own master (`d222767c`, newer).** `f280b269` is the base both binding conditions were discharged against last session; **branching off anything newer would have silently re-opened both**, turning a mechanical landing back into a re-validation. ‖ **Re-verified on the exact tree pushed rather than trusting last session's record of it (rule 12):** the patch applies 3-way cleanly to all seven files, and `llama_model_default_params()`'s positional initializer audits **18/18** — not ceremony, because upstream swapped `use_mmap`/`use_direct_io`/`use_mlock` for the `load_mode` enum and added `load_mtp` *inside* this bump's range, and a mis-ordered field in a positional initializer assigns **silently**. Before discarding the working tree, its diff was confirmed **byte-identical** to the canonical patch, so nothing was lost in transition. ‖ **⚑ THE ORACLE L1 NEVER HAD — a clone from nothing, every step run:** `git clone` → `vendor/llama.cpp` has **0 entries** (what a newcomer gets) → `git submodule update --init` → checked out `c00bcebf` → **`git status` CLEAN** → the layer-window symbols are present with **nothing applied by hand** → `cmake --build --target llama` **EXIT=0** → `cargo test -p hydra-engine-sys` **EXIT=0** with `split_vs_unsplit_f32_bit_exact_through_crate_api ... ok` and **ZERO `"building a stub"` messages**. **That zero is the whole result:** a clean clone links the **real** engine and reproduces the bit-exactness anchor, where before today it would have built stock upstream, been refused by `build.rs` as unpatched, and degraded to the stub. ‖ **The M−1 sweep re-run against the submodule AS PINNED** (not a worktree, not a scratch copy): **15/15 `M-1 DoD: PASS`**, Check A / Check C / Check D all `max_abs=0.000e+00` **15/15**, top10=10/10 on every combination, EXIT=0. **The three prompts are named in the receipt**, because the original sweep's never were. ‖ **⚑ CLOSING L1 MADE ITS OWN GUARD VACUOUS, AND THAT IS RULE 25 IN THE PLACE IT WOULD HURT MOST.** `patch_integrity.rs` asserted that every file **modified in the working tree** appeared in the patch file — correct while the patch *was* working-tree state.
