The moment the fork landed the tree went clean, the check found nothing to iterate, took its `modified.is_empty()` early return, and **reported `ok` while asserting nothing.** A guard that cannot fail, guarding the very finding it was written for. **Rewritten to the fork's real invariant:** *the pin is exactly one commit on top of its base, and that commit's diff IS the committed patch* — which catches a second commit on the branch, a hand-edit of the pinned tree, and patch-file drift, **none of which the old form could see**. **Verified to discriminate (rule 19):** perturbing one added line in the patch file turns it red naming the SHA; restoring it turns it green. ‖ **Verified both arms on the new pin: 94 suites / 392 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored, EXIT=0, 0 mangled; clippy `-D warnings` under `rustc 1.98.0` EXIT=0 on each.** ‖ **What L1's closure does NOT buy, recorded because it will be tempting to think otherwise:** the maintenance cost is unchanged in substance — **every upstream sync still requires re-porting the patch and re-running the sweep before a new SHA may be pinned** — and the project now **owns its engine's security updates**, so an upstream CVE fix does not reach Hydra until somebody performs that sync. **The fork converts a silent liability into a scheduled one; it does not remove it.**

67. **⛔→⏸️ THE METAL TEARDOWN ABORT SURVIVES THE BUMP — AND NO UPSTREAM REPORT WAS DRAFTED, BECAUSE ITS PREMISE DOES NOT HOLD (2026-08-25).** ‖ Re-run on the new pin, `HYDRA_TEST_NGL=99`: `local_pair` reports **`3 passed; 0 failed`** — **both bit-exact anchors green on Metal at the new pin**, so §7.65's GPU result carries over — and *then* aborts. The assert **moved from line 622 to 952** (the file changed substantially) and still fires. Receipt: `verification/ci-results/metal-teardown-2026-08-25.md`. ‖ **The duplicate search found two closed issues, and the way they closed is the finding.** [#19137](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/19137) — the exact assert — closed **COMPLETED**. [#22593](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/22593) — a precise root-cause report — closed **NOT_PLANNED by the stale bot after 14 days**, despite **two independent reproductions in its own comments**. **This is not §7.48's situation:** there the abort had been reported four times and *fixed*, so nothing was filed; here a correct diagnosis was auto-closed for inactivity while the symptom survived. That asymmetry looked exactly like something worth reporting. ‖ **⚑ AND THEN RULE 20 STOPPED IT.** *The authoritative text of an external finding is the auditor's own; a gloss is a pointer, never a substitute* — so #22593's root cause was checked **against our tree** instead of repeated. Its claim was that `ggml_metal_device_rsets_rm` is *"defined and never called."* **At `f280b269` that is FALSE:** `rsets_add` is called at `:2014` and `:2111` (two buffer constructors) and `rsets_rm` at `:2117` (`ggml_metal_buffer_free`). **Add and remove ARE paired, by buffer lifetime.** So the assert does not mean "rm is never called" — it means **a Metal buffer outlived `ggml_metal_device_free`**, which is a leak-or-ordering question whose answer is not necessarily upstream's. ‖ **The control points at us.** Suites that load a model onto the GPU **and drop `Model`/`Context` explicitly do not abort** — `bit_exact` and `shard_load` both allocate GPU buffers on Metal and exit cleanly. The aborting suites are those that build workers holding models and let the process exit with them alive. **Clean teardown demonstrably works at this pin.** ‖ **Therefore nothing was drafted, per rule 8b:** *permission to do X is permission to do X-as-described*, and an approval to report an engine defect is **not** approval to report a defect that may be the reporter's own. §7.48's discipline is to **report back rather than proceed**. **Owed:** first exclude Hydra-side lifetime — find which `Model`/`Context` instances are alive at process exit in the aborting suites and whether dropping them removes the abort (worth fixing regardless, since a Metal worker aborting at shutdown is real either way); **only if a leak-free case still aborts** does a filing become warranted, and it would be a **reopen request on #22593 with a corrected root cause**, not a new duplicate issue.

69. **✅ RATIFIED (design authority, 2026-09-01 — the held 2026-08-26 directive, item 1): the bump landing on `f280b269`; audit L1's closure by fresh-clone demonstration; the base selection (branch off `f280b269`, not the fork's newer master); the fresh-clone oracle; the re-run 15/15 sweep on the pinned tree; the vacuous-guard repair of `patch_integrity` (§7.66); the CI path-filter fix; and the NOT-drafted Metal report (rule 8b applied correctly).** All accepted as evidenced. **Two patterns recorded from it, at the authority's direction:** **(a) CLOSING A FINDING CAN INVALIDATE THE ORACLE WRITTEN TO GUARD IT — when a defect's precondition disappears, re-derive what its guard now asserts.** `patch_integrity` asserted over *working-tree modifications of the submodule*; the moment L1 closed the tree went clean, the check had nothing to iterate, and it reported `ok` while asserting nothing. Rule 25's shape inside a guard, caused not by a bug in the guard but by the world it described ceasing to exist. Binding: when an item closes, every test that names it is re-read for what it can still see. **(b) AN APPROVAL TO REPORT AN EXTERNAL DEFECT LAPSES IF THE DEFECT MAY BE OURS — rule 8b applied to ATTRIBUTION, not only to facts.** The Metal-abort filing was approved as "report the engine defect"; checking #22593's root cause against the pinned tree showed the assert means *a buffer outlived device free*, and the control (suites that drop `Model`/`Context` exit cleanly) pointed at Hydra. Permission to report *their* bug is not permission to report *ours* under their name; the approval lapsed and the decision returned to the owner — which is §7.48's discipline generalised from "is it already fixed?" to "is it even theirs?".

70. **⏸️ THE HELD 2026-08-26 DIRECTIVE WAS ISSUED ON 2026-09-01 as part of the STATE-OF-REPO ruling** (items 3–4 of that ruling), after the state-of-repo report established it had never been received. Item 3 is §7.69 above; item 4 (Metal-abort scoping, `vendored-gguf` wiring) is executed next, in order, and recorded as §7.71 (Metal abort) and §7.72 (`vendored-gguf`) when each lands.

| **Coverage-guided fuzzing (`cargo-fuzz`) over the same entry points (§7.27).** The M4·1 arm is deterministic and structure-aware, not coverage-guided: it trades the search's ability to *learn* which mutations reach new code for `(seed, iteration)` reproduction and a stable-Rust CI arm. Strictly additive upgrade; needs nightly in CI | agent | post-audit |
| **Sandboxed GGUF loader (report Addendum 2 §D1, "ideally").** Not attempted in v1 — the engine loads in-process. The parser is now hardened and fuzzed, which is the other half of §D1's fix; the sandbox is not | design | v2 |
| **🔶 HTTP surface — the REQUEST-SIZE half is CLOSED 2026-08-25 (§7.64 seam); RATE LIMITING remains open and is proposed ACCEPT-for-v1.** `MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES = 1 MiB`, applied **router-wide** so a later route inherits it, with the oracle `a_body_over_the_limit_is_refused_before_it_is_parsed` — **verified to discriminate**: with the layer removed a 1 MiB body returns **200**, so `axum`'s 2 MiB `DefaultBodyLimit` would never have caught it and the §8 concern was real for bodies between 1 and 2 MiB. **The point of stating the constant is rule 24's:** the limit had a value, but it lived in a dependency's changelog where a version bump could move it unreviewed. **Still open: no rate limiting** — see `verification/M4-PRERELEASE-TRIAGE.md` for the proposed release-notes line. *(Original:)* API auth is the only control on the OpenAI endpoint; a caller holding a valid token can submit an arbitrarily large body | agent | **rate-limit half: owner call at the gate** |

The pipeline is model-agnostic (proven on 0.5B; the split/recovery/sampler machinery is size-independent). **P2·10b note (2026-08-22): the per-worker memory ceiling is retired** (sharded weights, −50.0 % measured per worker), so 70B is no longer bounded by "every worker loads 40 GB" — it is bounded by the **aggregate** RAM of the cluster across enough stages. **Still hardware-contingent and still not attempted**: ≈40 GB aggregate needs real machines the project does not have (VMs dead Aug-5), and no number here may be claimed without a run | agent+owner (hardware) | real hardware |

Not attempted (no download, no OOM chase). Re-attempt on real M2 hardware or once weights are sharded (a later engine change). **⚡ CAVEAT REMOVED 2026-08-22 (P2·10b): weights ARE sharded now.** `hydra_model_load_shard` loads only a stage's assigned layers from a manifest-verified per-stage shard GGUF — **measured 1202.09 MiB full-load → 601.04 MiB per shard on the dev GGUF (−50.0 % per worker)**, bit-exact (rule-14 anchor green with shard-loaded weights). The arithmetic that aborted this stretch (4.5 GB × 2 = 9 GB > 8 GB) **no longer describes the system**: a 2-stage 7B Q4 split is ≈ 2.25 GB × 2 ≈ 4.5 GB of weights, which **fits the 8 GB box on paper**. **This is now a RE-MEASURE, not a known-impossible** — and it stays an owed item, not a claim: the paper arithmetic ignores KV cache, activations, and the `output`/`token_embd` globals that are not split evenly, so it must be *measured* before anything is asserted. **Do not report 7B as feasible until a real run says so** | agent | re-measure (no longer hardware-blocked in principle) |

**⛔ AUDIT RESIDUALS — ACCEPTED AS OWED, NOT CLOSED (Wave 1d, ratified 2026-08-23). Each carries its unblocking condition, because "owed" without one decays into "forgotten".**

| Residual | Why it is not closed now | **Unblocking condition** |
|---|---|---|
| **H2 residual — `completion_id` / `complete_record_hash` are still dropped at decode.** The epoch and attempt checks are in (§7.38), so a stale finalize is refused; what is still missing is verifying the *evidence* the frame carries. | There is nothing to compare them **against**. The check is "this `completion_id` matches the coordinator's durable `ACTIVATION_COMPLETE` record" — and the stage does not hold that record; the coordinator does. Implementing it stage-side would mean inventing a second source of truth for completion, which is the defect H4 just closed in another guise. | **The coordinator driver exists and its durable `ACTIVATION_COMPLETE` is readable at the point `FINALIZE_ACTIVATION` is emitted.** Then: carry `completion_id` + `complete_record_hash` through `wire::decode` into `Msg::FinalizeActivation`, and have the stage refuse a finalize whose evidence does not match the one it was told to expect at `COMMIT_ACTIVATION` time. Blocked on M4·2/3/4-era coordinator work, not on a decision. |
| **M9 residual — a duplicate OLDER than the last position is refused (`ERR_GAP`) rather than answered.** The retransmit R1 actually produces (the frame still in flight) is answered from cache; an older one is not. | Answering it needs either **recomputation** — which R2 forbids outright ("in-shard recomputation never authorized") — or a **per-position reply cache**, which is unbounded in exactly the shape H20 flags for `sampled_ring`. **The limit is the right v1 answer** (ruled 2026-08-23): a bounded refusal beats an unbounded cache, and the coordinator's resume rule (§2.3d) already knows what to do with `ERR_GAP` + a frontier. | **Only if a real workload shows legitimate older-position retries** — i.e. R1 is observed retransmitting beyond the in-flight window. Then the answer is a **bounded** ring keyed to the R3′ retention window (which already exists and is already bounded), never an unbounded map. Until then this is a documented v1 boundary, not a defect. |

**✅✅ V1-BLOCKER CLOSED 2026-08-25 (§7.64) — `ACCEPT_LEGACY_ZERO_COMPLETION_HASH` IS DELETED.** Evidence is compared exactly, with no special values. **⚑ It was NOT the one-line change this row advertised:** the allowance was load-bearing for `hydra_wire::encode_finalize_activation` itself and therefore for `hydra-worker::pair` and the three demo/CI binaries — including `hydra-2node-ci`, which **`container-2node` runs**, so a flip-to-false would have turned the standing multi-node verifier red. Fixed at the encoder (it now delegates to the evidence-carrying one), and it exposed that the canonical *"activation succeeds"* test had **never supplied real evidence**. New oracle: `an_all_zero_completion_hash_is_refused_now_that_the_rollout_allowance_is_gone`. *(Superseded text:)* **⛔⛔ V1-BLOCKING — DELETE THE COMPLETION-EVIDENCE ROLLOUT ALLOWANCE (`ACCEPT_LEGACY_ZERO_COMPLETION_HASH`, added 2026-08-24, §7.50).** The stage accepts a `complete_record_hash` of **all zeros** as well as the correct one, so a pre-H2 encoder keeps working through a mixed-version rollout. **It is a single named constant in `hydra-state::stage`, not a comment**, and this row is its deletion notice. **Condition: it must be `false` before v1 ships** — while it stands, an all-zero hash is a valid finalize from anyone the role gate admits, which is most of what H2 closed. Guarded by `a_finalize_whose_evidence_names_a_different_activation_is_refused`, which asserts a **non-zero mismatch is refused**, so the allowance cannot quietly widen into "any hash passes". **Unblocking condition: no peer in the cluster predates H2** — i.e. after M4·3 packaging pins a single build.

**✅ H21 CLOSED (2026-08-24, §7.52)** — the `hydra-coordinator` binary serves the router over the cluster's own rustls material; verified by running it (plaintext on the API port is refused). The client must trust the self-signed cluster CA, which is a documented setup step.

**✅ M10 CLOSED (2026-08-24, §7.54)** — refusals in the stage SM emit `ERR_FENCED`/`ERR_TRANSITION` instead of silence. *(Superseded text:)* **⛔ M10 — STRUCTURED ERROR REPLIES ARE STILL NEVER EMITTED (owed, with a concrete instance as of 2026-08-24).** `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`, `ERR_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `ERR_BAD_CHECKSUM` and friends exist in the schema and are never sent; a refused control frame produces **silence**. **The instance:** M4·0's acceptance test hung rather than failing, because a stage refusing a finalize on mismatched evidence simply does not reply, and a hang reads as a network fault rather than a protocol refusal. **Unblocking condition:** none — it is a decode/serve-loop change (reply `ERR_*` instead of `Ok(vec![])` on refusal) plus the coordinator-side handling. Scheduled with M4·3, since it changes what an operator sees when something is wrong.

**✅ ALL THREE CONVERGENT ITEMS CLOSED IN M4·0b (2026-08-24, §7.56):** `Terminate`, H10's recovery record, and M13's sender half.

**✅ M4·0b's RESIDUAL CLOSED IN M4·0c (2026-08-24, §7.57)** — the strategy path (catch-up, sampler install, re-activation) is `ActivationDriver::reconstruct`, and the acceptance harness holds no connection with which to participate. **The README may state the recovery claim without qualification**, noting only that the rebuild half is demonstrated on an engine-gated run (CI status: unavailable, not green, pending L1). *(Superseded:)* **⛔ M4·0b RESIDUAL — the recovery STRATEGY path is still driven by `pair.rs`, not the coordinator (2026-08-24).** The coordinator now decides that a recovery begins, records it durably, and tells the stages. What happens *between* `BEGIN_RECOVERY` and re-activation — driving catch-up with `REBUILD_APPLY`, installing the sampler checkpoint, re-activating the rebuilt stage — is still executed by the pipeline drivers in `hydra-worker::pair`. **Consequence for the README:** it may honestly say the coordinator drives crash-safe session recovery *end to end for the control plane*, and must not yet claim it drives the **data-plane rebuild**. **Unblocking condition:** none — it is the same `ActivationDriver` shape over the catch-up messages, and the encoders (`encode_catch_up_context`, `encode_install_sampler_checkpoint`, `encode_apply_token`) all exist. Sized as one seam, and it is the last one before the claim is unqualified.

*(Superseded:)* **⛔ M13's SENDER HALF and H10's RECOVERY RECORD — still owed after M4·0 (2026-08-24).** The wire decode arm and the SM both exist; what does not is a coordinator that *decides* to reset or to begin a recovery, because M4·0's driver covers the **activation** transaction and not the recovery orchestration. Likewise `hydra-coordinator` still writes no `BEGIN_RECOVERY`/truncation record. **Unblocking condition:** the recovery half of the driver — the same shape as `ActivationDriver`, over `CoordEvent`'s recovery transitions. Naturally M4·3-or-later work; reported as still-owed rather than folded in.

*(Superseded — closed in M4·0b:)* **⛔ `WalKindTag::Terminate` — STILL UNREACHED (2026-08-24).** M4·0 gave it a durable record path, and nothing emits `SESSION_TERMINATE` yet: the coordinator has no session-lifecycle driver (cancellation, TTL detach, explicit termination per spec §11). **Unblocking condition:** the session-lifecycle half of the driver. Stated rather than counted as covered.

**⛔ M3's REVOCATION HALF — STILL OWED after M4·2 (re-stated 2026-08-24, §7.55).** Pairing can re-issue but **cannot revoke**: a certificate believed compromised stays valid until it expires. Bounded by the 397-day leaf lifetime, which is a ceiling and not a remedy. **Unblocking condition:** a CRL the coordinator publishes and every peer's verifier consults (`rustls`'s `with_crls`), plus a `hydra-cli revoke` that adds a serial and re-publishes. It needs a distribution path — the same one M4·3's packaging has to define for the CA certificate itself — so it is naturally M4·3-or-later work, not a decision waiting on anybody.

**⚑⚑ FLAG FOR M4·3 (asked for by the design authority, answered 2026-08-24): DO THE THREE REMAINING CONVERGENT ITEMS LAND IN v1?** **Recommendation: `Terminate` and H10's recovery record — YES, in v1; M13's sender half — YES; and all three land together, because they are one component.** They need the **recovery / session-lifecycle halves of the coordinator driver**, which is the same `ActivationDriver` shape over `CoordEvent`'s recovery transitions — not new design, and M4·0 proved the pattern. **Why v1 and not documented gaps:** without them a shipped coordinator can *activate* a session but cannot **recover** one it did not start, cannot **terminate** one cleanly, and never writes the `BEGIN_RECOVERY` record §6.5's restart classification reads — so the D1 recovery story that M2 and M3 demonstrated would be a property of the **test harnesses** and not of the product, which is precisely the gap M4·0 existed to close and would leave half-closed. **The README cannot honestly claim crash-safe sessions otherwise**, and that claim is the product. **Estimated shape:** one seam, mirroring M4·0's — a `RecoveryDriver` owning the same `Coordinator`, its own adversarial acceptance suite re-pointed at the kill-window scenarios, and the three items closing as consequences with named tests. **If the owner rules otherwise, the README must say the coordinator does not yet drive recovery** — that is the honest alternative, and it is a much weaker product claim.

**⏳ NORMALISE-BEFORE-VALIDATE SWEEP — owed (2026-08-23, from §7.41's fourth shape).** Every place the code **sorts, dedupes, clamps, coerces, or otherwise normalises** before a check, ask what the check can still see. `recovery::read`'s sort-before-gap-check was one instance; the sweep is to find the rest before an audit does. **Unblocking condition:** none, it is a reading task with a grep-able shape (`sort_by`, `dedup`, `clamp`, `min(`, `max(`, `saturating_`, `unwrap_or`, `retain`, `filter` upstream of a validator).

**⏳ RULE-19 ORACLE GAPS — named in §6.0, owed here (2026-08-23).**

| Gap | Unblocking condition |
|---|---|
| **The fuzz arm cannot reach `Worker::on_frame`** — it stops at `wire::decode`, so the role gate, the C4 data-plane fence, M9 sequencing and every SM transition are unfuzzed. | An `on_frame` fuzz target seeded with a valid fence tuple (so cases get past F1 instead of dying at it), driving a real `Worker` with the engine absent. Additive to the existing seven; the enumeration rule (17) already says the dependency list is the register. |
| **The fuzz arm has no timeout oracle** — a hang is not a crash, so an H19-class infinite loop would be invisible to it. (H19 itself was found by the audit, not the fuzzer.) | A per-case wall-clock watchdog in the driver, with the case's `(seed, iteration)` reported on expiry — the same reproduction contract crashes already have. |
| **The fuzz arm has no allocation oracle** — a large-but-legal reservation is not a crash, which is precisely the D2 class it was built for. | A per-case allocation ceiling (a counting allocator, or an rlimit around the shard process) so "reserved 4 GiB from a 40-byte file" fails the case instead of passing it. |
| **TLC cannot construct ill-formed messages** — how H2 and H3 survived: the code was weaker than the model and the model could not tell. | A receiver-level fuzz/property layer driving `hydra-state` events directly with out-of-range values (epochs, attempts, `truncate_to`, goals), asserting the SM refuses rather than transitions. |

|---|---|---|

| **H10 (auditor's) — reconciled against the auditor's text on 2026-09-01 (rule 20): TWO clauses CLOSED, ONE PARTIALLY, and ONE RESIDUAL FOUND BY THE RECONCILIATION.** Auditor's title: *"Recovery's discard decision is never made durable; post-recovery commits go to a fresh file that re-appends already-durable positions"*; fix: *"Implement CommitStream::open → (stream, RecoveryState) over WalScan + open_append; restore every watermark and next_commit_id; fsync a recovery record before any wire traffic; never re-append recovered positions."* **(a) `CommitStream::open` — CLOSED:** `commit_stream.rs::open` restores `generation_durable_pos`, `prefill_stable_pos` (from `INPUT_CHUNK_COMMIT`), `committed_sampler_checkpoint_id` and `next_commit_id`; oracle `commit_stream.rs::a_recovery_resumes_from_the_durable_frontier_and_never_re_commits`. **(b) the recovery record — CLOSED:** `control_wal.rs` writes `BeginRecoveryRec` and `driver.rs` emits it on `ControlMsg::BeginRecovery` (M4·0b). **(c) "`recovery::read` ignores `INPUT_CHUNK_COMMIT`" — PARTIAL, at a different site:** the prefill watermark is restored by `CommitStream::open`, but `recovery::read` itself still reads no `INPUT_CHUNK_COMMIT` record (verified: no such handling in `recovery.rs`), so a caller that reconstructs from `read()` alone still has no prefill frontier. **(d) RESIDUAL, found by reconciling (not by memory): the shipped `hydra-coordinator` binary never calls `CommitStream::open` — it `create`s a fresh stream on every start (`bin/hydra-coordinator.rs`), and no production path calls `recovery::read` either (grep over `src/` and `src/bin/`: only tests and `hydra-3node-kill`'s `verify`).** The M2 DoD (d) property — *"kill the coordinator mid-generation → restart resumes from commit stream"* — is therefore demonstrated by test harnesses (`coordinator_drives_production.rs`, `d1_recovery.rs`) and **not by the product's startup path**. **Verdict: labels were not shuffled and the mechanism is complete and tested; the wiring of that mechanism into the binary's restart is OPEN and is stated here as its own item.** | (c): read the chunk-commit watermark in `recovery::read` (or retire `read` in favour of `open`); (d): a restart path in `bin/hydra-coordinator.rs` that `open`s an existing `commits.wal` bound to the same session and classifies per spec §6.5 — the pattern `coordinator_drives_production.rs` already exercises. Nothing blocks either. |

bumping before that would reproduce L1 at a new SHA and re-port the patch twice. *(Original ruling retained:)* **⚑ SEQUENCED (owner-ruled 2026-08-23): the bump happens WITH L1's fork pin, as its own seam AFTER M4·0's coordinator driver** — so the layer-window patch is re-ported **once**, and the M−1 spike sweep and the M2 golden-token gate re-run against a **stable tree** rather than one still moving underneath them. **Two binding conditions when it happens:** (1) **the FULL 15-combination spike sweep re-run with quoted results** (BLUEPRINT §1.2 — `spike/shard_split`, all split × prompt combinations; quoted per rule 16, not summarised); (2) **a diff review of what six weeks of upstream changed in the seven patched files** — `include/llama.h`, `src/llama-context.cpp`, `src/llama-cparams.h`, `src/llama-graph.cpp`, `src/llama-model.cpp`, `src/models/{llama,qwen2}.cpp` — **before assuming the patch still applies cleanly.** A patch that applies without conflict is not thereby still correct. | Blocks nothing; scheduled after M4·0. |

Sequence it with L1's fork-SHA pin so the patch is re-ported once, not twice. |

Its CI status is **unavailable**, not green, and the required-target list says so. | L1 — pin a fork SHA or commit the patch application, and build the engine in CI. Scheduled in this wave. |

**⛔ WAVE 3 RESIDUALS — named, not implied (2026-08-23, §7.45).**

| Residual | Why it is not closed | Unblocking condition |
|---|---|---|
| **H18's structural half — the handshake still runs inline in `accept()`.** The 10 s timeout bounds the damage; it does not remove the serialisation, so a slow (not silent) peer still delays the next accept by up to that bound. | Moving the handshake into the spawned task changes the shape of every call site (`serve_multi_conn` + five `pair.rs` endpoints), each of which currently receives an already-authenticated `AcceptedConn`. That is a refactor with its own regression surface, and bundling it into a security seam would mix two kinds of risk. | Scheduled work, not a decision: return the raw stream from `accept()`, handshake in the spawned task under the same timeout, and bind the role there. Nothing blocks it. |
| **M1's other half — no per-peer or global connection semaphore.** The timeout bounds one connection's hold; it does not bound *how many* holds one peer may open. | The cap belongs at the accept loop, which is the same code H18's structural half rewrites. Doing them separately would mean touching it twice. | Do it with H18's refactor: a per-peer and a global permit, acquired before the handshake and released with the connection. |
| ~~**L1's real fix — pin a fork SHA.**~~ **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 (§7.66)** — `PavanManchikatla/llama.cpp` @ `c00bcebf` on `f280b269`; **verified by a fresh clone that builds the real engine with ZERO stub messages**, not asserted. *(Original:)* The build now refuses to produce an ABI-mismatched engine, and the patch is asserted to match the tree, but the patch is still working-tree state. | **Creating a fork is an owner action** (a new remote repository under the owner's account), not something the agent should do unilaterally. | Owner creates `PavanManchikatla/llama.cpp` with the layer-window patch applied at a tagged SHA; the submodule is repointed and `spike/*.patch` becomes provenance rather than the mechanism. **Until then CI still never builds the real engine, so the `vendored-gguf` fuzz target stays UNAVAILABLE there (H6).** |
| **M3's revocation half — no CRL/OCSP, no rotation flow.** | Bounded validity is the half that needs no new protocol; revocation needs a distribution mechanism and a re-pair UX. | M4·2's pairing work, where the re-pair flow already has to exist. |

**✅ THE COORDINATOR DRIVER LANDED AS M4·0 / M4·0b / M4·0c (2026-08-24, §7.52 / §7.56 / §7.57) — row rewritten to observed status 2026-09-01.** `hydra_state::Coordinator` drives activation, recovery, the strategy path and termination through `ActivationDriver`, and `hydra-coordinator` is a real binary. Of the seven convergent items: H2's completion evidence, `Terminate`, H4's quorum, H10's recovery record, M12's CSPRNG ids, M8's session-bound WAL and M13's sender half all closed with it (§8 entries above). **One residual found while reconciling H10 (2026-09-01, rule 20): the shipped binary only ever `CommitStream::create`s — see the H10 row.** *(Superseded text, retained:)* **⏸️ THE COORDINATOR DRIVER — PROPOSED AS AN M4 SLICE, PAUSED FOR THE OWNER (§7.46).** Seven owed items converge on this one absent component and close with it: H2's completion evidence · the `WalKindTag::Terminate` path · H4's live quorum · H10's recovery record · M12's CSPRNG session ids · M8's session-bound WAL · M13's sender half. **The verdict determines whether they close before ship or ship as documented gaps.** Evidence for the proposal is in §7.46 and was checked, not inferred: the `Coordinator` SM is constructed only by `hydra-sim`; `hydra-coordinator` has no transport dependency; there is no coordinator binary; and every non-test activation is a hand-rolled `COMMIT`→`FINALIZE` pair with `attempt` hard-coded to 1.

**⚠ Performance-claim honesty rule (M2 DoD amendment (d), binding):** until a real **wired-LAN** run exists, the envelope below and any README claims are **report-derived targets, NOT measured** — carry this annotation; no LAN numbers may be implied from `--local-pair` or containerized-CI runs.
**Honest performance envelope (report targets, not yet measured on wired LAN):** 70B Q4 ≈ 2–7 tok/s wired desktop+Mac, 1.5–4 over good Wi-Fi; TTFT tens of seconds at 4k prompt; recovery <15 s target (4k ctx, D1). Hydra's value is correctness + running-at-all, not speed.

## 9. Environment & toolchain facts (agent machine)

> **⚑⚑ THE ENGINE IS A PROJECT-CONTROLLED FORK (2026-08-25 — audit L1 CLOSED, §7.66).**
>
> | | |
> |---|---|
> | **Submodule URL** | `https://github.com/PavanManchikatla/llama.cpp.git` |
> | **Branch** | `hydra-layer-window-f280b26` — named so the base is legible from the branch alone |
> | **Pinned SHA** | **`c00bcebf79cbf95be22aa711d485a287914672c5`** |
> | **Upstream base it derives from** | **`f280b26983ad0fdb705a0d9ebf0503e76f2899b0`** (`ggml-org/llama.cpp`, 2026-08-25, *"metal : per-device tuned (Q, NE) for flash-attn vec (#26570)"*) |
> | **Delta from base** | exactly one commit: the layer-window patch (7 files, +130/−6) |
>
> **Why this is the L1 fix and not a relocation.** The layer-window patch existed only as an
> **uncommitted working-tree modification** of the submodule, so a clean checkout silently got stock
> upstream and every green engine-gated result was evidence about a tree **no clone reproduces**. It is
> now a **commit at a pinned SHA**; `git status` in `vendor/llama.cpp` is **clean — for the first time
> in this project's history** — and `spike/llama-cpp-layer-window.patch` is **provenance, not the
> mechanism**.
>
> **⚑ THE MAINTENANCE COST DID NOT DISAPPEAR — IT BECAME VISIBLE, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** A fork is
> not free: **every future upstream sync requires re-porting the patch onto the new base and re-running
> the full M−1 sweep before the new SHA may be pinned**, plus the positional-initializer audit on
> `llama_model_default_params()` (upstream swapped three booleans for the `load_mode` enum *inside* the
> last bump's range, and that initializer is positional with comment labels — a mis-ordered field
> assigns silently). **That obligation was always real; before the fork it was carried by one machine's
> working tree and invisible to everyone else.** The fork converts a **silent** liability into a
> **scheduled** one. It does not remove it, and this row exists so nobody later reads the fork as
> having made the problem go away.
>
> **Standing consequence:** the project is now responsible for its own engine updates. **An upstream
> CVE fix does not reach Hydra until somebody performs that sync.**

**⚑ TOOLCHAIN, STATED BECAUSE "CLIPPY CLEAN" IS A CLAIM ABOUT A VERSION (§7.61, 2026-08-24):** the agent's **default** toolchain is **`rustc 1.93.1 (2026-02-11)`**; **CI's `stable` is `rustc 1.98.0 (2026-08-18)`** and is the stricter, authoritative one. **`1.98.0` is installed locally as a NAMED, NON-DEFAULT toolchain** so CI's clippy can be run before pushing (`cargo +1.98.0 clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`) **without re-basing the measured test baseline onto a toolchain it was not measured on**. Any lint claim must name which of the two produced it. **⚑⚑ BINDING (owner-ratified 2026-08-25): EVERY FULL-SUITE RECEIPT SEPARATES THE STREAMS AND REPORTS A MANGLED-LINE COUNT.** Run `cargo test … > out 2> err` — **never `2>&1`**. `ggml`'s Metal teardown writes to **stderr** while `libtest` writes results to **stdout**; merged, they interleave and **corrupt the result line itself** — the log literally contained `test result: ggml_metal_free: deallocating`, a suite whose verdict is unreadable, which is how a run summed to **93 suites / 387 passed** when **94** started and `EXIT=0` (§7.61). **The receipt states three numbers, not one:** `running`-line count, readable-result count, and **mangled = the difference**. A receipt reporting a passed-count without its mangled-line count is incomplete, because a silently-dropped suite is indistinguishable from a suite that never ran. **Why this is a rule and not a habit:** the numbers this project quotes into gate tables come out of these logs, so the parser is verification infrastructure under rule 12 — **and it was defeating itself in the very session that wrote rule 12 into a commit message.** Cross-check: `running`-count == readable-count, or the receipt is not quotable. *(A checked summariser that asserts this equality is owed — §8.)*

Apple Silicon M2, 8 GB RAM (→ small models locally; 70B targets need M2-milestone hardware), macOS, Metal + CPU backends; Rust/cargo; cmake 3.28; flatc 25.12.19; OpenJDK 26 (headless, substituted for temurin cask — approved) + TLC 2.19 (`tla2tools.jar`, git-ignored). Concurrent TLC runs require unique `-metadir` (collision incident logged and fixed). **Thermal constraint (environment fact):** long TLC runs overheat the laptop and are killed at session end — so **long model-checking is CI-owned** (`.github/workflows/tlc.yml`); local TLC is **smoke-only** (parse, Mut2, Mut4), and any exceptional local run must use `-checkpoint 1 -workers 1 nice -n 19` and be `-recover`-resumable. Two Mut3 local runs already burned heat for zero retained progress — not a third.

**☠️ CLOUD VMs DEAD — 2026-08-05 deadline PASSED (Azure credits ended; owner deallocated).** `myVm-1` (B2ms 8 GB) + `myVm-2` (B2als_v2 4 GiB) are gone; the Tailscale IPs (`100.115.200.62` / `100.73.205.31`) are no longer routable. **The 3-node heterogeneity set no longer exists.** **All prior `WAN/Tailscale` results are HISTORICAL/BANKED and permanent** — `THREENODE_WAN_OK` (12/12 argmax), `THREENODE_KILL_OK` (real kill-window, gate-cond-(i) on real HW, 23.8 s WAN), the 4.0/2.1/1.0 heterogeneity table (`docs/heterogeneity.md`), the M2 real-second-machine WAN demo (`docs/wan-run.md`). These DoD components stay **CLOSED** (demonstrated is permanent). **Any multi-real-machine rerun is now HARDWARE-CONTINGENT** (needs the owner's hardware decision — same bucket as the wired-LAN owed item, §8). **From here the project is Mac + container-CI only** (all remaining Phase-2 work is VM-independent); **the standing multi-node verifier is the containerized CI** (`container-2node`, green weekly). The historical VM facts (VNet fast-path, NSG posture, `ssh hydra-vm*` runbooks in `docs/wan-run.md`) are retained for provenance only.

## 10. Glossary (project-specific terms a newcomer will hit immediately)

**Fence tuple** — identifiers on every message (cluster/manifest/session/epoch/recovery_id/attempt/context/generations) enabling rejection of stale traffic (F1/F2). **Ledger** — durable token history at C; single source of truth (I3). **applied_pos vs sampled_pos** — input/KV progress vs sampled-output progress; never interchangeable (I13). **GENERATION_COMMIT** — atomic record binding tokens + the sampler snapshot for the same prefix (I19). **PREACTIVE/ACTIVE_FINAL** — reversible vs finalized activation states; data plane only in the latter (I20). **Supersession** — replacing a decided-but-unservable activation at epoch+1 instead of blocking (I22). **Strategy A/B** — survivor-preserving recovery with catch-up vs full-state rebuild in a fresh context. **DST** — deterministic simulation testing; the sim that asserts I1–I25 under adversarial schedules. **Mutation parity** — the sim must catch the same four sabotages the TLA+ mutations encode.

## 11. Update protocol for this file (agent-binding)

1. Update **in the same commit** as: milestone progress, gate verdicts, findings, TLC/sim results, amendments, owed-item changes.
2. Sections 3, 4, 6, 8 must always reflect *now*; §7 is append-only history; §12 gets one line per update.
3. Never delete history; strike-through + supersede.
4. Gate reports to the design authority may quote this file; therefore it must never be aspirational — only verified facts, with evidence pointers (test names, log paths, commit hashes).
5. If reality and this file disagree, fixing this file is the *first* task.

Reconcile against the source before declaring any finding closed, and an **ID-reconciliation table is a required artifact** for any future external review. **M7 done properly:** retain-or-fail on commit error (`peek`→append→`confirm`, so a failed commit no longer discards tokens S_P already sampled), contiguity refused before the disk, and gaps/order checked on read **before the sort** — the sort had been quietly repairing the very disorder that would have been evidence. Recorded how M7 and H9 compose: the batch is retained *and* the stream is poisoned, so the retry is refused and recovery is reopen-then-recommit; the poison check is ordered first because a poisoned stream's watermark no longer describes the file. The double-commit test moved **below the API**, since the write-side guard now prevents building the corrupt ledger through it. **Three more gloss drifts found by the rule-20 sweep:** M11's `chunks_exact` truncation (now refused), **M13's missing wire decode arm** (spec §6.4's reconstruction-invalidating restart was unreachable over the wire; its spec half was already correct per the H1/M13 amendment), and **M5's `text_len` narrowing** (a ≥ 2 GiB prompt wrapped negative). **The upstream filing:** approved, and **not posted**. Searching the tracker first showed the abort is `GGML_ASSERT(!key.empty())` (`gguf.cpp:143`), reported at least four times, and **fixed on master** — our pin `13f2b28b` simply predates the guard. Filing would have duplicated four closed issues. Reclassified in §8 from "report upstream" to "bump the submodule", with BLUEPRINT §1.2's golden-token re-run and L1's patch re-port named as the real cost. **An approval carries its premise, and the difference was one search away.**
- **2026-08-23** — **✅ WAVE 3 COMPLETE** (H6 · H12 · H11 · H17 · H18 · H20 · M1 · M2 · M3 · L1 raised, §7.43–§7.45) **and ⏸️ THE COORDINATOR-DRIVER PROPOSAL (§7.46), paused for the owner per rule 5.** Asked to say whether the driver is an M4 slice or a v1.1 item, I checked the tree rather than reasoning from the residual list, and the answer reframes the question: **`hydra_state::coordinator::Coordinator` is constructed in exactly one place — `hydra-sim`**; **`hydra-coordinator` has no `hydra-transport` dependency**, so it cannot open a worker link; **there is no coordinator binary**; and every non-test activation is a hand-rolled `COMMIT_ACTIVATION` → `FINALIZE_ACTIVATION` pair with `activation_attempt_id` hard-coded to `1`, no acks, no intent, no complete, no abort, no unservable. **So the activation transaction is implemented outside `hydra-state` on the coordinator side, which BLUEPRINT §2 names as a defect in as many words.** The stage half is genuine (`Worker::on_frame` steps the real SM); the coordinator half is a script. **Precisely: the coordinator SM is verified but not deployed.** The M2/M3 demonstrations stand exactly as recorded and this does not re-open those gates — the narrower claim is that those runs prove the choreography works *when driven correctly*, and the SM exists for when it is not. **Recommendation: an M4 slice sequenced FIRST within M4, not v1.1** — without it M4's own DoD ("a non-author sets up a 3-machine cluster from the README") is unreachable because there is no coordinator to run, and deferring costs correctness rather than features. The counter-argument (ship v1 as a single-session demo runtime) is stated in §7.46 rather than buried, and rejected for the same reason. Seven owed items close as a consequence.
- **2026-08-23** — **⛔ WAVE 3 PLATFORM GROUP: H17 · H18 · H20 · M1 · M2 · M3 · L1 (§7.45).** **All seven are the same oracle failure (rule 19, INDISTINGUISHING):** every transport and provisioning test drove a *well-behaved peer* — it handshook, it sent what it declared, it bound to loopback, it finished before any certificate could expire — and under that driver seven pairs of functions are extensionally equal, so no amount of running the suite could separate them. **H17:** the bootstrap blob is a private key file and was written 0644 into a shared `/tmp` with `Drop`-only cleanup; now `create_new` + 0600 in a 0700 directory (`O_EXCL` also refuses a planted symlink). **H18:** one silent TCP connection parked the accept loop for everyone, no certificate needed; bounded at 10 s and the refusal now names the peer — **the structural fix (handshake in the spawned task) is owed in §8, not implied**. **M1:** the 64 MiB hold is now bounded in *time* (60 s, per-connection settable so the test costs 250 ms not 60 s); **the connection semaphore is the other half and is owed**. **M2:** `is_unspecified` is false for `::ffff:0.0.0.0`, which on Linux accepts on every IPv4 interface — new `is_wildcard`, and the repo-wide grep now looks for it too. **M3:** `rcgen` defaults meant certificates valid until **4096**; leaves 397 days, CA 10 years, `BasicConstraints::Constrained(0)`, verified by parsing the DER — bounded validity is **not** revocation and is not claimed to be. **H20:** `sampled_ring` bounded to a 256-position window and the installed penalty window capped at `penalty_last_n` (a 1 MiB snapshot carried 262 144 entries that were re-emitted into every later snapshot and walked on every sample). **L1 raised and acted on:** `build.rs` now refuses to build against unpatched headers instead of producing a silent ABI mismatch, and `patch_integrity.rs` asserts the committed patch describes every modified vendored file — **pinning a fork SHA remains owed because creating a fork is an owner action.** **353 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored across 84 suites, exit 0; clippy clean.**
- **2026-08-23** — **⚑ RULE 19 REFINED INTO THREE DEGREES OF ORACLE FAILURE (§7.41), and ⛔ THE AUDITOR'S ORIGINAL REPORT BANKED (§7.42), which corrected the record three ways.** **The taxonomy:** *silent* (cannot produce the failure) → *asserting* (encodes the defect as intended behaviour, so the fix turns the test red and a red test reads as "the fix broke something") → *indistinguishing* (**the worst**: a driver under which the correct and incorrect implementations are extensionally equal, so running it more never helps and coverage metrics look fine). **Binding heuristic: when a fix changes a function's definition but no test changes colour, ask which driver made the two versions equal.** **The report:** `verification/audit-2026-08-23-AUDITOR-REPORT.md`, verbatim, all 57 findings with severity, confirmation status, file:line, exploit and fix — **nothing is "named only by count" any more** (H11, H12, all 20 mediums, all 12 lows). **(a)** The auditor's own closing recommendations *are* this project's waves — the directive transcribed the grouping faithfully. **(b)** But the per-finding **glosses** differ: Wave 1c's "C3+M4+H7+M5" are really *position-count-from-length / silent `i64→i32` narrowing / out-of-vocab token across `extern "C"` / no exception barrier at the 14 shim entry points* — all four were implemented, but under different labels, so *"H7 is fixed"* had been a claim about the summary rather than about the audit. **(c) One genuine gap in my own Wave 2: M7 is `GENERATION_COMMIT` contiguity, not boundary-store contiguity.** `Session::commit_group` still takes the batch out of the buffer *before* the durable append, so **a failed commit discards tokens S_P already sampled** and leaves a permanent hole. Recorded as an incomplete Wave-2 item in §8 with H10's two residuals, not quietly folded into Wave 3.
- **2026-08-23** — **✅ WAVE 2 COMPLETE: H8, H9, H10, H5, M6, M7 (§7.39–§7.40), oracle-first throughout per rule 19.** **H10:** `CommitStream::open` did not exist, so the restart path had no test to fail — a resumed coordinator would have re-used commit ids already on disk, accepted a backwards input frontier, and re-opened the emit-after-commit gate. It now restores all four watermarks and performs a **durable** discard (truncate + `fdatasync` before returning), and refuses a damaged ledger outright. **M6:** the sim's hard-coded `fsync` and the SM's atomic record-and-transition were **two oracles agreeing with each other and with neither the spec nor a disk** — no schedule could place a crash in the write→durability window. Both fixed; `UnservablePending` gates on `WalDurable`. **The TLA+ model does not change:** `Wal(r) == wal' = wal ∪ {r}` already means *durably written*, so the code was violating the model's abstraction, not extending it — model semantics unmoved ⇒ no TLC rerun owed (rule 11), the same shape as H2/H3. The two tests that asserted the atomicity were themselves enshrining; rewritten, plus the previously-unreachable window as a new test. **H5+M7:** the durability drivers fed one boundary per position in order, under which a `max()` frontier and a contiguous frontier are *the same function* — so nothing could see that `max()` **acks durability over holes**, freeing (via R3′) the exact boundary a D1 rebuild needs. Now: contiguous frontier, idempotent duplicates, (cluster, session, epoch) fence on every stored record, poisoning on I/O error, no swallowed `unwrap_or(-1)` in the serve loops, and contiguity enforced on read. **339/0/7 across 82 suites, clippy clean.** §6.0's register updated: three more gates moved from "cannot see" to closed, each naming its adversarial driver.
- **2026-08-23** — **⛔ WAVE 2 SEAM 1: H8 + H9 (§7.39), oracle first per rule 19.** **H8:** the torn-write contract only ever corrupted payload and tag bytes; a corrupt frame **header** was the case it could not generate — and the one the scanner mishandled. Three new header-byte cases showed **one flipped magic byte silently discarding 3 of 5 durable records**, which `open_append` would then have truncated away for good. Fixed by resync: record starts are 8-aligned, so the scan probes aligned offsets for the magic and refuses the log if any checksum-valid record follows the damage; the discardable tail is bounded to **one max record** (a torn tail is at most one partially-written record); probes are capped and exceeding the cap **refuses**, fail-closed. The control — the same corruption in the *last* record is still a tail — keeps the fix from turning every crash-during-append into an unopenable log. **H9:** the chaos sink discarded the payload on failure, so the post-failure log state was inexpressible; a sink that persists bytes and *then* fails showed the writer **accepting the next append**, placing a valid record behind a partial one and manufacturing the very corruption H8 refuses to open. `WalWriter` and `CommitStream` are now poisoned by the first I/O error, refuse every later append without touching the sink, and keep the first cause; `CommitError::Poisoned` is distinct from a fresh I/O error on purpose. **42/0 across `hydra-wal` + `hydra-coordinator`, clippy clean.**
- **2026-08-23** — **⛔ AUDIT WAVE 1d COMPLETE — WAVE 1 IS DONE (§7.38): C4 + M9 + H2 + H3 + H4 + H19.** The **data plane is fenced** (`session_epoch`, non-zero `stage_generation`, and serve-eligibility by frame class per F1 — `ACTIVE_FINAL` for decode, the rebuild states for `NO_SAMPLE`, **nothing** from `PREACTIVE`/`LOST`; `ERR_FENCED`). **Positions are exactly-once** (I1/R2): the next position applies, the last one is re-answered **from cache** (never recomputed), anything else is `ERR_GAP` — closing the case where **the R1 retransmit the protocol prescribes applied a position to the KV twice**. **H2/H3 were the code being weaker than the model it mirrors** — `StageRecvFinalizeAt` always required `m.tgt = stEpoch[s]` and `SendBeginRecovery` always emits `base = target−1`, so **TLC could never have reported either**: a model that constructs only well-formed messages cannot test a receiver's handling of ill-formed ones. **H4** closed structurally: `AuthenticatedRank` (private field, no constructor in `hydra-state`) can only be minted by `BoundPeer::authenticated_rank()` from the C2 binding — a non-stage peer yields `None`, not rank 0; new one-way dep `hydra-transport → hydra-state`. **H19** bounded (`goal ∈ [0, n_ctx]`, rebuild-state-only loop). **⚑ The wave's blind oracle, named — one oracle, not six: every driver in the harness was a perfect coordinator** (each position once, in order, in its starting epoch, to a stage it never activated), so it could not produce a retransmit, a gap, a stale epoch or an unactivated serve — **the rule-14 anchors included, which are structurally incapable of catching a double-apply because they never retry.** The harness now activates (`pair::activate` in every driver, the 3-node **middle stage** activated at all, frozen survivors put through the §7.19 catch-up→reinstall→reactivate choreography), and **two tests were found freezing only some survivors of a session-wide epoch transition**; three were sending the degenerate `BEGIN_RECOVERY{base = target = 0}`. **Verification: 325 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored across 81 suites, exit 0; clippy clean.** ‖ **✅✅ H1/M13 IS VERIFIED** — TLC run 32654396107 completed: `baseline-safety` **and** `baseline-live` `complete=1 verdict=GREEN`, the liveness leg a **clean drain** (3 981 633 distinct, 0 on queue) evaluating `ResetPreservesAttemptSpace`; **Mut1 and Mut3 both still fire** (no escalation). Receipt completed in place. ‖ **Baseline redo done honestly:** `7cf1445` in an isolated tree = **309/0/7, exit 0** — the earlier exit-1 was this session's own overlapping build (rule 18 turned on the agent), not the tree.

Every failure is a distinct structured `ShardRefused` variant; **there is no fallback path** — a manifest failure never degrades to a full load or to a control-plane-only worker, because a silent downgrade is precisely the attack the signature exists to stop (binding point 1). `WorkerConfig.shard_manifest` (`None` ⇒ the unchanged pre-P2·10b full load) is carried in `Bootstrap` **append-only** after the forwarding block, so older bootstraps decode to `None`. **`hydra-modelsvc` promoted dev→regular dep of `hydra-worker`** (one-way; modelsvc deps are gguf/blake3/ring/thiserror — no cycle; classified crate-dep change, rule-5 proceed+log, same precedent as §7.18). **⚑ THE REAL DoD (binding point 3) — GREEN:** `crates/hydra-worker/tests/shard_anchor.rs::two_worker_anchor_is_bit_exact_with_shard_loaded_weights` — the **standing rule-14 anchor**, identical in every respect except that each worker loads **its own manifest-verified shard file** instead of the whole model: two real TCP+mTLS endpoints, teacher-forced NO_SAMPLE, boundary serialized→transmitted→injected, S2's final unsampled logits **BLAKE3-equal to the unsplit golden**. M4·2 / M4·3 / M4·4 NOT started.** External full-repo security + penetration audit is commissioned against this hardened core; **audit findings will arrive as design-authority directives and get triaged per the standing repair sequence (rule 11)**. Standing loops unchanged: #25577 (checked 2026-08-23 — OPEN, zero maintainer replies) and the weekly fuzz verdicts.
- **2026-08-23** — **🧹 Pause housekeeping: background-job table audited under rule 12, nine jobs stopped, the one legitimate in-flight job recorded in §0(b); two authority notes landed.** **The audit found a rule-12 failure in my own bookkeeping and it is recorded rather than quietly corrected:** auditing the job table I `cat`-ed a **persisted tool-output file** from an earlier command, read a `d1_two_stage … 00:15 elapsed` line out of it, and announced an orphaned runaway test. **There was none** — the line was stale captured *text*, not live state, and `pgrep` showed nothing. **A job's status is `pgrep` / `gh run view`, never a log file that merely contains a status line** — the same failure shape as reading a CI job's ✓ instead of its `verdict=`, which §7.14/§7.15 caught twice at the protocol layer. Verification-infra-gets-no-presumption-of-correctness applies to the agent's own job table. **Nine jobs stopped:** eight were **permanently hung** — each an `until grep -q <sentinel> <file>` poller whose target run had been killed before ever writing the sentinel, so the loop could never exit (five waited on the same `CLIPPY_DONE` a `pkill` had already prevented); one was a fuzz-run monitor **superseded** by a narrower leg-2 watcher. None held a lock or a result. **The lesson for the loop pattern itself: a sentinel-poller with no liveness check on its target is a guaranteed leak the moment the target dies** — the waiter must watch the producer, not only the file. **One job legitimately in flight, now documented in §0(b) with its obligation, not just its existence:** fuzz leg 2 (run 32618760313, 8×900 s ≈ 2.0 CPU-h). Its receipt **does not exist yet**; when it lands, quote its per-leg `verdict=` lines into `verification/ci-results/fuzz-32618760313.md` and **append the cumulative row** to leg 1's running table (→ ≈ **2.4 / 24 CPU-hours**). §0(b) also flags this session's scratchpad logs as **historical**, so a fresh session cannot mistake them for fresh results. **Authority notes landed:** the prefill/`REBUILD_APPLY` digest half is stamped **⚑ OWED BY DIRECTIVE** in §8 with the ruled fix (an explicit per-session opt-in, **not another `no_sample` heuristic**); and §7.29 now names the **SELF-INVALIDATING REGRESSION** as a reusable pattern — *a test that asserts its own premise, so it fails loudly when its reason to exist disappears* — with the failure mode it defeats (a regression whose justification silently stops holding, leaving a test that passes while covering nothing) and where to reach for it next. **⏸️ HOLDING at the M4·1 pause.** M4·2/3/4 not started. Audit findings will be triaged per rule 11.

- **2026-08-23** — **⛔⛔ EXTERNAL AUDIT RECEIVED (4C/21H/20M/12L, accepted as a body); threat-model ruling landed; standing lesson §7.31 ratified; WAVE 1a COMPLETE (C1 + H13 + H14).** Banked `verification/audit-2026-08-23.md` — **with a provenance note that it is the design-authority directive transcribed, NOT the auditor's own report**, complete only for the findings named by ID and silent on the rest, because *what was acted on* and *what was found* are different claims and conflating them is how a ledger becomes a lie. **§7.31 — the audit's central thesis, ratified: the checklist's rows outrun their assertions.** M4·1 taught *a documented invariant nothing checks is not an invariant*; the audit pushed it to where that lesson is blind — **a row that claims more than its test proves is the same failure wearing the test's clothes, and is WORSE than a missing test because it terminates inquiry.** A missing test leaves a visible hole; an over-promising test **fills the hole with an answer** and a reasonable reader stops looking. **C1 is the specimen and every clause of the checklist row was true**: *"verifies the signature and every tensor hash before any shard weights are read and refuses on any mismatch"* — all true, and the signature was checked against a key **the attacker supplies**. *"Verified"* silently meant *"internally consistent"*, and nothing prompted anyone to ask **verified against what**. **⚑ THREAT-MODEL RULING (normative, BLUEPRINT §1.9 + spec scope): v1 assumes every certificate holder is HONEST.** mTLS + signed manifests defend against **outsiders and accidents**, never a **compromised stage**. Written down because the identity story was quietly implying it away — *per-device Ed25519 + mTLS* reads like a defence against a malicious participant and is a defence against a **non**-participant. **What rests on it is named**: teacher-forced recovery trusts a survivor's boundaries, the solver trusts reported capability, `BOUNDARY_COPY` trusts the copier, S_P is the sole source of sampled tokens. **v2 scope — a boundary stated loudly, not a gap fixed quietly.** The criticals were fixed regardless: none depends on worker trust. **WAVE 1a (§7.32) — C1 fixed structurally, not by adding a check.** `Manifest::verify()` checked the signature against `self.signer_pubkey`, a field **inside the signed bytes**: the artifact carried its own answer key, `generate_keypair()` is public, and a re-signed manifest verified perfectly while every downstream check — per-tensor BLAKE3, layer-range map, admission hashes — validated **the attacker's numbers**. The refuse-on-fail posture was intact and pointed at the wrong oracle. Fix: the argument-less form is **removed, not deprecated** (while it exists it is the shorter call, and the shorter call gets written); `verify_against(trusted)` is the only verification; `TrustedSigner` + `ShardManifestConfig { path, trusted_signer }` put the anchor **in the same struct as the path** so `Some(..)` cannot be written without it; the bootstrap carries the key beside the path and **refuses a path without one rather than defaulting**. **H13** — the comment said *"signature before anything else is trusted"* while the code parsed the full structure first and then self-attested: it did **neither** the order nor the anchor. `verify_and_parse` splits the trailer, verifies, and parses nothing until that passes. **H14** — a signature says *one of ours*, never *the one this session agreed on*; `blake3(manifest)` is now bound to the fence tuple's `manifest_hash`, a value already in `WorkerConfig::keys`, so no second source of truth was invented. Also: the manifest reader's `with_capacity` on declared counts (**the §7.28-D2 defect, third instance, in the one parser M4·1's fuzz arm never covered**) clamped, string lengths bounds-checked before the cast, and a **`manifest` fuzz target added** which CI now requires to report or the leg is INCONCLUSIVE. **Regressions name the argument they make:** `a_manifest_signed_by_an_untrusted_key_is_refused` re-signs the **real fixture** with an attacker key and **first asserts the forgery passes the old self-attestation**, so the reader sees that the forgery is structurally perfect and **only the anchor distinguishes it**; `an_unsigned_but_structurally_hostile_manifest_never_reaches_the_parser` asserts H13 **by consequence** — a manifest declaring `u32::MAX` shards is refused on the *signature*, not on a parse error, which is only possible if the parser never ran. **Rule-14 anchor green on the real shard fixture; 9/9 `shard_anchor`, 9/9 modelsvc, 6/6 fuzz smoke; clippy clean.** **⏸️ H1 (attempt-fence bounds) and M13 (reset/attempt-floor gap) PAUSE for ratification — genuine spec silences, needing amendments, not patches.**
- **2026-08-23** — **🧹 STANDING RULE 17 ADDED + the §7.28-D2 class swept across every untrusted-input parser; audit L2 fixed as the FOURTH instance (§7.33).** Enumeration banked: `docs/UNTRUSTED-INPUT-PARSERS.md`. **The rule exists because of the repetition, not despite it:** `Vec::with_capacity(declared)` was found in **GGUF** (M4·1's fuzzer, first second of its life), then in the **manifest** (while fixing C1 — the parser M4·1's arm never covered), then as **L2** in the **bootstrap** blob. **Three discoveries, three different activities, and not one of them found by remembering the previous.** A class is not closed by fixing instances; it is closed when the check becomes **mechanical**. **The sweep's own finding: the definition was too narrow.** *Untrusted input* had been read as *the network* — and **three of the four instances arrive from the DISK** (a downloaded model, a signed manifest, a provisioning blob). Rule 17 now says **anything the process did not compute itself**; under the old reading this sweep would have inspected one parser and declared victory. **Seven parsers enumerated; all clamped; six fuzz targets, up from three.** GGUF ✅ · manifest ✅ · **bootstrap FIXED (L2)** + a `checked_add` in `Reader::bytes` so a hostile length cannot wrap the offset back into range · **WAL record clean BY CONSTRUCTION** (borrows, length-checks before every slice, never allocates) **and given a target anyway — "clean by construction" is a claim, and rule 12 grants verification-infrastructure claims no presumption**; its oracle asserts any record handed back is self-consistent with the buffer it came from · frame header ✅ · wire body ✅ · **HTTP justified, not swept** — not a length-prefixed binary parser, nothing attacker-declared drives a reservation, and a target is deferred to **Wave 4** because the thing worth fuzzing is the auth/admission logic H15/H16/H21/M16 will reshape, so a corpus banked now is waste dressed as coverage. **⚠️ One entry is deliberately NOT a ✅** — writing it as one would have been §7.31 in miniature. `framed::Conn::recv` validates a 12-byte header and then **allocates `payload_len + 32` before the payload arrives: 12 bytes reserve up to 64 MiB**, ~5.6-million-fold, held while a peer stalls the `read_exact`. **Not** the D2 class (the cap is real and pre-allocation — that is what `MAX_FRAME_BYTES` is *for*), but not clean either: recorded **accepted-with-assumption** under the honest-worker ruling, since the peer must hold a cluster certificate, with the real fix named (stream into a bounded buffer; a per-connection in-flight budget) and marked not-v1. **⚑ The enumeration IS `crates/hydra-fuzz/Cargo.toml`'s dependency list** — a parser whose crate is absent from it is itself the finding. **CI requires all six targets to report or the leg is INCONCLUSIVE.** All six GREEN locally (`FUZZ SUMMARY targets=6 cpu_seconds=12.0 crashes=0 verdict=GREEN`); `local_pair` green, so the bootstrap wire round-trips with the C1 trust anchor in it.
- **2026-08-23** — **🔒 AUDIT WAVE 1b COMPLETE: C2 — peer identity is now bound to a ROLE at `accept()`, fail-closed (§7.34); the M12 verdict is stated both ways (§7.35); H1/M13 amendments drafted.** **C2's substance:** mTLS answered *"is this peer in the cluster?"* and **nothing answered "which stage is it?"** — so **any certificate holder could send any message family**, and the F1 fence passed all of it because F1 checks *session* identity, not *sender* role. The CA's signature was being read as an **authorisation** when it is only an **authentication**. Bound at `accept()` rather than per-message (a per-message check is one that gets forgotten on the next message type); `AcceptedConn` makes serving a connection without a role unrepresentable; an **allow-list** family gate runs on a pre-decode peek, so a family nobody has authorised has **no sender** and forgetting produces a refusal rather than a grant. **⚑ The enumeration-avoidance is a rule-17 consequence worth naming:** the binding **tests configured names against the certificate** instead of reading its SANs — which both prevents a multi-SAN peer choosing its own role by ordering, **and avoids introducing an X.509 parser that would itself need a fuzz target and a reservation audit.** **⚑⚑ THE FIRST THING THE FAIL-CLOSED GATE REFUSED WAS US.** The rule-14 anchor `direct_worker_to_worker_fwd_is_bit_exact` went **RED** on the first run: the harness dials S2 with a separate outbound identity (`worker-s1-client`) the dev fixture never named, so S2 refused it at `accept()`. **The property demonstrating itself on the project's own traffic** — and the reason every dialling identity is now listed individually rather than the fixture being widened: the moment a table becomes "whatever shows up", the binding stops being a binding. Production builds its table from `Bootstrap::expected_peers` (append-only wire; `role_table()` **refuses an empty list at startup**), and the six fail-closed regressions drive a **real listener with a real table**, never the fixture. **M12 verdict, stated both ways as directed (§7.35): VESTIGIAL as a security control, LOAD-BEARING as a correctness one.** `SessionKeys` **was never a secret** — it travels in cleartext in every frame, so anyone who can read one can forge the tuple. It was doing **misrouting prevention that read like authentication**, which is §7.31's shape at the *design* level: a mechanism whose name promised more than its construction delivered, and whose presence discouraged asking where the real authentication was. `cluster_id` and `model_instance_id` now do no security work; `manifest_hash` does **more** (H14's identity binding) but as a **public** value; `session_id` keeps its real job — rejecting stale-but-honest traffic, an accident-class property. **Fencing-highs re-scoping, by evidence rather than the audit's severity ordering: C2 changes NOTHING in Wave 1c/1d's scope.** C4 (stale-epoch data plane), H1 and M9 are **independently reachable** — the role gate stops a *durability target* sending `APPLY_TOKEN`, and does nothing about the **legitimate coordinator** sending one at a stale epoch, which is the actual finding. Different axis: C2 closes *who may speak*, C4/M9 close *whether what they say is current*. **H2–H5, H19, H20 I decline to classify** — the ledger records them as named-only-by-count, and guessing a drop-to-defense-in-depth for findings whose text I have never seen is precisely the over-promising move §7.31 forbids. They need the auditor's report. **H1/M13 amendments drafted** (`verification/spec-amendments-H1-M13.md`), not applied. **H1**: the floor is one-sided, so one frame carrying `u32::MAX` is accepted, **becomes the floor, and permanently denies every legitimate future attempt** — a *silent, permanent* liveness kill in which the stage behaves exactly as specified; window `{highest, highest+1}` adopted per the authority's prior, sufficient because §6.4 gives the coordinator exactly one way to advance. **M13**: §6.4 says nothing about the attempt space across a reset, **the model quietly chose "preserve" and is self-consistent so TLC reports nothing** — but F2 scopes the floor to (session, epoch) and a reset does not change the epoch, so an implementation reading "fresh reconstruction ⇒ attempts restart at 0" deadlocks permanently. **Two conforming implementations, one deadlock.** Both land spec+TLA+code together with a TLC re-run, per rule 11. Workspace **307 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored**; clippy clean; both rule-14 anchors green.
- **2026-08-23** — **🏷️ M12 ACTED ON: `SessionKeys` → `SessionFence` across 36 files (§7.35); audit ledger filled with the supplied H2–H5/H19/H20 content and the waves re-scoped by evidence.** **The authority's phrasing is now the canonical statement of the finding: *a mechanism whose name promised authentication while providing misrouting prevention, thereby suppressing the question of where authentication actually lived.*** That is **§7.31 at the DESIGN layer** — an over-promising *mechanism* rather than an over-promising *test* — and it is the pattern flagged as **most likely to recur when v2 revisits worker trust**, because attestation, redundant recomputation and stage quorum will all *sound* like they answer "is this worker honest?" and will at first answer something narrower. **The question to ask of each is the one this took an external auditor to ask: what exactly does this authenticate, and what is it merely making tidy?** **Renamed rather than merely re-documented, deliberately:** by §7.31's own logic **the name is the thing that terminates inquiry**, so leaving it accurate-in-a-doc-block and misleading at 208 call sites would have been half a fix. Type `SessionKeys` → `SessionFence`, `WorkerConfig.keys` → `.fence`, and a doc block whose **first line states it has never held a key** — every field travels in cleartext in every frame, so anyone who reads one frame can forge the tuple — followed by a table of what each field does *now* and a closing pointer: **authentication is the peer certificate; authorisation is `roles::PeerRole`.** **The rename surfaced a real latent confusion worth recording: several files already defined `fn fence() -> WalFenceCtx`, so the WAL fence and the session fence had silently been sharing a word.** Those are now `wal_fence()`. **Behaviour-preserving, and verified as such rather than assumed: 307 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored — identical to the pre-rename run — clippy clean.** **Ledger filled (the gap was the directive's, and the refusal to guess was correct):** H2 `FINALIZE_ACTIVATION` accepted with no completion evidence (epoch + `recovery_id` unchecked, evidence fields dropped at decode) → **1d**; H3 forged/stale `BEGIN_RECOVERY` freezes a serving stage and wipes KV (`target` not required to be `base+1`, `truncate_to` unbounded and negative-accepted) → **1d, folded with M4**; H4 activation quorum counts **self-reported ranks** and the wire carries **no rank at all** — the model gets one-ack-per-stage free from set semantics, a **modelling artifact, not a protocol guarantee** → **1d, keyed on authenticated peer identity EXPLICITLY, not implicitly**; H5 durability plane (`max()` frontier, **swallowed append errors ⇒ acking durability over holes**, no dedupe) → **Wave 2 with H8–H10**; H19 `CATCH_UP_CONTEXT{goal}` loops `goal+2` with no fence first — `i64::MAX−2` **hangs the single-threaded worker** → **1d**; H20 unbounded `sampled_ring` keyed by attacker-chosen `output_pos` → **Wave 3 hygiene**. **Post-C2 each is recorded with what the role binding DID and did NOT close** — forgery paths drop to defense-in-depth, and in every case an **independently reachable** half remains, which is why 1c/1d scope did not shrink.
- **2026-08-23** — **⚖️ H1 + M13 SPEC AMENDMENTS LANDED — spec + TLA+ + code + regression in one commit (§7.36). ⚠️ RATIFIED AND LOCALLY SMOKE-VERIFIED ONLY; CI VERIFICATION PENDING.** Draft/rationale `verification/spec-amendments-H1-M13.md`. **These two differ in kind from every other audit finding: the rest fix code that disagreed with the spec — here the spec DOES NOT SAY, so two conforming implementations could choose incompatibly, and the repair goes to the document first** (rule 11). **H1:** the attempt fence was bounded below and **not above**, and acceptance *adopts* the value as the new floor — so it was **unforgeable-past but forgeable-future**: one message carrying `u32::MAX` was accepted, became the floor, and **permanently fenced every legitimate attempt thereafter**. A **silent, permanent denial of activation in which the stage behaves exactly as specified** — nothing caught it because there was nothing to catch. **Not an attack under the honest-worker ruling, which is exactly why it still had to be fixed: it is an accident case, and the assumption excuses malice, never accidents.** Amended to `{highest, highest+1}` — sufficient because §6.4 gives the coordinator exactly one way to advance. **The regression asserts the property that matters rather than the obvious one:** a fenced attempt **must not move the floor**, because a rejection that still advanced it would reproduce the entire denial while looking like a fix. **The test's own first draft was refused by the new window** (it tried to jump floor 0 → attempt 2) — the amendment biting on its author, recorded rather than quietly corrected. **M13:** §6.4 said nothing about the attempt space across a reset and **the model had quietly chosen** (`UNCHANGED << attempt, stAttempt >>`). **Self-consistent, so TLC had nothing to say — the model was not wrong, it was silently opinionated, and its opinion was invisible to anyone reading the spec.** A second implementation reading "fresh reconstruction ⇒ attempts restart at 0" deadlocks permanently, because F2 scopes the floor to (session, epoch) and a reset does not change the epoch. **Two conforming implementations, one deadlock.** Amended, plus the **checked** property `ResetPreservesAttemptSpace` so the choice stops being incidental. **⚠️ VERIFICATION STATUS, exactly:** local (**through the project's runner flags**, per the new rule 18): SANY clean; **Mut2 fires**, **Mut4 fires**. **NOT run, NOT claimed: baselines, Mut1, Mut3, and `ResetPreservesAttemptSpace` — all CI-owned, dispatched at session close.** **The M13 property needs a CLEAN BASELINE DRAIN to be meaningfully evaluated — a leg that stops early on a violation may never reach the temporal check** — and **Mut3 going quiet would be an ESCALATION, not an explanation.** Rule 13: the `.tla` changed, prior checkpoints void (moot post-chaining, stated anyway). **⚑ STANDING RULE 18 ADDED (rule 12's third instance): the invocation is part of the verification infrastructure.** Running a checker differently from how the project runs it produces a result about a **different system**. The H1/M13 smoke, run ad-hoc without `-deadlock`, reported "Deadlock reached" on both mutation legs and **an escalation was already being drafted**; `run-tlc.sh` passes `-deadlock` because TERMINAL is deliberately absorbing, and the counterexample was a legitimate terminal state, not a fencing stall. **Consequence, binding: verification tooling is invoked ONLY through the project's own runners/scripts; an unavoidable ad-hoc invocation yields a PROVISIONAL result until reproduced through the runner.** Rule 12's three instances now span the whole stack — a CI ✓ instead of its `verdict=`, a persisted output *file* read as live state, and a checker run with different flags.
